Every session call is **skipped while `CarPlayRecordingService.anySessionActive`** — CarPlay owns its single `.playAndRecord`/`.voiceChat` session, has its OWN interruption handler, and must stay untouched (deactivate-once); the interruption handler itself still reconciles STATE under CarPlay (a phone-side reply riding CarPlay's session flips truthfully; its arms make no unguarded session call). macOS (no `AVAudioSession`) and Watch (own mic session) unaffected.

### Spoken Replies — opt-in per-surface auto-speak (all default OFF)

Three independent device-scoped toggles decide *when* a reply auto-speaks; the engine is unchanged (everything routes through `ReplyVoice`/`WatchReplySpeaker` with Apple fallback). **HARD RULE: a TYPED composer send NEVER auto-speaks on any surface** (you're reading the screen you typed into). A VOICE (mic) composer send never auto-speaks either — **except on Apple Watch**, the one glance/hands-free surface, where in-thread VOICE follow-ups DO speak (`.composer` is auto-speak-eligible on Watch; `.composerText` is not). So the Watch rule is **"voice speaks, text doesn't"**; every other surface keeps "no in-chat composer auto-speak". The per-message Speak control covers the silent cases. CarPlay is toggle-free (always speaks). Toggles live per surface: iOS/iPadOS in **Voice**, macOS in **General → Menu Bar** (paired with its input sibling — the menu-bar ask's OUTPUT belongs with the surface, not Voice's engine config), Watch in **Apple Watch** (a wrist-only behavior). Storage keys keep their existing literals everywhere (`speakReplyOnNotificationOpen` / `speakQuickLaneReplies` / `watch.readRepliesAloud`) — renaming orphans stored prefs.

| Toggle | ON means |
|---|---|
| iOS/iPadOS (Voice) | tapping a **reply** notification opens the thread AND speaks the latest agent bubble |
| macOS (General → Menu Bar) | quick-lane replies (popover voice OR ⌘⇧1/⌘⇧2 compose) speak on arrival; the main-window lane never. Routing is popover-visibility-aware (`MenuBarCoordinator.viewModel(for:)` rewires the VM's `replySpeaker`): popover OPEN on the reply's thread → staged via `AutoSpeakMailbox`, consumed by `DictationPopoverView.attemptAutoSpeak`, spoken on the popover's OWN `ThreadSpeaker` (Speak control shows loading→playing, pause works); popover CLOSED → `ConversationDetailViewModel.speakArrivalOnSharedEngine` fires the always-alive `ReplyVoice.shared` (hands-free) |
| Watch (Apple Watch; iPhone-hosted, iCloud + WCSession-couriered) | every VOICE turn's reply speaks — headless + Ask + in-thread composer mic (`.composer`); TYPED (`.composerText`) never. A reply is STAGED on arrival regardless of wrist state and SPOKEN while `.active`, re-firing on the wrist-raise so a reply that landed wrist-down speaks when you look (within the mailbox freshness window ≈15 s; older → stays a tappable notification). A reply-notification tap-open speaks source-independently |

**`AutoSpeakMailbox`** (`Services/TTS/SpeakEngine.swift`, cross-target) — ONE shared `@MainActor @Observable` one-shot mailbox every view-routed auto-speak stages through (iOS/Watch deep-link paths + the macOS popover-open arrival; macOS deep-links/notification taps still never stage, and a popover CLOSE clears any unconsumed request). `request(conversationID)` latest-wins → the thread view's `attemptAutoSpeak()` calls `consume(matching:)`: atomic one-shot, strict-`<` freshness window (`#if os(watchOS)` 15 s else 60 s); an EXPIRED request is cleared on ANY consume so a stale flag can't ambush a later open; a FRESH mismatch stays pending for its real target (e.g. an iPad split-view pane) — then speaks via the thread's own `ThreadSpeaker` (single audio owner; pause/supersede work). **What text gets spoken** is resolved by the pure `AutoSpeakSelection.resolve(staged:arrayLatest:)`: a Watch reply-ARRIVAL request CARRIES the exact reply (`Request.messageID` + `text`, staged by `WatchRecordingService.handleBackgroundReply`), and that payload WINS — so the speak can't re-derive the PRIOR `agent` bubble from a not-yet-refreshed `threadMessages` (the async, coalesced `.conversationsDidChange` refresh lands the new bubble AFTER the synchronous arm, so a follow-up turn would otherwise read stale); the notification-tap paths (iOS/macOS + Watch) carry NO payload and fall back to the latest `agent` bubble (their thread is fully loaded before the speak). `resolve` returns nil when nothing is speakable yet → the view SKIPS the destructive `consume` so the one-shot survives to the refresh. **Mic-wins:** `clear()` wired into `InAppAudioRecorder.startRecording()` (iOS) + Watch recording start; macOS routes mic-wins through `SpeechExclusivity`.

**Gating deciders (pure, unit-tested).** iOS `ReplyAutoSpeakDecider`: speak iff the notification identifier `hasPrefix NotificationDeepLink.replyIdentifierPrefix` AND parseable `conversationID` AND toggle ON AND `!CarPlayRecordingService.anySessionActive` (prefix gate: FAILURE notifications also carry a `conversationID` for tap-to-retry and must never speak; CarPlay guard: an unrelated speak must not perturb the held route). Watch `WatchAutoSpeakVerdict` decides **staging eligibility** (NOT play timing): eligible iff `captureSource ∈ {headless, ask, composer}` (VOICE turns; TYPED `.composerText` excluded — "voice speaks, text doesn't") AND the reply's conversation matches the in-flight one AND toggle. The `.active` **play gate** is separate — it lives at the play site (`WatchConversationThreadView.attemptAutoSpeak`) and re-fires on the wrist-raise (`.onChange(of: scenePhase) == .active`), so a wrist-down reply is STAGED on arrival then SPOKEN when the wrist rises, within the `AutoSpeakMailbox` freshness window (≈15 s).

**macOS speech exclusivity (`Services/TTS/SpeechExclusivity.swift`).** macOS has NO `AVAudioSession` arbitration and runs speech on separate engine instances, so this bus is the ONE arbitration point. Weak registries; `claim(claimant)` stops every OTHER party (`claim(nil)` = mic's stop-ALL); the registered party is the `ThreadSpeaker` STATE MACHINE not its engine (an engine-level cancel never fires the guarded completion → bubble stuck `.playing`), plus `ReplyVoice.shared` ONLY (never per-view engines = double-party desync; never CarPlay's instance = unpreemptible). **Mic lease:** two `RecordingExclusivityAuthority` owners (menu-bar `DictationService` + main-window `InAppAudioRecorder`) register live-queried; `acquireMicLease(excluding:)` is REFUSED (→ `audioMicBusy`) if any OTHER is live, since the two own SEPARATE `AVAudioRecorder`s and macOS can't share the input (concurrent starts hit HAL `error 35`) — the live capture is sacred, the second start refused. `isActivelyRecording` folds in each owner's synchronous startup flag to cover the async-start TOCTOU. Arrival speak enters via `claimForAutoSpeak` — REFUSED while any mic `.recording`; manual Speak taps + previews claim unconditionally (user-initiated audio obeys the user). Closing the menu-bar popover stops speech AUTHORITATIVELY in `MenuBarController.popoverDidClose` (the NSPopoverDelegate callback fires on EVERY close path — Esc, outside click, programmatic — unlike the content view's `.onDisappear`, which the app-lifetime-retained hosting controller makes unreliable): the popover's own `ThreadSpeaker` (via the coordinator's weak `popoverSpeaker` handle, guarded on an active turn so idle closes don't VoiceOver-announce "Stopped") + a targeted `ReplyVoice.shared.cancel()` + `AutoSpeakMailbox.clear()` — NOT bus-wide, so a main-window speaker keeps playing. The view's `.onDisappear` twin stays as a second net.

### TTS error taxonomy

Codes 36–43 are defined and tabulated in Error Taxonomy — `AppError`. Load-bearing residue: `ttsEmptyAudio` (38) is **retryable** (covers Gemini's preview model returning text tokens; production providers never emit empty 200s, so they never spin). **All TTS errors have `shouldPreserveForRetry = false`** — a failed spoken reply is NEVER queued for background retry (the text reply is already on screen; only the in-call loop retries, then Apple synth takes over).

### Key-readiness convergence (device-local; iOS/macOS)

iCloud Keychain delivers a synced provider key opportunistically (minutes–hours) with **no app-visible arrival event**, so a device whose ACTIVE cloud TTS provider is still keyless silently speaks Apple (chat contract) with nothing to converge it. Three pieces close the gap; all consume the SECRET-FREE `ActiveTTSKeyProbe` (`providerID` + `keySlotID` + `APIKeyState`, derived inside `SettingsManager.activeTTSKeyProbe()` from the same atomic `activeTTSSnapshot()` playback resolves — banner truth ≡ playback truth; the raw key never leaves the actor). `keyState` speaks ONLY to local credential availability — `.present` never claims the provider works.

- **Typed Keychain read** — `apiKeyReadResult(forPresetID:)` (`.present`/`.missing`/`.unreadable(OSStatus)`; pure classifier `APIKeyReadResult.classify`, `Services/TTS/TTSSnapshot.swift`); `getAPIKey` is its nil-collapsing wrapper. Empty/undecodable payload = `.unreadable(errSecDecode)`, never `.present`.
- **Readiness banner** (`Views/Settings/TTSKeyReadinessBanner.swift`, mounted atop iOS `VoiceProviderListView` + `MacVoiceCategory`) — shown ONLY on `.missing`/`.unreadable`. Observation-phrased copy: states the local fact + the consequence ("replies use Apple's built-in voice"); the iCloud-sync possibility is a secondary hint, NEVER asserted as fact (the app has no sync-queue visibility). Actions: Check Again (VM `recheckActiveTTSKey`, generation-guarded against a stale probe overwriting a later provider switch) + Add Key (`.missing`) / Manage Key (`.unreadable`) → the vendor detail via a TYPED `.vendor` route case (never a second `String` destination). The VM refreshes the probe UNFENCED on `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` (it binds no editor buffer, and the arrival post must clear the banner mid-edit).
- **`TTSKeyArrivalMonitor`** (`Services/TTS/TTSKeyArrivalMonitor.swift`, `@MainActor`, started by both platform `ConduckApp.init`s) — the bounded poll that substitutes for the missing OS event. Foreground-active only (`willResignActive` cancels — covers Control Center/interruptions); one finite backoff window per activation (5s→160s doubling, ~5¼ min, 6 Keychain reads); generation-stamped so a late read after cancel/switch is a no-op. Posts `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` **exactly once, only from a timer-discovered missing→present transition** — notification-triggered re-evaluations never post (a local paste already fanned out), and unrelated settings churn never restarts the window (requirement fingerprint = providerID + keySlotID; exhaustion holds until re-activation or a fingerprint change). That one post is the whole convergence: the VM clears the banner and `PhoneSessionManager` re-broadcasts the Watch envelope the wrist was missing.

The Keychain-migration flag (`keychainSyncMigratedKey`) is set ONLY after a definitively complete pass — a failed enumeration or a transiently unreadable item keeps it unset (and keeps the in-process latch open) so the pass re-runs; without this, a locked-Keychain launch would permanently strand un-migrated keys behind the sync-only reads and the probe would assert a false `.missing`.

---

## Remote Agent Round-Trip

The agent hop runs AFTER STT: `text` → user's gateway → reply. Two independent device-direct hops (STT = device → STT provider; agent = device → user's gateway). Conduck's developer never sees URLs, tokens, replies, session IDs, backend type, or audio.

**What the backends ARE (mental model — not dumb LLM proxies).** OpenClaw + Hermes are full self-hosted **multi-channel personal-assistant agents**; their primary UX is messaging channels (Telegram/WhatsApp/Slack/…) and the OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions` Conduck rides is an **opt-in side-door**. Each runs a **multi-step tool-using loop** and keeps its **own server-side memory** (e.g. Hermes injects on-disk `MEMORY.md`/`USER.md` into its system prompt). Two consequences are **inherent to the agent, NOT client bugs — do not fix either client-side**: (a) the **drift boundary** — Conduck's store holds only the turns made *through Conduck*, never the same agent's Telegram/CLI turns or its private memory; (b) **image-after-history latency** (~10× slower) from the gateway's fat fixed system prompt + a fresh agent loop rebuilt every request + per-turn image re-feed.

### Architecture (load-bearing — don't re-litigate)

1. **Client-owned history (OWNER of the stateless shape).** The CloudKit-synced conversation store (see Conversation Store) is the single source of truth for display AND upstream context. Each turn the client builds `messages[]` from the store (subject to trim policy) and sends it **stateless — no session header, no `conversation` field, NO OpenAI `user` field** (wire lock-tested to exactly `{messages, stream}` + optional `model`, `ConverseWireTests`). Justified: neither backend exposes a history API; the gateway re-sends full history to the stateless provider regardless, so server-side state saves NO tokens — only the request-assembler can trim cost. **Why no `user`:** OpenClaw key precedence is `x-openclaw-session-key` → `user` → random UUID; sending `user` would silently pin a stable server-side session and defeat the keyless-ephemeral model.
2. **Per-turn HTTPS, no push.** No persistent WebSocket. Each turn opens its own request — background URLSession (iOS/iPadOS/watchOS/CarPlay), foreground URLSession (macOS). Background URLSession is the **only** transport surviving iOS suspension for the 1–4 min hop (hands the transfer to the out-of-process `nsurlsessiond`, which relaunches the app on completion) — so **SSH, embedded mesh-VPNs (iroh/TailscaleKit), WebRTC, and any operator-run relay are all closed; do not re-propose.** Dropping background-only surfaces would not unlock an alternative: it removes the *background* requirement but not NAT reachability (a separate axis).
3. **One terminal contract, no per-backend dispatch.** Every capture (iOS entry 1/2, macOS, Watch, CarPlay) runs the identical contract — STT → forward → record turn to the store — and both backends take the identical stateless `/v1/chat/completions` request; `RemoteAgentClient` has one code path. Only base URL + setup recipe differ. Adding a backend = one `RemoteAgentBackend` case + one recipe.
4. **Routing is per-`Conversation`, not per-global-backend** (binding locked once a thread has turns; unconfigured bound ref throws `remoteAgentNotConfigured`, no silent reroute — see Architectural Invariants). Each send resolves the bound ref via `ConversationStore.fetchConversation(id:)?.backend` → `RemoteAgentRef(rawString:)`; request SHAPE is unchanged, only the optional `model` field may be set for a custom (see Request shaping).
5. **Continuity via the store, not a wire identifier.** Cross-device continuity rides CloudKit sync; the active-conversation pointer only selects which `Conversation` the next HEADLESS quick capture appends to — App-Group only, per-device, never on the wire (see Settings & Storage → active-conversation pointer lifecycle).

### Shared protocol surface (both backends)

| Aspect | Contract |
|---|---|
| Endpoint | `POST /v1/chat/completions` — **disabled by default on both gateways; user must enable** (OpenClaw `gateway.http.endpoints.chatCompletions.enabled=true`; Hermes `API_SERVER_ENABLED=true`). |
| Auth | `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (or no header on an explicit `.none` scheme — see Architectural Invariants fail-closed rule). |
| Statefulness | Stateless per request — see Architecture #1. |
| Body | OpenAI chat-completions `messages: [{role, content}, …]`, `stream: false`. `model` **omitted for built-ins** (gateway default → wire byte-identical to pre-custom); **sent only when a custom gateway's roster `model` is non-nil** (required by vLLM/Ollama-style servers). |
| Streaming | SSE supported by both; **Conduck uses non-streaming only** (`Accept: application/json`) — less state, simpler debug. |
| Reply | Eagerly decode the full `choices` array, then extract `choices[0].message.content`: every choice must contain a STRING `message.content`, unknown fields are ignored, and an empty string is shape-valid. A malformed later choice invalidates the response even when choice zero is valid. |
| Health | `GET /v1/models` — Settings "Test Connection" only, no periodic probes (wire is source of truth). |
| Reachability | Behind home NAT (Mac mini): expose via Tailscale Funnel / Cloudflare Tunnel / port-forward + Let's Encrypt. Public-IP VPS: already reachable, point straight at its `https://` URL. **watchOS has no Tailscale client**, but the Watch reaches a tailnet-only gateway (`*.ts.net` / `100.x`) **via the paired iPhone** — OS companion-routing runs the Watch's own HTTPS on the iPhone, through its full stack incl. Tailscale (see Per-Surface → Apple Watch). **Only STANDALONE (iPhone off / out of Bluetooth range) is a tailnet-only gateway unreachable** — the dominant standalone-Watch failure mode. No APP-level converse relay (the converse is the Watch's own background `URLSession`); the proxying is OS-level. |
| Audio | Neither accepts uploads. STT happens device-direct BEFORE text reaches the agent. |

The versioned public [`converse-response-v1.json`](../../Conduck/ConduckTests/Fixtures/converse-response-v1.json) corpus is the canonical, Apple-authoritative app-level reply contract. Its cases distinguish a reply (including the empty string) from `remoteAgentInvalidResponse`; compatibility implementations must follow those outcomes rather than independently interpreting the prose.

### OpenClaw + Hermes (Conduck-actionable specifics)

- **OpenClaw** — default port `18789`. Sessions NOT used: with no key (and no `user`) the gateway mints a fresh random-UUID session per request and the client `messages[]` are authoritative. Never contended **because keyless** (each request gets a unique session/lock). On contention the WIRE response is **HTTP 500** body `{"error":{"message":"internal error","type":"api_error"}}` — **NOT 423, NOT a `session file locked` body**; do NOT reintroduce a 423 / `remoteAgentSessionBusy` path (see Error Taxonomy).
- **Hermes** — same `/v1/chat/completions` + full-`messages[]` path, no new network code. Default port `:8642` = the **full-agent `API_SERVER`** (tools/memory/skills); Hermes also ships a tool-less `hermes proxy` on `:8645` — **Conduck targets 8642, never 8645.** On the keyless path Hermes reuses a deterministic session/sandbox across a conversation's turns — benign (tool working-dir continuity; content always comes from our body). Its SQLite-persisted server sessions live only on `/v1/responses`, which Conduck must NOT adopt: server-side state saves no tokens, and `/v1/responses` adds a last-writer-wins concurrency race + O(N²) base64 image replay.

### `RemoteAgentClient`

Shared type at `Services/RemoteAgent/RemoteAgentClient.swift`; used by iOS capture intent, macOS `DictationService`, `CarPlayRecordingService`, Watch-side `WatchAudioUploader`. Reads URL (Settings) + token (Keychain) for the resolved conversation's backend at construction; builds the OpenAI body from the active conversation's history (trimmed); applies `Authorization` + `Accept: application/json`; URLSession delegate handles trust eval (cert pinning, below); eagerly decodes every choice and returns `choices[0].message.content` under the Reply rule above; maps HTTP/errors to `AppError` (see Error Taxonomy + body-aware pass below) — **no per-backend specialization**. **Session injection:** each call site supplies its own `URLSession` (foreground for Mac; background for iOS/Watch/CarPlay); the client doesn't own its session.

### Request shaping (backend-agnostic — load-bearing)

The client owns history, so it sends the full (trimmed) conversation each turn; **no backend branch**. The only per-message branch is attachment-presence (a `Message`-shape branch): text-only turns send `content` as a **BARE JSON string** (portable, back-compat); image-bearing turns send the OAI content array `[{type:text}, {type:image_url, image_url:{url:"data:image/jpeg;base64,…"}}, …]` — base64 data-URIs are the only portable image input across arbitrary BYO gateways (Chat Completions dropped portable file input Sept 2025). Prior-turn images are **RETAINED**, not dropped. Trim = `priorTurns.suffix(Constants.contextMaxTurns)` (the cost lever, see Conversation Store). **Text/code files** are extracted and spliced INLINE into the turn's text as fenced, filename-labelled blocks (works on any model) AND dual-route to the file-server when configured so the agent's TOOLS can act on the real file (see Agent File Transfer → Composer text/code files).

**Single choke point.** `ConverseRequest.priorTurns(...)` is a pure synchronous mapper over a PRE-RESOLVED `[Message.id: [dataURI]]` map; its **SINGLE production caller** is `ConversationHistoryAssembler.assemble(...)` (`Services/ConversationHistoryAssembler.swift`, cross-target incl. Watch) — the shared async choke point (store fetch → prior-turn image-byte resolution → per-gateway `ImageHistoryPolicy` lookup → `priorTurns`) used by ALL SIX dispatch surfaces (in-app send + retry, headless `ConverseIntent`, CarPlay, Watch, share drainer), so every surface assembles identical history for the same stored conversation. Bypassing it once let three surfaces send image-blind history. Map predicate `isImage && !isServerReference` — agent-output download chips carry image MIME with EMPTY local bytes; mapping them mints a degenerate empty `image_url` part and wrongly consumes an inline-window slot.

**Honesty floor** (in `priorTurns`, wire-locked by `ConverseWireTests`): an image-bearing turn whose bytes are missing/unresolved NEVER flattens to bare text — all-keyed → `spliceImageTextRefs` disk ref; any-unkeyed → `spliceImageUnavailableNote` (count-honest: there is no file to point at, so it instructs "say you can no longer see these image(s), do not guess"); a partially-resolved turn rides the resolved subset inline + notes the missing count. Floor turns never consume an inline-window slot. Encodes a real failure: image-blind replay made the agent deny ever seeing a user's screenshot and retro-claim its own correct vision answer was a hallucination. **Both send paths carry this** (`RemoteAgentClient.send` foreground + `BackgroundRemoteAgent` background) — kept in lockstep.

**`ConverseRequest.model: String?`** custom `encode(to:)` **omits the key entirely when nil** (built-in requests stay byte-identical to pre-custom wire; `ConverseWireTests` `Set(json.keys) == {"messages","stream"}` lock holds), emits `"model": "<name>"` only when a custom gateway has one set. Threads through both send paths and bakes into the on-disk body file for the background path → survives a cross-launch resume.

### Body-aware error pass (`classifyBodyError` / `mapBodyError`)

Runs in ALL THREE converse transports (`RemoteAgentClient.decodeReply` foreground · `BackgroundRemoteAgent` delegate · `CarPlayConverseUploader` delegate) BEFORE the status map. **Two tiers, wire code first:**

1. **Structured `error.code`** (adapter contract v1 rev 1.3, frozen `AdapterWireCode` vocabulary: `image_unsupported`/`model_not_found`/`context_too_long`/`image_too_large`/`overloaded`/`upstream_timeout`/`upstream_failure`) classifies EXACTLY at any 4xx/5xx — clients key on the code, prose is free. Unknown/absent/numeric codes are NO code (never a confident classification) and fall through to tier 2. `classifyBodyError` returns `ClassifiedRemoteAgentFailure` (the mapped `AppError` + the wire code); `decodeReply` THROWS that carrier for body-classified errors — failure writers catch it BEFORE `catch let error as AppError` and persist the classification (`Error.unwrappedAppError` unwraps for handlers that only need the taxonomy case). `mapBodyError` is the AppError-only wrapper (legacy call sites + pinned tests).
2. **Regex heuristics on `error.message`**, gated to 400/404/413 — the cases where status alone is ambiguous (pure-status 408/429 stay in `RemoteAgentStatusMap`). **422 is admitted for the model-required check ONLY** and returns `nil` for every other shape: FastAPI-backed servers (vLLM, some LiteLLM) answer a MISSING model with an Unprocessable-Entity envelope, but the vision / image-size / context arms are pinned by test to 400/404/413 — a 422 must not become vision-eligible just because its body happens to match. First match wins, mapping to `AppError`: vision-unsupported → `.remoteAgentVisionUnsupported` (32); 413 or "image too large" → `.remoteAgentImageTooLarge` (33); "maximum context length"/"context_length_exceeded" → `.remoteAgentContextTooLong` (56); "not a valid model"/"no endpoints found for"/"embedding model" → `.remoteAgentModelUnavailable` (55). **Vision is checked BEFORE model on purpose:** OpenRouter rejects an image to a text-only model with `404 "no endpoints found that support image input"` (matches `support.*image`) while a bad model id gives `404 "no endpoints found for <model>"` (matches `no endpoints found for`) — ordering keeps the two 404 shapes distinct. Prefers the structured `error.message` over `metadata.raw` provider-wrapper text; falls back to the raw body for non-OpenAI-envelope gateways. **Never logs the body.**

### ATS + self-signed cert handling (SPKI pinning)

No `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads`; `http://` rejected at Settings save. ATS satisfied by a publicly-trusted cert (Funnel/Tunnel/Let's Encrypt) OR a self-signed cert + fingerprint pin. "Custom certificate fingerprint" = SHA-256 hex of the gateway leaf-cert **public-key DER** (the field normalizes on entry — lowercased, colon-stripped so an openssl-style `AA:BB:…` paste is accepted, `^[0-9a-f]{64}$`-validated); on each server-trust challenge the delegate recomputes and compares — match → accept; mismatch → `AppError.remoteAgentCertMismatch`; unset → default ATS chain validation.

**Local Network Privacy.** A gateway (or file server) reached by a same-subnet private IP (`192.168.x.x` / `10.x` / `100.64–127.x` off-tunnel) or a `.local` mDNS host is unicast local-network access — gated on iOS 14+/macOS 15+, BLOCKED with no prompt unless the app declares `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` (in the shared `Info.plist`, covering iOS/iPadOS/macOS/CarPlay). The prompt fires lazily on the FIRST local-network access; Settings "Test Connection" runs foreground (in-process `RemoteAgentClient`) so it surfaces the grant before any background-`URLSession` turn — and Tailscale (utun tunnel) / Cloudflare Tunnel / public-VPS hosts never trip it, so non-LAN users see no prompt. No `NSBonjourServices` (explicit URLs only, no Bonjour browsing). LAN + self-signed needs BOTH this string and the per-host pin resolved on the live send path.

"Test Connection" fires **`GET descriptor.verdictProbePath`** (**GET not HEAD** — reverse proxies sometimes strip HEAD or return 405; GET works universally) → `TestConnectionOutcome`: `.ok`, `.okNoModels`, or `.untrustedCert(presentedFingerprintHex:)` (no pin + system-rejected self-signed). The evaluator captures the presented leaf SPKI digest on EVERY challenge (incl. the no-pin default path) so the editor offers a TOFU "Trust & Save" banner on iOS AND macOS (writes fingerprint to the pin field + re-probes; the `.invalid` row stays suppressed while the pending cert awaits the banner's verdict). A **pin-set mismatch always throws `…CertMismatch` and is NEVER auto-offered for re-trust** (would defeat pinning). The bearer token never reads back into the UI — Trust & Save requires it re-entered. A plain **re-test of an already-saved config** is the one path reading the stored secret from Keychain (`retestRemoteAgent`/`retestKey`, in-actor, sent exactly as a normal turn, never surfaced) — lets a user confirm a months-old token still works without re-pasting.

**The verdict is decided by the BODY, not the status** (`validateProbeBody`, shape from `descriptor.verdictBodyShape` — derived from `connectionProbe` so a path and its envelope can't drift apart). **A 2xx is not sufficient:** OpenClaw with its chat endpoint disabled (the OFF-by-default state) serves the Control-UI **HTML at HTTP 200** on `/v1/models`, so a status-only verdict reports a gateway that cannot answer a single turn as "Connected" — the user discovers it only when their first message fails. `.modelListEnvelope` requires a top-level **`data` ARRAY** (an EMPTY one is structurally valid → `.okNoModels`: the route is real, but a gateway advertising no models can't answer); `.keyEnvelope` requires a top-level `data` OBJECT (OpenRouter `/v1/key`). A 204/empty body FAILS (proves the host answered, not that the route exists). Bytes are parsed — **`Content-Type` is never trusted** (working gateways mislabel JSON). The failure exit splits by what the body IS: not JSON at all (HTML, plaintext) → `.remoteAgentEndpointUnexpectedResponse` (58); valid JSON in the wrong shape (bare array, `{"models":…}`, `{}`) → `.remoteAgentEndpointWrongEnvelope` (62), whose copy names the top-level-`data`-array rule + the adapter-contract URL — the likeliest failure of a home-built custom adapter (`keyEnvelope` wrong-shape stays 58: OpenRouter is Conduck-managed, the contract copy can't help there). Both pair in the editor with `descriptor.endpointDisabledRemedy`, which names the lane's likely cause (OpenClaw's `chatCompletions.enabled`, Hermes's `API_SERVER_ENABLED`) **as a likelihood, never an assertion** — the same symptom also comes from a reverse-proxy login page or a Cloudflare Access interstitial. **Custom lanes** (no descriptor) store no server provenance, so their 58/62 callout presents both checks rather than guessing: `bash conduck-connect.sh --check-server` for existing OpenAI-compatible software and `bash conduck-connect.sh --check-adapter` only for an adapter built for Conduck. The callout states that both checks send live requests without changing server configuration and that an interactive PASS can continue into setup. A built-in whose descriptor carries no remedy (hosted OpenRouter) shows nothing, because its user can't run anything server-side. A **404 is classified probe-only** (`.remoteAgentEndpointNotFound`) inside `performTestConnection`, NOT in the shared `RemoteAgentStatusMap`: on the send path `mapBodyError` runs first *because* a 404 can legitimately mean model-not-found, and the map sees only an Int — no operation, no path — so it cannot tell them apart.

**Scope of Test Connection:** it proves address reachability, authentication, TLS trust, and the configured probe envelope (for custom/self-hosted gateways, `/v1/models`). It does **not** send a chat-completions turn and is not a full compatibility verdict. Existing-server users who want the chat wire exercised before pairing use `bash conduck-connect.sh --check-server`; adapter builders use the stricter `bash conduck-connect.sh --check-adapter` contract gate.

**A green "Connected" is session-scoped and retracted on ANY edit.** `remoteAgentLiveValidated` (+ the per-ref `remoteAgentLastErrorCodes`, which carries the error's IDENTITY so the editor can offer a failure-specific remedy — the validation state itself holds only a rendered String) is cleared at probe START, on success, on remote reload, and by every user-edit writer: URL / token-commit / cert / auth-scheme all route through VM setters that call `invalidateLiveValidation`. `validateRemoteAgent`'s own canonical write-back stays DIRECT (routing it through the invalidating setter would erase the mark it is about to set). A **per-ref validation generation** token guards the in-flight race: an edit mid-probe increments it, and a completion whose captured generation no longer matches is dropped whole — otherwise a stale probe writes its old URL back and re-greens a config the user has already changed.

**Transport-error classification:** `RemoteAgentTrustEvaluator.classifyTransportError(...)`. Load-bearing distinction: the SPECIFIC `.serverCertificate*` codes are an unambiguous cert rejection (→ TOFU / `…CertMismatch`), but the GENERIC `URLError.secureConnectionFailed` (and `.cancelled`) ALSO fire for transient cold-tunnel handshake failures → read as a cert problem ONLY when the trust layer actually rejected (`systemTrustRejected` or `pinRejected`, lock-guarded, read after the request returns); otherwise → retryable `unreachable`. Live/background paths that can't read per-challenge signals apply the minimal half (generic → retryable, specific cert codes → mismatch). Prevents a transient hiccup on a trusted cert from being mislabeled "Untrusted certificate."

**Custom-gateway model discovery.** `discoverModels`/`parseModelIDs` (sibling of Test Connection) best-effort-parses `GET /v1/models` to populate the custom-gateway editor's model-suggestion field — **never throws, returns `[]` on any failure** (field degrades to free-text). Parses the OpenAI `{data:[{id}]}`, a `{models:[…]}` variant, and a bare `["id",…]` array. Advertised ≠ loaded/active (the "ghost model" caveat is the user's responsibility). Built-ins don't call it.

**Discovery is tolerant; the VERDICT is strict — the asymmetry is deliberate.** A missed suggestion costs the user a free-text field; a wrong verdict costs them a broken gateway that claims to work. So `validateProbeBody` accepts ONLY the canonical `data` array, while `parseModelIDs` keeps the loose shapes. Concretely: LM Studio's NATIVE `/api/v1/models` returns `{models:[…]}` while its native chat route is NOT `/v1/chat/completions` — accepting that envelope as a verdict would green-light a base URL whose chat route doesn't exist. Do NOT "unify" the two parsers.

### Background URLSession identifiers + timeouts

Per-feature identifiers avoid delivery cross-talk; a two-hop turn uses two. Backend-neutral:

| Identifier | Hop | Surface |
|---|---|---|
| `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.converse` | Agent | iPhone/iPad |
| `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.watch.converse` | Agent | Watch |
| `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.carplay.converse` | Agent | CarPlay (`CarPlayConverseUploader`) |
| `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.filetransfer` | File up/download | iPhone/iPad (`BackgroundFileTransfer`; longer 600s/3600s timeouts for multi-MB bytes) |

Delegates wired via `.backgroundTask(.urlSession(...))` in `ConduckApp.swift` / `ConduckWatchApp.swift`, dispatched by identifier.

**Timeouts (`Constants.swift`; do NOT lower).** Self-hosted gateways on user hardware routinely take 1–4 min for LLM compute; the default `timeoutIntervalForRequest = 60` would kill as "Network Offline" mid-compute. `.converse` (iOS + Watch + CarPlay): `timeoutIntervalForRequest = 300`, `timeoutIntervalForResource = 600` (lifetime incl. delegate retries), `sessionSendsLaunchEvents = true`, `isDiscretionary = false`. `.stt` background session: 300s / 600s (shared ceiling with `.converse`, so slow self-hosted custom STT endpoints survive); foreground cloud STT requests keep the tight 120 s `requestTimeout`.

### Background-wake drain contract

Every `.backgroundTask(.urlSession(...))` handler awaits `handleBackgroundEvents()` and resumes only after `urlSessionDidFinishEvents` fires AND all reply-persistence work (reply append → status flip → notification) completes — waiter array + persistence counter, delegate-queue-confined (`BackgroundRemoteAgent`/`BackgroundSTT`/`CarPlayConverseUploader`) or MainActor-confined (`WatchAudioUploader`). Reply append PRECEDES the user-turn status flip + `FileTransferOutputDetector` probes + the notification, so a kill mid-handler can never persist "sent with no reply." Why: an immediately-returning handler lets the OS suspend/kill mid-persistence — silent reply loss.

### Send-state lifecycle (`Message.status`)

Every dispatch surface appends the user turn `status: "sending"`; the owning delegate terminalizes it — `sent` AFTER the reply append, `failed` on error AND on live cancel (macOS foreground cancel flips in the VM — no delegate there). Flips are EXACT per-message (`userMessageID` rides `RemoteAgentBackgroundMetadata`, additive/tolerant decode); conversation-wide `markPendingUserTurns` is the legacy-metadata fallback only. **Cross-launch `.cancelled` (force-quit resurrect = no in-memory task registry) maps to FAILURE, never a silent user-cancel.** Healing for slips: launch-time `ConversationStore.sweepStaleSendingUserTurns` (30-min grace; excludes conversations with live bg tasks; ANSWERED-TURN guard flips to `sent` when a later agent message exists) + a one-shot re-sweep at grace+1 min. Why: `retry()` requires `failed` — a stranded `sending` is an eternal spinner with no recovery. Watch user turns are the deliberate V1 exception (nil status; the wrist surfaces failures live via `surfaceTurnFailure`).

**Failure classification (atomic, race-converging).** `failTurn(messageID:classification:)` is the ONE writer that both flips `failed` AND persists `failureCode`/`failureWireCode`/`failureHadHistoryImages` in a single save, with an explicit transition rule so the racing writers (iOS delegate vs foreground VM; macOS VM vs share drainer) converge on the richest classification regardless of order: `sending`→`failed` writes the incoming classification (nils allowed); an already-`failed` turn with NO stored code accepts a coded upgrade in place; anything else is a no-op (a resolved turn is never disturbed). `failPendingUserTurns` = the conversation-wide legacy fallback. Cancel paths and pre-flight aborts flip STATUS-ONLY (`markPendingUserTurn`) — a cancel is not a gateway verdict, so any prior classification stays. **Retry claims atomically:** `beginRetry(messageID:)` compare-and-sets `failed`→`sending` FIRST (before any await in `retry()`); the loser of two rapid taps — or "Try again" racing "Resend without photo" — aborts (fail-fast, never a double dispatch). The claim KEEPS the classification; **only a `sent` flip clears it** (`applySendState` — the single site), matching "clear on successful retry". Every failure writer carries the classification: macOS VM catch arms + iOS `postTurnFailed` (wire code + the metadata-carried `requestHadHistoryImages`) + CarPlay `routeError` + share-drainer `failWithTurn` + `ConverseIntent` pre-dispatch (code only — no gateway verdict pre-dispatch).

### Audio-file cleanup

The agent hop's request body is written to a temp file for `uploadTask(with:fromFile:)` (OS does not auto-delete). The audio-cleanup mandate — canonically defined under STT Wire Formats — applies identically to both `.stt` and `.converse` hops.
```

---

## Agent File Transfer

Hands the agent's TOOLS a real arbitrary file (PDF/CSV/zip/…) and pulls an output file back — a layer ON TOP of the converse pipe. **BYO file-server**, no operator-provided binary: the user serves the agent workspace over **HTTPS WebDAV** — any server (rclone · Caddy · nginx · NAS); Conduck is a pure client and hosts nothing. Server provisioning is outsourced to `conduck-connect` (stands up + exposes the server, ships URL+credential in the pairing code — see Gateway Setup & Pairing) or the setup guide in the `conduck-connect` repo README (the app's "Read the setup guide" link); the in-app MANUAL path is the irreducible contract only — a machine-minted credential the server must accept, the `https://` URL, and a passing staged Test. **Credential generation is HIDDEN once the lane is `.ready`** — Why: a proven Keychain credential must not be casually overwritten before the server holds the new one, so rotation at `.ready` = Forget → set up again. **No capability probe** — there is no portable OpenAI-compatible signal for "agent loop with filesystem tools at this cwd", so a passing test proves only that Conduck can PUT/GET/DELETE bytes (never that the agent reads them); status stays `.optional` (ship-no-opinion). The undetectable class is SAID instead of probed: the setup guide's amber card carries THREE rules no test can catch (wrong served folder · narrower reach · **gateway tool policy denying the agent's read/write** — `fileTransfer.rule.toolPolicy`, pointing at re-running conduck-connect, which checks + offers the fix; gateway-specific config keys deliberately not named in-app, they drift). A plain model/proxy endpoint still chats with inline text/images but can't open files. Bound **per-gateway** (built-in OR custom) via `RemoteAgentRef.storageKeySuffix`.

**File-server auth = HTTP Basic** (`basicAuthHeaderValue` → `Authorization: Basic base64(username:password)`, username `conduck`) — distinct from the gateway's Bearer scheme.

**Routing.** Non-image files take the server route. A single pure `AttachmentDeliveryPlanner` (consumed by both composers AND `SharedInboxDrainer`, so the picker never decides semantics) routes composer text/code files (.txt/.md/.csv/.json/source) by extracted-UTF8 size + file-server presence + a per-turn inline budget: small (≤`textInlineMaxBytes` ≈32 KB, within `textInlineTurnBudgetBytes` ≈96 KB/turn) → `.dualText` (text rides inline as a fenced block AND the raw file uploads); large/over-budget → file-only (`.serverFile`); no file-server → inline-only. `.dualText` never gates Send (inline text is the fallback); on replay `priorTurns` drops the inline text once a `storedKey` exists (the file is then its only transport). **Fence trap** (`spliceText`): the backtick fence is chosen LONGER than the longest run in the content + labelled untrusted user data — Why: a Markdown/source file carrying its own ``` would otherwise break out of the fence (latent injection). **Composer images** take BOTH routes when a file-server is configured — the inline base64 vision part (downsized, EXIF/GPS-stripped JPEG) AND an eager background upload of the ORIGINAL raw bytes (true format, metadata intact), so the agent's tools act on a byte-faithful file while it SEES the inline copy. `spliceImageServerRefs` tells the agent the visible image is ALSO on disk and to NOT re-open it just to describe it (suppresses a redundant read). The dual-image `storedKey` PERSISTS on the attachment (`isServerReference=false`, inline bytes retained) so prior-turn images stay referenceable on replay (see Image history replay). The Share Extension drainer dual-routes images identically (see Share Extension → Per-type transport); headless image entry points (iOS Action-Button screenshot · macOS ⌘⇧2 capture) are inline-only by design — pure see, no file-server (see Trigger Architecture + Per-Surface → macOS). **Send is never gated on an image/dual upload** — `.dualImage`/`.dualText` are excluded from the upload send-blockers; if the eager upload hasn't landed by send, the content rides inline-only.

**Flow.** (1) eager **PUT** the bytes — `BackgroundFileTransfer`, dedicated `.filetransfer` background `URLSession`. Composer storedKey = `[<conversationID>/]<8-hex of a per-attachment UUID>__<sanitized>` (`makeStoredKey`); the share-drain mints a DISTINCT format `[<conversationID>/]<8-hex of envelopeID>-<sequence>__<sanitized>` (`deterministicStoredKey`) — Why: the drain must re-mint the SAME key on relaunch with no persisted per-attachment UUID (only `envelopeID`+`sequence` survive in the manifest), and `-<sequence>` guards two same-named files in one envelope → idempotent re-PUT recovery. EVERY file is namespaced under a **per-conversation folder** `<conversationID>/` via the single key-mint choke point (`mintStoredKey`) — Why: the agent cwd is shared across ALL conversations, so a flat root piles every chat's files together (also enables per-chat cleanup). Exception: a VM-less new-chat upload mints a FLAT key (no conversation exists yet). Every nested PUT is preceded by a best-effort **MKCOL** of the parent collection (`ensureCollection` — probe AND uploads; WebDAV never auto-creates a parent on PUT, RFC 4918 §9.7: rclone 409s, so without MKCOL the stock rig degrades to flat keys; 405 = already-exists is fine, the PUT stays the authoritative verdict). A nested PUT that STILL 409s runs the handshake's second half ONCE — explicit MKCOL + one re-PUT (`NestedPutParentMissing` sentinel through the upload continuation; 409-only — a PUT 405 means the target is a collection/method-disallowed, which MKCOL can't fix) — then fails visibly; this is the WebDAV create-parent handshake completing, the sole carve-out from the fail-fast NO-auto-retry policy (guaranteed while executing, may pause across suspension, unrecoverable after process death — same envelope as the relaunched-process drop). A Test-Connection nested-write probe (MKCOL→PUT→GET byte-echo) sets per-gateway `folderCapable`; a gateway that rejects the nested PUT even after MKCOL falls back to flat keys (see Silent capability re-probe below for the automatic upgrade path). (2) splice a PLAIN-TEXT line into the turn (`spliceServerFileRefs`, **never a content part** — the bytes already sit in the agent's working dir; a pure INPUT reference — output guidance deliberately does NOT live here because this splice replays on every prior turn), ordered base → text-file fences → server-file refs; prior-turn refs re-splice from `AttachmentRecord.isServerFile`. (2b) **per-turn file-delivery instruction** (`ConverseRequest.fileDeliveryInstruction`, spliced LAST by `assembleMessages`, NEWEST user turn only, gated `fileTransferReadySnapshot != nil` — so it rides attachment-LESS turns too; "write me a report.md" with nothing attached is the turn the ref splices can't cover): opens with the `[Conduck file transfer]` tag (a deterministic marker gateway-side agent instructions can scope on), tells the agent to write output files to the working-directory root and NAME them in plain reply text, and warns off channel-attachment directives (`MEDIA:` lines are STRIPPED by OpenClaw's OpenAI-compatible endpoint — verified live 2026-07-17: the reply arrives file-less and nameless, so detection correctly finds nothing). Wording is reply-direction-only (never "this channel has no attachments" — the request may visibly carry inline images). `BackgroundRemoteAgent.send` derives the gate internally from its `ref` (choke point — no caller can forget); the foreground client takes a REQUIRED `fileServerReady` param (macOS VM + drainer call sites derive it). CarPlay + Watch pass the gate truthfully too (their chips arrive via the retroactive scan below): CarPlay computes it in-process (`CarPlayRecordingService` → REQUIRED `uploadConverse` param); the wrist — which never holds the file-server credential — reads the per-ref readiness map synced via `RemoteAgentBroadcastEnvelope.fileTransferAvailable` (see Cross-Device Sync; a stale `true` is a bounded degradation — a promised chip just arrives late via retro-scan, never a crash or credential exposure). (2c) **spoken-summary instruction** (`ConverseRequest.spokenSummaryInstruction`, spliced AFTER the delivery clause, newest turn only, when `assembleMessages(surface: .spoken)` — CarPlay + Watch uploaders hardcode `.spoken`, every other surface defaults `.standard` with byte-identical wire): opens with the `[Conduck voice]` scoping tag (mirrors `[Conduck file transfer]`), tells the agent the reply will likely be HEARD on a compact hands-busy surface — if the work produces a file/long artifact, say the exact filename and summarize the useful result in 1–3 spoken-friendly sentences, never recite long contents/code/URLs aloud. Deliberately makes NO delivery promise → rides voice turns even with no file lane (the agent can still write inside its own workspace). (3) **output detection** (`FileTransferOutputDetector`): scan the reply for filename tokens with an allowlisted extension (defeats `e.g.`/`v1.1`/`example.com`), dedup, EXCLUDE the conversation's own inbound storedKeys (non-agent turns' keys + a nested key's last path component, pre-cap — Why: the turn text tells the agent each upload's stored name, so a reply merely echoing it probes `.exists` and would chip the user's own file back at them), cap 5, ranged-GET-probe each (`probeExistsWithLength` — `Range: bytes=0-0`; the total is read from `Content-Range` on a 206, or `Content-Length` ONLY on a 200, so the chip shows the size without pulling the file) → persist a server-ref `Attachment` on the ASSISTANT turn → chip downloads (a >100 MB download soft-confirms, mirroring the upload side) → **Quick Look preview** (SwiftUI `.quickLookPreview`, both platforms) over a scratch copy adopted to `tmp/AgentFileDownloads/<UUID>/<filename>` (`AgentDownloadScratch` — clean leaf so QL resolves type + title; an extension-less leaf infers one from the MIME, an existing extension always wins). The SAME chip renders on USER bubbles for sent server-route files (their bytes live on the file-server too — the chip re-pulls them; role-aware styling mirrors `InlineTextFileChip`), so both directions preview identically. INLINE text/code attachments (`isText` — every non-image, non-server-reference type, any gateway, file-server or not) preview through the SAME presenter with ZERO network: `InlineTextFileChip` writes the locally-persisted `extractedText` bytes into the same scratch store (`AgentDownloadScratch.adopt(data:)` — same leaf/extension contract) and hands the file up; a record with no local text (partially-synced CloudKit row / non-UTF-8 decode miss) renders as a passive label. ONE presenter per thread (`FilePreviewCoordinator` at the `ConversationThreadView` root, latest-tap-wins claim tokens — never per-chip: macOS `QLPreviewPanel` is app-shared, row-local presenters in the recycling `LazyVStack` would compete for it). No idle glyph — the chip itself is the tap target. The QL chrome's share button covers share + Save-to-Files on iOS; macOS keeps the durable save via a visible neutral-tint "Save As…" chip button (+ context-menu mirror) → `NSSavePanel` (copy runs off-main). Re-tap re-downloads — no cache; the agent may have rewritten the file. Gated on a configured file-server → ZERO network in the common reply. Probes stream via `bytes(for:)` + immediate task cancel on response — the verdict + length come from HEADERS, so a `Range`-ignoring BYO server's full 200 never buffers the file into memory. **Retroactive scan (Watch/CarPlay landings).** Those landing paths run NO detection at reply time; at a later thread open on iPhone/iPad/Mac, `ConversationDetailViewModel.runRetroOutputScan` re-runs the SAME detector over agent turns whose `sourceDevice` base is `watch`/`carplay` and `outputScanDone != true` (newest-first, cap 20/pass; snapshot + inbound-exclusion resolved ONCE per pass; each turn's already-attached storedKeys pre-excluded, so a prior PARTIAL success retries only its missing candidates — never gate candidacy on "has a server ref"). Persistence = `ConversationStore.reconcileOutputScan`: ONE background save for the pass, transaction-local per-storedKey dedupe, sequences continue after the message's max, `Message.outputScanDone = true` stamped ONLY when every probe was conclusive (`.exists`/`.missing`; auth/5xx/unknown leave it unset → retried at the NEXT thread open — a per-VM attempted-set stops reload-echo re-probes, honoring no-silent-retry). No snapshot → the pass aborts UNMARKED (configuring file transfer later still chips); a mid-pass lane repoint (snapshot `identitySignature` drift) aborts unpersisted. CloudKit has no distributed compare-and-set — near-simultaneous passes on two devices can merge duplicate rows, so the render layer dedupes server chips by storedKey (`MessageRowFormatters.dedupedServerFiles`, applied on iOS/macOS AND the Watch bubble: a preview-bearing row — `thumbnailData`/`previewKind` — beats a preview-less one, then lowest sequence). **Preview enrichment (wrist-viewable output files).** TWO-PHASE, chips first: after a landing's chips persist + complete (and after each retro-scan reconcile), a detached best-effort pass downloads BOUNDED content from the user's own server and patches the rows — a slow/failed download can never delay or block chip creation, reply delivery, or notifications. Lanes: previewable-TEXT extension (`AttachmentRecord.isPreviewableTextFilename` — extension-based, NOT MIME: agent output carries `application/*` types for json/yaml/shell) ≤128 KiB → strict-UTF-8 `previewData` + `previewKind="text"`; image extension (png/jpg/jpeg/gif — svg excluded, ImageIO can't rasterize) source ≤8 MiB → `ImageProcessor.thumbnailOnly` (ImageIO downsample, full bitmap never decoded, decode success = content proof, ≤128 KiB hard max) → the ordinary `thumbnailData` field. Fetch = `BackgroundFileTransfer.fetchBounded` (`Range: bytes=0-cap` + client-side hard stop past cap — a Range-ignoring 200 can't buffer past it; bytes actually received are charged against the budget even on an over-cap bail). Budgets, sequential in draft order (skip-too-big, continue-to-smaller): per-reply 8 MiB source / 512 KiB stored; retro-pass global 16 MiB / 1 MiB across its ≤20 replies. Persistence = `ConversationStore.applyPreviews` — one background save, match `(messageID, storedKey, isServerReference)`, FIRST-WRITER-WINS per nil field (concurrent devices can't clobber), identity-drift re-check before it (mirrors the retro guard). Best-effort ONCE: no retry state, no backfill of older preview-less chips; capable devices' chip tap still downloads FRESH bytes — the preview is a snapshot. PRIVACY: enrichment copies bounded content from the user's self-hosted server into their own iCloud conversation store (developer-blind both sides) — a deliberate replication-boundary extension; the pipeline logs no filenames/storedKeys/content.

**Image history replay.** Prior images replay under the per-gateway `ImageHistoryPolicy` (cross-target; key `imageHistory.policy.<refSuffix>`, dual-stored App Group + iCloud KVS): **`.recent`** (default) = newest `imageInlineWindow` (3) image-bearing turns inline · **`.extended`** = newest (10) · **`.all`** = every image-bearing turn inline (for a gateway whose vision-on-disk re-read degrades). The current turn is never trimmed. Beyond the window an aged ALL-KEYED turn DROPS the inline base64 and splices an imperative on-disk reference (`spliceImageTextRefs` — distinct from the suppress-read `spliceImageServerRefs`; tells the agent to OPEN/read the file before answering) from the persisted `storedKey`. A turn with ANY unkeyed image (un-uploaded or no file-server) stays inline through the **orphan grace** (`imageOrphanInlineWindow` = 10, an absolute depth on the newest-first image-turn counter), then EXPIRES: keyed subset → disk refs, unkeyed remainder → the count-honest `spliceImageUnavailableNote` — bounds the token burn of never-uploaded images. **Why a window:** prior-image re-feed drives the ~10× image-turn latency; the agent re-sees on demand via its `read`/`vision_analyze` tool. Replay is assembled by the shared `ConversationHistoryAssembler` on EVERY dispatch surface (incl. headless `ConverseIntent`, CarPlay, Watch — the wrist always runs `.recent`, the policy being App-Group-only and never broadcast); `priorTurns`' honesty floor guarantees an image-bearing turn whose bytes can't ride splices a disk ref or a count-honest note instead of flattening to bare text (see Remote Agent Round-Trip → Request shaping). **Gateway-side reality (keep the client agnostic):** OpenClaw's stateless `/v1/chat/completions` forwards ONLY the newest user message's `image_url` parts and silently discards prior-turn inline images — so the disk-ref route is the ONLY prior-image memory there (headless screenshots, never uploaded, are one-shot). Hermes forwards ALL inline images every request (hard 10 MB request-body cap — `.all` on a long image thread can 400). Net: the inline window is a real cost/latency lever on Hermes + bare custom endpoints, not on OpenClaw.

**Locked decisions.**
| Decision | Detail / Why |
|---|---|
| **Existence = ranged GET, NEVER HEAD** | a read-only HEAD/GET 200 false-positives on a Control-UI HTML page; rclone answers GET with bytes — the only reliable signal. The probe is capped `Range: bytes=0-0` so an existence check can NEVER pull a large output file into memory (an unranged GET of an agent-written `export.zip` would OOM); a server that ignores `Range` still 200s the full body (parsed identically). `parseProbeOutcome`: 200/206/416 → `.exists` (416 = present-but-empty: shorter than the probe range; a missing file 404s), 404 → `.missing`, 401/403 → `.unauthorized`, 5xx → `.serverError`. The real chip download (`buildDownloadRequest`) carries no `Range`. |
| **Eager-on-attach + strict send-gating** | upload starts on stage; the turn dispatches only after every upload lands (Send disabled on `hasLoadingItem ‖ hasUploadingItem ‖ hasFailedUpload ‖ hasNeedsSetupItem`). EVERY dispatch path mirrors the full chain (both bars + iPad ⌘Return) — `pendingAttachments` silently OMITS a non-uploaded tile and post-send `clear()` deletes its staging temp, so an ungated path destroys the file. Fail-fast + visible Retry, NO silent retry (a suspended bg `uploadTask` does not resume). Retry REUSES the staged `storedKey` (overwrites the partial blob, never orphans a fresh key). |
| **Staged Test Connection** | `reachability → auth → PUT → GET → DELETE`; sets `fileTransferAvailable` ONLY on a full pass. The GET stage **byte-echoes** — the returned body must EQUAL the bytes just PUT, not merely 200 — so a read-only Control-UI or a uniform-200 auth-wall / SSO proxy that serves its own HTML to every path can't false-pass. The nested-write capability probe byte-echoes identically. |
| **ONE unified importer; no-server binaries = blocking `.needsSetup` tile** | a single broad-UTType importer (`.content`/`.data`/`.image`/`.audiovisualContent`; **`.item` deliberately ABSENT** — it admits folders, which `copyItem` staging would recursively duplicate; the stage loops also `isDirectoryURL`-skip). The CLASSIFIER routes (image → inline/dual · text probe → planner · binary → server), never the picker — same classifier for macOS `.onDrop` + iPad ⌘V paste. Binary + NO file-server → a blocking amber `.needsSetup` tile gating Send (`hasNeedsSetupItem`). On setup completing, `promoteNeedsSetupTiles` flips tiles to `.serverFile` + kicks the eager PUT; promotion is IDEMPOTENT (mint-then-recheck, no await between flip and kick — one save posts the notification 3×) and REF-STAMPED (a tile staged for gateway A never silently uploads to switched-to gateway B). Resolve the composer's ref AUTHORITATIVELY from the conversation's persisted `backend` (`fetchConversation`), **NOT `vm.boundRef`** (async-populated nil-fallback leaks the default gateway's config onto an unconfigured conversation). |
| **VM-less composer uploads under FLAT storedKeys** | `uploadServerFile`/`deleteOrphanServerFile` are STATIC (zero instance state, snapshot via `SettingsManager`); a brand-new (VM-less) composer stages + eagerly uploads with `folder: nil`. Why: `.needsSetup` blocks Send but the VM only mints ON send — a VM-gated upload deadlocks the new-chat flow. Covers BOTH binaries AND images — a first-turn image on a fresh chat dual-routes (inline vision + flat-key original upload) exactly like a later turn; the image path carries no VM gate. |
| **No hard size cap** (user's own server) | >`fileTransferSoftConfirmBytes` (100 MB) → a soft confirm BEFORE staging (the `isPresented` binding setter is a deliberate NO-OP — SwiftUI sets it false on EVERY button incl. Attach, a cancel-in-setter would discard the confirmed file). Staging runs OFF the main actor — every blocking file op (the `copyItem` file-to-file copy, the ≤100 MB image byte read, the `original.write` temp, the `TextFileExtractor.extract`) hops to a background executor so a slow / iCloud-not-downloaded / external-volume pick never freezes the UI; a multi-file pick stages the urls SEQUENTIALLY (one ordered `@MainActor` task, not per-url concurrent tasks) so tiles AND the wire `sequence` preserve selection order. The text-vs-binary probe (`TextFileExtractor.extract`) is guarded by `textProbeMaxBytes` (10 MB) + UTType (audiovisual/archive skip to binary) in BOTH composers AND `SharedInboxDrainer`. An image over the soft-confirm threshold falls through to the binary route ONLY when a file-server is configured (its sole no-heap-load transport); with no file-server it stays inline (the rare, off-main heap-read is accepted over failing the send) — both composers AND the drainer apply this identically. No auto server-side cleanup on conversation delete (V1). |
| **PROPFIND browser deferred (V1.1)** | the 207-multistatus parser (`parsePropfindBody`, tolerant — never throws) is built + unit-tested; the consuming browser is V1.1. The output path uses the single GET probe. |

**Storage (per-ref, `RemoteAgentRef.storageKeySuffix`).** `fileServer.{url,available,folderCapable}.<suffix>` → App-Group UserDefaults + iCloud KVS, **with an inbound mirror**: `handleiCloudChange` prefix-scans exactly these THREE prefixes into App-Group defaults, and `performInitialSync` hydrates them cold-launch — both passes share ONE prefix-list constant (never a blanket `fileServer.` scan), so a second device assembles a ready lane from sync alone (URL+flags via KVS, credential via iCloud Keychain; its missing per-device cert pin degrades a self-signed setup to a VISIBLE per-upload failure, never a silent-off lane). The hydration is **inbound-only — deliberately NO local→KVS push-up** (unlike the gateway-URL blocks): a push-up cannot distinguish "configured while signed out" from "deleted remotely while offline", so it would resurrect a Forgotten server into KVS and ping-pong it across devices; a signed-out-configured lane stays device-local until any re-save dual-writes it. **Every config-identity mutation** (URL save, pairing import, credential regeneration, Forget) calls the single actor choke point `revokeFileTransferReadiness(for:)` BEFORE writing the new value: it dual-writes `available=false` (reaches KVS no later than the new config — a peer must never pair new config with a stale synced verdict), forfeits `testedLocally`, and clears the silent-probe markers (a surviving marker would disarm the re-probe against the replacement server). A FAILED credential rotation restores the prior `available`/`testedLocally` (the old credential is still live — a working lane must not stay demoted by a write that never happened). Test passes write `folderCapable` BEFORE `available=true` and drop the probe markers (a fresh staged verdict supersedes any recorded silent-probe outcome). `fileServer.certFingerprint.<suffix>` → App-Group **only** (per-device pin, NEVER KVS, never mirrored); `fileServer.credential.<suffix>` → **Keychain** (`kSecAttrSynchronizable: true`, same service/shape as the gateway token). Device-local probe provenance (App-Group only, never KVS/mirrored): `fileServer.testedLocally.<suffix>` = THIS device ran a passing staged test (one-time seed converts pre-mirror local `available=true` — which only a local test could have written — into the marker; seeded before the mirror's first write); `fileServer.folderProbeRevision.<suffix>` + `fileServer.folderProbeAttempt.<suffix>` = silent-re-probe bookkeeping. The client is **root-relative at runtime** — uploads always target the WebDAV root, so there is no folder-override key or field (`fileServer.folderCapable.<suffix>`, the nested-write capability probe, is a DIFFERENT mechanism and remains). `fileTransferSnapshot(for:)` = atomic `{baseURL, username (=`conduck`), credential, certFingerprintHex?, available}`, nil unless URL AND credential present. `RemoteAgentBroadcastEnvelope.fileTransferAvailable: Bool` broadcasts the READY gate (`fileTransferReadySnapshot(for:) != nil` — URL + readable credential + `available`; NOT the raw `available` flag, which can disagree when the credential is unreadable) per gateway; the Watch persists a per-ref readiness map (`WatchSettingsReader`, App-Group, rebuilt WHOLESALE per accepted multi-envelope so an omitted ref drops to false, cold-launch hydrated) and uses it ONLY to gate the per-turn file-delivery instruction — the wrist still has no transfer UI or download lane.

**Silent capability re-probe (upgrade-only).** `FileTransferCapabilityRefresher.refreshIfNeeded()` — launch-chained after `performInitialSync` on BOTH platform seams (iOS `ConduckApp.init`, macOS `AppDelegate.applicationDidFinishLaunching`) — re-runs the nested-write probe for refs where `folderCapable == false` AND `testedLocally` AND `fileTransferReadySnapshot != nil`, once per `Constants.fileServerFolderProbeRevision` (bumped when the probe/upload algorithm changes — the unstick lever for verdicts persisted by an older algorithm), 24 h backoff between attempts. `FileServerClient.probeFolderCapability` is tri-state (`capable`/`rejected`/`indeterminate` — MKCOL status INSPECTED: a nested-PUT 409 after an inconclusive MKCOL proves nothing and stays indeterminate, never a persisted verdict); the staged test consumes it collapsed to Bool. Writes: `capable` → `folderCapable=true` (dual-write, upgrades peers) + revision marker; `rejected` → marker only; `indeterminate` → nothing. A pre-write apply-guard re-fetches the snapshot and requires `FileTransferSnapshot.identitySignature` unchanged (the ONE definition of lane identity, shared with DiagnosticsRunner's stale-result guard) — a config edited mid-probe drops the result. The markers are per-identity, not forever: `revokeFileTransferReadiness` (any config mutation) and every staged-test pass clear them, re-arming one upgrade-only probe for the new identity / fresh verdict. It NEVER degrades `folderCapable`, NEVER touches `available` (the staged Test Connection stays the only trust gate), never probes a server this device hasn't locally tested (no automated writes / surprise Local-Network prompt at a synced-only peer; the flat-stuck device is by definition locally tested, and its upgrade syncs). Topology limit (accepted): a lane whose originally-tested device is gone stays flat until some device re-runs a Test Connection.

**Privacy (load-bearing).** Credential is **machine-minted, never user-invented** — client-minted (32 lowercase hex, `SystemRandomNumberGenerator`) or minted server-side by `conduck-connect` (`openssl rand -hex 16`) and delivered via the pairing payload. Plaintext appears ONLY in (a) Keychain, (b) the intent-gated, session-only, masked-by-default credential row the user deliberately reveals (dropped on dismiss, never read back from Keychain) — `regenerate` AWAITS the Keychain write before flipping the `credentialPresent` UI bool, so a swallowed failure can't show a phantom credential. **https only** (rejected at the validation layer). NEVER log a URL / token / credential / storedKey / filename / reply. Temp-file `defer { try? removeItem }` everywhere: the upload body is a throwaway COPY (`BackgroundFileTransfer` deletes the URL it's given on enqueue); downloads return a caller-owned temp. The Quick Look path is the sole, deliberate relaxation: the download chip adopts the temp into its per-download `AgentFileDownloads` scratch dir, reclaimed on preview dismiss (iOS) and by a 24 h age-bounded sweep at launch + on each adoption on both platforms — macOS never deletes on panel close because the panel's "Open with" hands the target app the live path (files are scratch retention, never a reusable cache). CarPlay/headless: blocked (no file picker). A bg upload that completes after a process-kill is NOT recovered — the staged tile is ephemeral; the bytes are a harmless orphan on the user's own server.

---

## Gateway Setup & Pairing (`conduck-connect` + QR import)

Reduces BYO-gateway setup friction **without operating any infrastructure** (no-backend invariant intact). Two halves: a server-side pairing wizard (`conduck-connect`, published at https://github.com/gigaduckai/conduck-connect) and the app-side import (Settings surfaces). The manual path (type URL + token into Settings) always survives; pairing is an accelerant, never a requirement.

### Three blessed HTTPS exposure paths (co-equal — present trade-offs, let the user pick)

The gateway must be reachable at an `https://` URL. Three first-class paths + one advanced; **no auto-recommendation** (honest trade-offs, user picks). Each row = what the user gives up.

| Path | Apple-device install | Who can reach it | Who sees content | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Tailscale (Serve)** | Tailscale app on EVERY Conduck device | tailnet-only | nobody in between | **Standalone Watch CANNOT reach a tailnet-only gateway** (no watchOS Tailscale) — dominant Watch failure mode; teach at path-choice time |
| **Tailscale Funnel** | none | public | TLS passthrough, E2E | own option, not a Serve sub-option |
| **Cloudflare Tunnel** | none | public | CF edge sees plaintext (`device → CF edge → gateway`) | ~$8/yr domain; needs `cloudflared` gateway-side |
| **Own HTTPS** (reverse proxy / VPS / custom cert) | none | user-chosen | `device → gateway` | one option: user gives the URL; script auto-detects publicly-trusted (no pin) vs self-signed (pins the SPKI it computes). Broken cert (expired / wrong host) STOPS the run rather than pinning it |

### `conduck-connect.sh` — server-side pairing wizard

Runs on the **user's gateway host** (Linux + macOS). Detection turns "choose your path" into a confirmation, not a reading exercise.

| Phase | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Entry | No arguments opens a welcome/action menu: Setup · Check existing OpenAI-compatible software not built for Conduck · Check an adapter built specifically for Conduck · Show a saved code (when available) · Exit. The canonical public flags skip that menu: `--setup`, `--check-server [url]`, `--check-adapter [url]`, `--show-code`. Each public action has ONE documented spelling. One hidden compatibility arm remains for already-shipped App Store clients: `--generic` enters custom-server setup directly and skips OpenClaw/Hermes detection, preserving that client's original lane semantics without advertising the retired flag in help or menus. Other retired spellings return named errors that point to their replacement. Process status is stable: `0` = success or PASS, `1` = setup/runtime failure or a completed check's FAIL, `2` = invalid command-line use; signal exits keep their conventional `128 + signal` status. |
| Detect | `--setup` detects OpenClaw (`~/.openclaw/openclaw.json`) and Hermes (`~/.hermes/`), reports what it found, then always asks which gateway to configure: each detected install plus another OpenAI-compatible server/adapter. Detection informs the choice but never makes it; even one detected install has no default. Reads ports from the selected install's config; OpenClaw token from `gateway.auth.token` (runtime source, NOT the `.env` seed), Hermes from `API_SERVER_KEY`. |
| Configure | Enables the chat endpoint when off (#1 setup failure — OpenClaw `gateway.http.endpoints.chatCompletions.enabled=true` + restart; Hermes `API_SERVER_*` append + restart). Fallback token entry hidden (`read -s`, never echoed). Generic: probes `/v1/models` to pre-fill the model when exactly one returns. **User-entered gateway base URLs are normalized to the app's own rule** (`normalizedGatewayBaseURL` parity — strip exactly one terminal `/v1/chat/completions`/`/v1/models`/`/v1` segment-wise, drop query+fragment, keep port + path prefix; users paste the full endpoint their server's docs name, and both client and script append `/v1/…` themselves). **Hermes port guard:** an `API_SERVER_PORT` of 8645 (the tool-less `hermes proxy`) is challenged with an explicit confirm — it chats fine, so no downstream check would catch the silent tools/memory loss. |
| Exposure | The genuine human decision — interactive menu (NO default) of four paths, each row terse (one consequence + one decisive constraint; the standalone-Watch rule stays at path-choice time); answering `?` prints an ADDITIVE plain-words comparison (who-can-reach / install / who-sees-traffic / Watch) and re-asks — one voice, never a language fork, never a recommendation. The public-vs-private reach question is likewise a no-default explicit choice (it gates the keyless-public guard) with its own `?` explainer, which teaches "unsure → answer public" as the fail-safe direction (a wrong "private" SKIPS protections; a wrong "public" only adds checks). For the merged own-HTTPS path the script auto-classifies the cert on a real run: trusted (`curl` no `-k` passes) → no pin; untrusted/self-signed but valid (dates checked BOTH ways — expired AND not-yet-valid) → compute + pin SPKI; broken dates → **STOP** with an explicit "pin anyway" override (pinning bypasses chain checks, so a broken cert must not be papered over). **HTTPS port auto-selected, not asked:** Serve prefers 443/reuses an existing same-backend mapping; a same-backend mapping with the OPPOSITE verb is flipped **in place** (explicit confirm in BOTH directions; funnel→serve drops the AllowFunnel flag explicitly, then verifies the verb) — never re-allocated to a fresh port, which would leave the old exposure live. On a private choice, `sweep_stale_public_funnels` surfaces leftover public Funnels of the same backend on OTHER ports (gateway AND file lane) with an offer to remove; the removal is INTENTIONAL so it is deliberately never snapshotted into `APPLIED`/`FS_APPLIED` — a rollback must never re-publish an exposure the user just asked to kill. **Funnel is constrained to the only permitted ports {443, 8443, 10000}** (Tailscale hard limit). Inspect-before-change parses `tailscale serve status --json` (**fail-closed**), re-parses status afterward to confirm every mutation, and treats an unreadable status as "unproven", never as success. Snapshots are recorded only AFTER the user confirms, so a declined prompt leaves no undo record for an untouched port; cleanup replays records in reverse order so the earliest-recorded prior state wins. Typos re-prompt (never abort) and a closed stdin aborts rather than looping — **every `$()`-captured prompt writes its human output to stderr** (a retry warning on stdout would be captured as the answer, letting a typo silently decide a safety question) and pairs with `|| die` (a `die` inside `$()` kills only the subshell). `b` goes back only before any exposure change (Step-2 config changes are detect-and-reuse on re-entry; gateway globals reset so answers never leak across the back-loop). |
| File lane | Optional per-gateway rclone WebDAV unit (`conduck-files-<gwid>`). Detects + reuses an existing unit — never duplicates. **OpenClaw agent-side readiness (v0.9.0, runs FIRST — before any unit/exposure work, so a bail-out leaves nothing behind):** `openclaw_tool_policy_step` parses `tools.{profile,allow,alsoAllow,deny}` (JSON5-tolerant) and grades it — `ok`/`none` pass silently; `fix` shows the exact per-key before→after (minimum relaxation: `group:fs` in deny REPLACED by `edit`+`apply_patch` so read/write free while mutating members stay denied; allow-mode appends `read`/`write`/`pdf` to `allow` — never `alsoAllow`, the two are mutually exclusive per scope; profile-mode ensures `pdf` via `alsoAllow` [+`read`/`write` for minimal/messaging]; `full` needs nothing) and applies via the same `config set --batch-json` + restart machinery as Step 2, re-reading the config afterwards (never trusts the write); `manual` (wildcard deny matching a file tool · allow+alsoAllow both set) warns, never auto-rewrites. Declining the fix NEVER silently drops the lane — loud consequence + explicit keep/drop confirm (consent-ladder idiom; byte transport still works). Then `install_conduck_tools_block`: a marker-delimited (`<!-- conduck-connect:begin/end -->`) agent-guidance block in the workspace `TOOLS.md` — attachments are on disk (never web-search them) · media/pdf tools need the ABSOLUTE workspace path (container path `/home/node/.openclaw/workspace` named only for the stock Docker install + default workspace; unknown mappings get generic wording) · return files by writing to the root and NAMING them in plain reply text, never `MEDIA:` — **scoped to turns containing the app's `[Conduck file transfer]` wire tag** so the same agent's messaging channels (where `MEDIA:` is correct) are untouched. Idempotent (refresh-in-place between markers), symlink- and malformed-marker-refusing, confirm-gated, notes that bootstrap files load at session START only. Both honor `--dry-run` (plan + read-only verdict) and `--reuse-only` (report, never offer). Reuses the file lane's OWN existing HTTPS exposure when present. Scope-mismatch (lane reach ≠ gateway's) triggers a 3-way `require_choice` (no default, `?` explainer — number-free, since the reuse-only menus number differently): align (promote private→public = a SECOND confirm; or demote public→private), omit, or include-as-is (advanced — the lone scope-ambiguous case, since the payload carries one gateway-oriented `transport`); every option row names its consequence (where attachments work, what stays public). **Fail-closed rollback:** file-lane exposure tracked separately (`FS_APPLIED`); a dropped lane rolls back its exposure so a public Funnel is never left live while omitted from the QR; the gateway pairing is untouched. Rollback success is **proven by a status re-parse** — unconfirmed ports keep their undo record and print the manual undo commands immediately, never an "all clear" on faith. An unproven rollback also sets `FS_ROLLBACK_INCOMPLETE`, the one condition under which the EXIT backstop still prints undo hints after a successful emit: a green QR must never end a run that left a file server reachable. **Credential is a 32-hex secret in a `0600` state file, passed to rclone via env (`RCLONE_PASS`), never on argv** (argv leaks via the process table); reused, never silently rotated. |
| Verify | Real requests BEFORE the phone is involved, with normal TLS validation (self-signed via `--pinnedpubkey` against the computed SPKI, never blanket `-k`); the bearer token / WebDAV credential ride stdin curl configs, never argv (`ps`-visible): local health (any answer < 500 proves it's up — the loopback probe carries no token, so an auth-gated health route answering 401 is still healthy) → `/v1/models` returns **any 2xx + the canonical `{"data":[…]}` envelope** (HTML → a *hedged, kind-aware* diagnosis with the HTTP status shown — OpenClaw/Hermes: endpoint-off named as the likely cause with a proxy/login interstitial as the alternative; custom: a wider web-page diagnosis (reverse proxy / login page / wrong base address) that deliberately makes no endpoint-off claim — same likelihood-not-assertion rule as the app's `endpointDisabledRemedy`; a 401/500 JSON error body is a failure; valid JSON *without* a top-level `data` array is its own wrong-shape diagnosis pointing at the adapter contract — the script's check matches the app's strict `validateProbeBody`, so a pairing code can't green-light what the app's probe would reject; an EMPTY `data` array verifies green but warns, mirroring the app's `.okNoModels`; any other failure prints its concrete sub-cause — DNS / connection-refused / timeout / TLS / pinned-key-mismatch from the curl exit code, or the HTTP status — never a lossy "unreachable or rejected") → live `/v1/chat/completions` pong (JSON body built by a real encoder, never string-spliced; `--max-time 300`, matching the app's converse request timeout; green requires any 2xx and the Apple-authoritative reply shape: strict JSON, the complete `choices` array decodes eagerly, every choice carries a STRING `message.content`, and only choice zero is returned; an empty string is valid, while `content:null`, non-string content, a malformed later choice, or an error body fails) → file-lane PUT→GET→DELETE (DELETE best-effort, matching the app's delete semantics — a failed delete leaves a stray probe file, never blocks). **The pairing code emits only on all-green** — the app's Test Connection becomes a confirmation, not a diagnosis. |
