| Emit | Terminal QR rendered locally by the already-required `python3` (vendored stdlib-only encoder — no `qrencode`, no install, no web QR service) + a copyable paste string, both warned as token-bearing. When the QR won't fit the terminal width, prints the paste string + "widen and re-run" (paste always works). Points the user to Settings → Personal AI → the setup-code option **label-agnostically — the script must NOT name a single UI label or its row position** (they drift with the UI). Every successful **wizard** emit (incl. `--reuse-only` — but NEVER `--show-code`, whose configuration state is read-only: rewriting there could permanently strip a lane a transient probe failure dropped from one emission) also refreshes a **non-secret profile** `$STATE_DIR/profile-<gwid>.json` (`0600`, `schemaVersion:1`): routing facts only — gateway `id/kind/auth/transport/reach/url` + optional `name/localPort/model/certFP`, and a `fileServer` block (`url` + optional `localPort/certFP/folder`) only when a lane shipped — **never a token/credential**; a profile-write failure WARNs, never sinks a completed pairing. |
| Re-show (`--show-code`) | Re-emits a saved profile's QR with **no setup questions and no configuration changes** (`REUSE_ONLY` forced on, so a stray configuration mutation dies via `mutate_guard`). It still performs live verification requests; when a file lane is configured, that verification PUTs, GETs, then best-effort DELETEs a small probe file. It MAY still ask: a numbered profile pick, a custom gateway's bearer token, or a gateway-only confirm — never a setup/exposure question. **Never rewrites the profile** (configuration-read-only — see Emit). Discovers `$STATE_DIR/profile-*.json` (none → friendly "run setup once"; several → numbered pick; unknown `schemaVersion` → polite stop). **Validates the loaded schema before anything else** (fail-closed against hand-edits/corruption — a bad profile must die here, never emit a code the apps reject): kind ∈ {openclaw, hermes, custom} (custom requires `name`), auth ∈ {bearer, none}, `https://`-only URLs with a host (gateway AND fileServer), transport ∈ the wizard's own vocabulary (selfsigned additionally requires `certFP`), numeric 1–65535 ports, 64-lowercase-hex fingerprints. Reconstructs `GW_*` (health path from kind, missing local port re-detected from the gateway config — derived facts are never trusted blindly from the file) and re-derives secrets from their canonical homes (OpenClaw `gateway.auth.token` · Hermes `API_SERVER_KEY` · custom+bearer → paste, never stored · `none` → none) — **fail-closed: a bearer profile whose token can't be read DIES, never emits keyless**. File-lane credential recovered via the unit/state-cred file; unrecoverable → loud warn + explicit confirm to continue gateway-only; a lane that fails the live probe → explicit confirm to emit gateway-only THIS time (profile keeps its lane) or die. **Expected-vs-live mismatch gate (reads only, runs BEFORE verify so drift reads as "your setup changed", not a generic failure):** tailscale/funnel → `TS_STATE_KNOWN` required + the profile URL's **hostname must be one of this machine's live serve hostnames** (a profile naming another tailnet machine refuses) + its https port must carry the reach-implied verb (serve=private / funnel=public) proxying to `127.0.0.1:<localPort>` (gateway AND lane); selfsigned → recompute SPKI vs the pinned `certFP` (a changed cert refuses — no silent re-pin); cloudflare/public → no local introspection (reachability proven by verify). Any drift → refuse with a **secret-free** expected/live diff + "re-run setup to reconcile". |
| Adapter check (`--check-adapter [url]`) | Black-box **adapter-contract conformance check** — grades an adapter **built for Conduck** against conduck.com/setup/adapter/v1/ (**contract revision 1.3**). Runs against any OpenAI-compatible server technically, but all user-facing copy scopes it to built-for-Conduck adapters: grading OpenClaw/Hermes with it yields false FAILs (their keyless mode is legitimate app-side, contract-forbidden adapter-side). The check itself performs no setup, exposure, QR, or saved-state mutation (`REUSE_ONLY` forced on); it refuses `--dry-run`/`--show-code`/setup-selection flags. **Checks** (each verdict + detail line prefixed with its stable `[CHECK_ID]`): `MODELS_ENVELOPE` — canonical `/v1/models` envelope via the same `models_is_json` (empty `data` = FAIL; no usable string `id` = FAIL; **>15s = FAIL**, mirroring the app's Test-Connection give-up, timed by curl's `%{time_total}`; `Content-Type: application/json` required, params tolerated; also captures id count + first id for the selection probe — adapter-check-only globals, wizard grading untouched). `AUTH_{MODELS,CHAT}_{MISSING,WRONG}` — **auth ENFORCED on BOTH routes the app calls**: a missing AND a wrong token must each 401 on `/v1/models` **and** `/v1/chat/completions` (gating only the model list while the tool-running chat route sits open is the exact hole this catches; keyless run = single `AUTH_NOT_ENFORCED` FAIL); 401-body shape stays a soft warn, now requiring non-empty `message` AND `type`. `CHAT_BASIC` — live turn deliberately WITHOUT `model` + WITH an unknown extra field + `stream:false`. `MODEL_SELECTION` — one logical check, two requests: the first ADVERTISED id must select (strict 200 — the app sends the user's picked id in production), and a made-up id must answer **400 + `code:"model_not_found"`** when 2+ models are advertised (404 refused — the contract pins 404 to unknown PATHS; a single-model adapter MAY ignore the field). `HISTORY_IMAGE` — the anti-poisoning probe in the REAL failure shape (photo turn with no assistant reply → two consecutive user messages, then text-only newest): MUST answer — forward the earlier image or swap in the contract's verbatim disclosure; any rejection = FAIL (one bad photo must never kill later turns). `STREAM_SYNC` — `stream:true` still answers ONE synchronous JSON object (never rejected, never SSE). Every chat reply graded to the CONTRACT's response rules (stricter than the app's lenient decoder, deliberately): strict JSON (no NaN/Infinity), `Content-Type: application/json`, exactly ONE choice, non-empty STRING `content`, no truthy `tool_calls`, SSE framing its own diagnosis. `--deep` adds `IMAGE_INPUT`, the **semantic image probe** (replaces the old tolerance-only parts test — deep now checks honesty): a stdlib-rendered PNG of 4 random digits (5×7 glyphs scaled, 472×192 black-on-white; digits NEVER in prompt/filename/metadata; fresh per run) rides the newest message → reply carrying the digits as a standalone digit-run = **VERIFIED**; 400 + OpenAI error + `code:"image_unsupported"` = **DECLINED** (both pass); 200 without the digits = **UNVERIFIED** (FAIL — the forbidden silent drop); wrong/missing decline code = FAIL (clients key on machine codes). **Machine summary** — the final line of every failure and every non-interactive/CI exit; on an interactive PASS it prints before the optional setup handoff. Early failures and Ctrl-C/TERM still emit it through an EXIT dispatcher chained onto the setup cleanup (`on_exit`), with HUP/INT/TERM routed through `exit` because bash 3.2 skips EXIT traps on unhandled signals: `CONDUCK_CHECK_ADAPTER schema=3 contract=v1 revision=1.3 harness=<ver> profile=<basic\|deep> core=<PASS\|FAIL\|NOT_RUN> history_image=… stream=… image_input=<VERIFIED\|DECLINED\|UNVERIFIED\|FAIL\|NOT_RUN> file_transport=… file_access=… file_e2e=… checks=<n> failed=<n> exit=<n>` — fixed field order, ASCII enums, no ANSI; unrun = NOT_RUN; grammar changes bump `schema=`; consumers key on this line + exit code, NEVER check counts. `IMAGE_INPUT` failure exits 1 but never flips `core=FAIL` (it grades an optional capability's honesty, not the core wire contract). **`--check-adapter --files` — the ONE mutating adapter-check profile** (writes + removes small `conduck-check-*` files in the configured shared folder): three independent file meters, shared enum `NOT_REQUESTED\|NOT_RUN\|PASS\|FAIL\|ERROR` — `file_transport` (host WebDAV↔disk lane), `file_access` (the selected agent copies a sentinel and names it in its reply, detector-faithfully), `file_e2e` (the app-shaped immediate ranged probe + byte-faithful download). File-lane failures never flip `core=` but count in `failed=` and force exit 1. Lane config: env overrides (`CONDUCK_FILES_URL` + `CONDUCK_FILES_DIR` + `CONDUCK_FILES_PASS`, all-or-nothing; optional `CONDUCK_FILES_USER`, default `conduck`) or the saved pairing profile matched by exact normalized gateway URL. **Egress isolation:** adapter-check requests run `curl -q --noproxy '*'` (ignore `~/.curlrc`, refuse every proxy) so no `$http_proxy`/config line can carry the token off-host or write a file. Target URL positional (either order) or prompted; plain `http://` accepted toward loopback ONLY, host validated against userinfo/wildcard-DNS confusion (`http://127.0.0.1@evil` etc. refused); token via `$CONDUCK_TOKEN` or hidden prompt, never argv; reply content never printed (length only). Transport-level `/v1/models` failure aborts the remaining checks (one concrete sub-cause, not four echoes). Exit 0 = all green / 1 = any fail — loopable from a build script. After PASS in an interactive terminal, it asks whether to continue with setup and reuses the checked URL/token/model; non-interactive/CI runs print the summary and exit without prompting. **F1 regression suite** (the `conduck-connect` repo's own `tests/`): parameterized stdlib fixture adapter (deterministically decodes the digit PNG via the generator's fixed geometry) + `run-checks-suite.sh` — per broken mode asserts exit code, the EXACT failed-`[CHECK_ID]` set, the frozen summary grammar + field values, `failed=` vs ✗-count consistency, and the full green inventory on pass modes (`TERM=dumb` keeps output ANSI-free for grading). Deliberately OUT (need an in-process harness, not HTTP probes): 285s cancellation kill, concurrency/queue behavior, session/permission internals; a raw-NUL body (bash strips it pre-parse) is a documented near-nil-likelihood gap. **Discoverability surfaces (all custom-scoped, never OpenClaw/Hermes/OpenRouter):** contract page "Self-test (before pairing)" leads with the `--check-adapter` two-liner (curl trio demoted to manual fallback); `emit_payload` prints a `--check-adapter` hint on verify-failure AND in the success epilogue, gated `GW_KIND=custom`, interpolating the real `GW_LOCAL_PORT` (falls back to `GW_URL` when the user pasted a full URL); the app's custom-lane 58/62 callout. |
| Server check (`--check-server [url]`) | Black-box **existing-server compatibility check** for OpenAI-compatible software that was NOT built for Conduck (Ollama, LiteLLM, vLLM, LM Studio, and similar). It is intentionally separate from the adapter check: contract-only requirements such as mandatory bearer auth, synchronous handling of `stream:true`, and strict adapter error vocabulary must not make otherwise usable generic software fail. The check itself performs no setup, exposure, QR, profile, or configuration mutation; it does send several live model/chat/image requests. The scoring checks cover the app's `/v1/models` envelope + 15 s limit, the Apple-authoritative eager reply decoder, advertised-model selection, and earlier-image history tolerance; the current-image probe is capability information and never changes the wire verdict. Explicit keyless mode and a required `model` are valid compatibility outcomes. **The check never follows redirects:** the supplied URL must directly address the final gateway base URL, because accepting an auth/login redirect could grade the wrong endpoint and hide a deployment error. For a failure or non-interactive/CI run, the final line is the stable `CONDUCK_CHECK_SERVER schema=2 …` summary; on an interactive PASS it prints before the optional setup handoff. Exit 0 means the Apple app can use the server at the tested wire level, not that the server satisfies the Conduck adapter contract. After PASS in an interactive terminal, it asks whether to continue with setup and reuses the checked URL/token/model; non-interactive/CI runs print the summary and exit without prompting. **Routing:** existing software → `--check-server`; adapter built for Conduck → `--check-adapter`; an app record with unknown provenance presents both choices. |

**Self-signed key-algorithm gate:** pins only what `RemoteAgentTrustEvaluator` can pin — RSA-2048/3072/4096 + EC P-256/P-384; other key types (Ed25519/P-521) rejected with a clear message rather than emitting an unusable QR.

**Consent + safety posture (locked):** never installs gateways or daemons, never phones home, sends nothing except to the user's own gateway, and **never publishes a keyless gateway** (keyless on a public transport refused unless `--setup --allow-keyless-public`). `--setup --dry-run` = baseline-and-plan (shows state + would-be actions, no secrets/minting/requests/QR); `--setup --reuse-only` reuses config and refuses configuration changes; `--show-code` re-shows a saved profile's code with no setup questions and no configuration changes, but it still sends live verification requests and may PUT/GET/DELETE a small file-lane probe — it may also ask for a profile pick, a custom gateway's token, or a gateway-only confirm (the fast re-show path for a second device; conflicts with `--setup --dry-run`). Re-runnable (every step detects/reuses existing state).

**Home:** published as a standalone public repo (`github.com/gigaduckai/conduck-connect`) for auditability — pinned `v*` release tags + a release-asset `.sha256` + CI. The in-app command targets the moving `releases/latest/download/…` alias so the app never names a version. **Every surface (onboarding, website, editor) shows the same short `-O` download-to-disk-then-`bash` form, never `curl | bash`.** Trust anchor = HTTPS + a short, auditable script the user is told to skim — NOT the `.sha256`: it rides the same release channel, so it catches a corrupted download (which HTTPS already prevents), not a swapped release. The `.sha256` ships for release hygiene + an optional manual/advanced verify, never as the in-app trust step.

### Pairing payload v1 (LOCKED wire contract)

String form: `conduck-setup:v1:<base64(minified JSON)>` — identical in the QR and the paste string. TWO emitters (the shell wizard's python emission + the app's `PairingPayloadExport`) and the parser (`PairingPayload.parse`) must stay byte-compatible — golden-vector tests lock the emitters to identical bytes for identical values.

```json
{ "v": 1,
  "gateway": { "kind": "openclaw|hermes|custom", "name": "<custom only>",
               "url": "https://…", "auth": "bearer|none", "token": "<omit when none>",
               "certFP": "<SPKI SHA-256 hex; omit unless self-signed>",
               "model": "<omit unless required (Ollama/vLLM)>" },
  "fileServer": { "url": "https://…", "credential": "<32 hex>",
                  "certFP": "<64-hex SPKI SHA-256; optional — emitted when the file host's cert is self-signed>" },
  "transport": "tailscale|funnel|cloudflare|public|selfsigned" }
```

Rules: `v` gates parsing (unknown major → "update the app / the script"); builtin `kind` accepts only pairing-supported backends (`RemoteAgentBackendMetadata.pairingSupported` — `openrouter` → `.malformed`, the hosted lane is not QR-configurable); unknown keys/transports tolerated (tolerant decode, mirrors KVS discipline); `fileServer` absent when the lane isn't set up; **`transport` is the user's explicit path choice — supersedes URL-pattern inference** wherever both exist. Threat model: the secret displays on the user's own terminal in their own room (WireGuard/WhatsApp-Web precedent); the renderer warns it's scannable by anyone.

### App-side import

`PairingPayload.parse` (`Services/RemoteAgent/PairingPayload.swift`, pure Foundation, tolerant dict-decode) → typed errors `.notAPairingCode / .unsupportedVersion / .malformed / .insecureURL`; certFP normalized lowercase `^[0-9a-f]{64}$`; under `.none` a stray token is DROPPED so keyless stays an explicit token-free state (fail-closed; never infer keyless from a missing token). Lifecycle in `SettingsViewModel+PairingImport.swift`: `planPairingImport(payload, lockedTarget:)` → `.ready / .needsOverwriteConfirm / .blocked(.customGatewayCapReached | .kindMismatch)`, then `executePairingImport` (persist-only, ZERO network — all persistence funnels through the editor's single commit point `saveRemoteAgent`, so import never drifts from manual-save semantics), then `runPairingGatewayTest`.

**Two-staged success signal — `PairingImportSheet` exposes TWO callbacks:** `onImported(RemoteAgentRef)` fires once Stage 1 (save) passes on EVERY successful save (not only locked-target), so a free-target host (the guided flow's "I already have a code" path) learns which gateway landed; the distinct `onConnected(RemoteAgentRef)` fires ONLY on a passing gateway test (the guided flow advances to success on it alone) — "save success" ≠ "connected". Both fire EAGERLY at stage-pass time behind once-latches (`handleDisappear` is only a fallback), so host behavior never depends on sheet-dismissal ordering; the sheet blocks interactive dismissal while stages run.

**Status honesty:** `runPairingGatewayTest`, on a pass, sets `remoteAgentValidationStates[target]=.valid` AND inserts into `remoteAgentLiveValidated`, so the editor renders "Connected" (green) on the pairing happy path rather than the neutral "Saved".

| Aspect | Behavior |
|---|---|
| UI | ONE shared `PairingImportSheet` (input → overwrite alert → staged checklist: Save → Gateway connection w/ TOFU trust-and-retry → File server). iOS adds a VisionKit QR viewport (`isSupported && isAvailable` else paste-only; a REJECTED scan re-arms via `.id(generation)` — the one-shot latch would otherwise freeze it). macOS = paste-only. It is reached through the Connect section's Guided Setup flow or a per-ref editor's Quick connect row; the latter deep-links the same guided flow to that ref, and `lockedTarget` pins the destination so a kind mismatch is blocked. |
| Import mapping | `kind: openclaw/hermes` → that built-in `RemoteAgentRef`; `custom` → mint a roster draft (cap-checked; discarded on cancel AND on a failed execute). **Existing URL on the target → explicit overwrite confirm** (never silent); a payload WITHOUT `certFP` clears a stale pin on overwrite (test-locked); an ABSENT `fileServer` block on overwrite KEEPS the target's existing file-transfer config (chat and file lanes are separate doors — a gateway-only re-pair must not destroy a working lane; the overwrite alert then shows **"File transfer: keeps your current setup."**). |
| Partial commit (`PairingImportOutcome`) | `saveRemoteAgent` commits the gateway half atomically; the file-server credential is a SEPARATE Keychain write — on its failure the file-server URL rolls back to `.committedGatewayOnly` (stage 1 green, file stage failed with its own message), never "nothing saved". |
| Default rule | First-gateway bootstrap lives in `saveRemoteAgent`, so importing the FIRST gateway makes it default exactly like a manual save (avoids the `remoteAgentNotConfigured` dead-end). A later import never touches the default pointer — `setDefaultRemoteAgentRef` clears the active conversation, so it is never called unconditionally. Import never raises the manual editor's make-default prompt (a staged sheet is the wrong host for a mid-flow alert). |
| certFP | Gateway pin rides the save (App-Group, per-device — self-signed path only; other devices re-pin). FileServer pin: explicit `fileServer.certFP` wins, else copied from the gateway pin when `transport == selfsigned` && same host. **`fileTransferAvailable` is NEVER set by import** (only a passing staged test earns it) — an already-earned true is REVOKED before the first file-server write (fail-closed: the import replaces the lane's identity, so neither this device, a concurrent snapshot, nor a synced peer may pair the new server with a Ready the old one earned); the tuple commit forfeits local test proof + silent-probe markers with it, and the credential write bumps the per-ref generation, retiring any staged verdict the old password earned. |
| `transport` hint | Stored per-ref at `remoteAgent.transportHint.<ref>` — **App-Group ONLY, never KVS/broadcast**; cleared with the per-ref wipe. `tailscale` → Tailscale App Store callout shown BEFORE the tests run. |
| Platforms | iOS = camera scan + paste fallback; macOS = paste only (one iPhone scan propagates to Mac/iPad via the existing KVS + synchronizable-Keychain sync). |

### App-side export (re-show a setup code)

`PairingPayloadExport` (`Services/RemoteAgent/PairingPayloadExport.swift`, pure Foundation) is the INVERSE of `PairingPayload.parse`: it re-emits a configured ref's `conduck-setup:v1` code from stored config so a NEW device (either ecosystem) scans it without re-running `conduck-connect`. `serialize(_:)` reproduces the wizard's python emission BYTE-FOR-BYTE — a hand-rolled `pythonJSONString` matching `json.dumps(ensure_ascii=True)` (escape `"` `\` + control chars, `\uXXXX` every UTF-16 unit outside 0x20–0x7E incl. surrogate pairs, never escape `/`) with the wizard's fixed key order; `JSONEncoder` can't (no stable order, escapes `/`, no `\uXXXX`). Fail-closed: a `.bearer` gateway whose token read fails throws `.tokenUnavailable` (never a keyless code — the inverse of the parser's keyless-never-inferred posture); OpenRouter/non-pairable → `.notExportable` (mirrors the parser's `openrouter` reject; no UI entry point either). The `fileServer` block serializes only when BOTH url + credential are nonempty (the wizard's python truthiness, enforced for every representable input, not just the builder's outputs). Transport: stored per-ref hint, else `selfsigned` when the gateway is pinned (so a same-host file server inherits the pin on import), else `public`. Golden-vector + export→parse round-trip tests lock byte-compat.

**Reveal ceremony (`PairingExportSheet`, shared iOS+macOS, from the per-ref editor's "Show Gateway Setup Code" row — configured pairing-supported gateways only):** `LAPolicy.deviceOwnerAuthentication` step-up (fresh `LAContext` per reveal; the confirm-and-proceed no-lock alert is offered ONLY for `LAError.passcodeNotSet` — any other auth-unavailability locks shut as `.authFailed`) → non-mutating live preflight via the Test-Connection machinery (failure = soft "may not work" warning, never blocks — the device may simply be offline) → QR (`CIFilter` code generator, level M, integer-scaled) + a separately-gated paste-string reveal with copy. Auto-blanks after ~60 s, on resign-active/background, and on `UIScreen.isCaptured` (iOS); the in-flight reveal task is cancelled on `.background` so a code can never mount while backgrounded (the biometric overlay's own `.inactive` flip does NOT tear down auth — only true backgrounding does); QR + string are `.privacySensitive()`; the code is never logged or written to disk by Conduck. Copy uses `Pasteboard.copySensitive` — a ~3-minute clipboard lifetime (iOS `UIPasteboard` `expirationDate`; macOS best-effort timed clear while the app runs — AppKit has no expiry API), Universal Clipboard deliberately allowed (pasting on the user's own Mac is a designed transfer path). Copy discipline: it is a **setup code / reusable password** (a photo keeps working until the gateway token rotates — revocation guidance names BOTH the gateway token and the file-server credential); never "single-use" / "expiring" / "link device".

**Guided setup (Settings → Personal AI only — onboarding defers gateway setup):** `GuidedGatewaySetupView` runs the lane-aware flow — chooser → lane fork (`GatewaySetupForkView`) → readiness → helper-trust → commands → success; the hosted-model (OpenRouter) lane pairs directly. `PairingImportSheet` is the shared import surface across lanes. Full behavior in **Onboarding**.

**In-app helper for NON-script (manual-entry) users** (kept because manual entry survives): the manual URL field shows, for a host ending in `.ts.net`, the caption "A Tailscale address — this device needs the Tailscale app, signed in to the same tailnet." Detection is local; no telemetry.

Pairing preserves the locked posture (no backend · no analytics/telemetry · BYO-keys/free · single backend-agnostic `RemoteAgentClient` · `https://`-only) — see **Architectural Invariants**; pairing only moves the same `{url, token, scheme, fingerprint}` the user would have typed.

---

## Share Extension (System Share Sheet — Entry 3)

**Two `com.apple.share-services` appexes, additive under the frozen `ai.gigaduck.AgentRelay` namespace:** `ConduckShareExtension` (iOS/iPadOS, `…ConduckShareExtension`) and `ConduckShareExtensionMac` (native macOS, `…ConduckShareExtensionMac`). Ingest images / text / URLs / arbitrary files from any app, **plus the rendered text of a shared Safari page** (see Safari page-text capture). Each shows a **"Send to" picker** (target a specific gateway / existing chat) and ALWAYS sends on the chosen target. **No Watch/CarPlay** (no third-party system-share receive). Both appexes are thin capture-and-queue producers feeding the SAME App-Group inbox + the shared `appendMessage` pipeline — they add no new wire/render/error code.

**Thin capture-and-queue — the appex does ZERO processing.** iOS-26 appex memory cap ≈120 MB (a 48 MP HEIC decodes >100 MB), so the appex only COPIES each `NSItemProvider` representation into the App-Group inbox + writes one `manifest.json`, then exits; every real step (`ImageProcessor` / `TextFileExtractor` / file-server upload / `appendMessage` / dispatch) runs in the MAIN app at drain time. The copy happens INSIDE the `loadFileRepresentation` handler — Apple deletes the provider temp the instant it returns. Principals: UIKit `ShareViewController` hosting `UIHostingController` (iOS — Apple ships no SwiftUI principal); AppKit `NSViewController` hosting `NSHostingController` (macOS). The shared cross-process contract is `ShareTarget` → `SharedInboxManifest` routing, NOT the view — the two appexes carry SEPARATE `ShareView` layouts. Picker data source = an App-Group JSON snapshot (`ShareTargetsSnapshot`), NEVER Core Data (the appex can't open the store under its cap). Both share the pure-Foundation `ShareTargetFilter` (verbatim-mirrored, drift-guarded by tests). **Missing/empty snapshot ⇒ a single non-dead-end "New conversation" → default gateway** — the share never dead-ends.

**macOS Send/Cancel are IN-LAYOUT, not a SwiftUI `.toolbar`** — Why: the macOS share-panel host renders no `NavigationStack` toolbar, so a toolbar Send silently vanishes (the iOS sheet renders its toolbar fine).

**Activation rule diverges by platform because reliability inverts:**
- **iOS = `NSPredicate` form** (NOT dictionary — the dictionary form activates for non-image UTIs merely conforming to `public.image` with unreliable exclusions): accepts `public.image | public.text | public.url | public.data`; item-count ≤1; per-item attachments ≤10; whole-item acceptance (every attachment acceptable, no partial item). **Live Photos ACCEPTED** — copy the still, ignore the motion track; no `com.apple.live-photo-bundle` exclusion — Why: a Live Photo's still ALSO advertises that UTI, so excluding it suppresses Conduck for the iPhone's DEFAULT photo type. The predicate is UNCHANGED by Safari page-text capture — the `com.apple.property-list` JS-results carrier conforms to `public.data`, so it already matches (see Safari page-text capture).
- **macOS = predefined DICTIONARY form** (`…SupportsImage/File/Text/WebURLWithMaxCount` ≤10) — Why: on macOS Finder does not reliably populate `registeredTypeIdentifiers`, so a `UTI-CONFORMS-TO` predicate silently evaluates false and the extension NEVER appears in the Share menu (Apple-confirmed Sharing Services bug, openradar 30184633). `TRUEPREDICATE` avoided (App Store review flags it). macOS Safari runs the capture JS off this existing dictionary rule (config A = JS key + the standard `Supports…WithMaxCount` dict, no WebPage entry); a `NSExtensionActivationSupportsWebPageWithMaxCount = 1` fallback (config B) is documented in the plist comment should macOS Safari click-through need it.

Broad activation is intentional + safe: Conduck is a thin forwarder; the drainer's catch-all copies any unrecognized attachment as a generic file and uploads binaries to the gateway's file-server, so Conduck forwards anything and the gateway decides — the only graceful stop is a binary to a gateway with no file-server (quarantine, never lost). **`file://`:** rejected on **iOS** (not durable post-exit; shared files arrive as item-provider representations, not `file://`); on **macOS** accepted — Why: Finder/Preview/Quick Look share documents AS security-scoped `file://` URLs (not representations), so the macOS appex copies their bytes inside a balanced `start`/`stopAccessingSecurityScopedResource()` pair (on every path incl. throw) into the same `tmp/att-<seq>.<ext>` + the same `SharedInboxManifest.Item` shape. Web `http(s)` URLs route to `urls[]`.

**App-Group inbox** = `Application Support/Inbox/` (App Group `group.ai.gigaduck.agentrelay`), `.completeFileProtection`. NOT `Library/Caches` — Why: iOS purges caches and a queued share must survive pending user work.

```
Inbox/tmp/<uuid>/         ← appex writes attachments + manifest.json (in-progress)
Inbox/<uuid>/             ← ONE same-volume atomic rename publishes the envelope
Inbox/processing/<uuid>/  ← drainer CLAIMS (atomic move) before doing work
```

**Single-writer + atomic-rename, NO `NSFileCoordinator`** — the appex writes ONLY under `tmp/<uuid>/` then one rename; only the drainer moves published dirs. Drainer enumeration SKIPS `tmp/` + `processing/` — Why: defeats extension-termination races (Apple kills appexes mid-write). Inbox originals are retained as the replay source until converse succeeds or a terminal-failure delete; uploads use throwaway copies. **No `failed/` graveyard** — a terminal failure surfaces in the conversation + a notification, then deletes the envelope.

### Safari page-text capture
The share sheet (NOT a Safari Web Extension) captures the **rendered text** of a shared Safari page — or the user's selection — alongside its URL. Both appexes declare `NSExtensionJavaScriptPreprocessingFile = ConduckWebCapture`; Safari runs `ConduckWebCapture.js` INSIDE the page at share time and delivers the result as a `com.apple.property-list` `NSItemProvider` under `NSExtensionJavaScriptPreprocessingResultsKey`.

**iOS vending fact (spike-verified, iOS 26):** with the JS key declared Safari vends ONLY the property-list carrier — the usual `public.url` provider DISAPPEARS. Two consequences: (a) the share URL must be recovered from the JS payload (there is no `public.url` to read), and (b) the loader's property-list branch is what makes a Safari share usable at all (without it the plist bytes would ride as a generic `public.data` file). The iOS activation predicate is unchanged (`com.apple.property-list` conforms to `public.data`). macOS runs the JS off its existing dictionary rule (config A; config B fallback in Activation rule above).

**macOS SELECTION vending fact (founder-verified, macOS 26):** macOS Safari does NOT run the preprocessing JS for a text-selection share (unlike iOS, where the in-page `getSelection()` covers it). The selection travels ONLY in `NSExtensionItem.attributedContentText`; the attachments are EMPTY (private-browsing window) or a single `public.url` (normal window) — and the shape is NON-DETERMINISTIC: Safari sometimes vends URL-only with NO side-channel text at all (the selection never leaves Safari; nothing client-side can recover it → the share degrades to a plain link share). `ShareViewController.selectionFallbackCapture` (mac appex ONLY) synthesizes a selection-scope capture from the side-channel text through `WebPageCapture.parse` — the same untrusted-input entry point, so clamping/rejection stay uniform — with `CaptureLoad.providerID = nil` (no carrier provider to skip; nil-only-here is the one deliberate iOS/macOS `CaptureLoad` divergence). Hijack guards: the attachments must be web links only (zero, or all `public.url` that are NOT `public.file-url`) so file/image/text shares ride the provider ladder untouched, and the side-channel text must not be a bare URL echo (link-only shares from other browsers put the URL string there). A `public.url` attachment still rides the normal envelope loop, so the agent gets link + selection; the synthesized payload itself carries no URL (markdown Source line omitted).

**Payload contract (JS → Swift).** JS returns `{title, url, selection, pageText, originalByteCount, truncated, scope}` (`scope ∈ {"selection","page"}`). **Selection beats page** — a non-whitespace selection is captured INSTEAD of the whole page (stronger intent signal; avoids shipping a page the user wanted one passage of). `getSelection()` can't see `<input>`/`<textarea>` selections → falls back to page scope. The JS caps text at `MAX_BYTES` with UTF-8-safe (no split code-point) truncation; a hostile/broken page degrades to an empty payload (per-field try/catch + swallow-all outer catch → Swift parse `nil` → the share proceeds as a plain URL share).

**`WebPageCapture`** (pure-Foundation enum; canonical `ConduckShareExtensionMac/WebPageCapture.swift`) — the parse/format/truncate contract between the JS probe and the drainer. `parse` treats the JS dict as **UNTRUSTED input** (a page script can replace the bridge payload): every field default-fills on a type miss, a self-inconsistent "selection" scope with empty selection falls back to page, over-cap text is RE-CLAMPED `Character`-safely to `maxCaptureBytes`, and it returns `nil` for no-usable-text (graceful absence). `Payload` = `title/url/text/originalByteCount/truncated/scope` with computed `capturedText/isSelection/returnedByteCount` (byte counts RECOMPUTED, never trusted from JS; `originalByteCount` floors at the actual pre-clamp size so a lying JS can't understate the truncation note).

**Three paired 128 KiB literals** (the JS can't read Swift; the appexes can't import `Constants`): JS `MAX_BYTES = 131072`, `WebPageCapture.maxCaptureBytes`, `Constants.webPageCaptureMaxBytes` — pinned equal by `ConduckTests/WebPageCaptureTests`, which ALSO enforces the appex-mirror byte-identity + the JS-pair byte-identity. **Mirror discipline:** `WebPageCapture.swift` + `ConduckWebCapture.js` each exist once per appex dir and stay byte-identical (JS = full-file; Swift = below the header); the canonical `WebPageCapture.swift` compiles into the macOS appex AND the main app (explicit pbxproj file ref — the `ShareTargetFilter` precedent) so the drainer + tests reach it, while the iOS appex carries a verbatim mirror.

**Toggle UX.** When the parse produced a payload the picker shows a **"Page text" / "Selected text" toggle (default ON)** naming the scope + captured size (+ truncation note). No payload → no toggle (byte-identical to a plain URL share).

**Envelope write (`ShareViewController.writeEnvelope`).** The appex memoizes a `capturePayloadTask` yielding `CaptureLoad {providerID, url, payload?}` — carrier identity + URL bound whenever the results key exists, `payload` only on parse success. Providers split into `rawProviders` (envelope source) vs `visibleProviders` (preview/count/header — plist-conforming providers excluded EAGERLY, no load, so no "property list" flashes in the preview); an empty `visibleProviders` → `resolveLeadHeader` synthesizes a header from the capture (title, else host + "Web page"). On write: skip the carrier provider (its bytes are the bridge payload, not an attachment); **the page URL joins `urls[]` TOGGLE-INDEPENDENTLY** via `WebPageCapture.shouldAppend(url:toExisting:)` — Why: toggle-OFF (or a failed parse) must not lose the share's only URL, since Safari vended no `public.url`. That gate = http(s)-only + normalized-equivalence dedupe (scheme/host case-folded, fragment + trailing slash stripped; query/port PRESERVED so hash-routed SPA URLs stay distinct — the appex de-dupe and the drainer's URL splice are exact-string). When the toggle is ON the appex writes a synthetic `att-<seq>.md` (`WebPageCapture.markdown` — metadata header of title/source/scope + optional truncation note, `---`, then the captured text VERBATIM; filename `WebPageCapture.suggestedFilename` = `"Captured Page — <title>.md"`) with manifest `mimeType "text/markdown"`, `utTypeIdentifier "net.daringfireball.markdown"`, and provenance marker `sourceKind = "webpage"`.

**Drainer transport.** `SharedInboxManifest.Item.sourceKind == WebPageCapture.sourceKindWebpage` is the IDENTITY (NOT the `.md` filename — a user-shared `*.md` carries `sourceKind == nil`). Two webpage-only effects: (1) the display filename shadows `extracted` ONCE with `item.originalName` so chips/fences/upload-keys/replay all show `"Captured Page — <title>.md"` (persisted on the `AttachmentDraft` so prior-turn replay reads it every later turn); (2) on a server-LESS gateway the drainer passes `clampInlineWhenServerless: true` → `AttachmentDeliveryPlanner.plan` carries `inlineByteLimit = Constants.textInlineMaxBytes` (32 KB) and the inline copy is cut via `WebPageCapture.truncatedForInline` (honest note counted INSIDE the cap → result ≤ limit for ANY limit, hardened for limit < note size). **Why the no-server clamp:** the client-owned-history protocol RE-SENDS the full conversation every turn, so an unbounded 128 KiB page would tax every subsequent turn; regular (non-webpage) text keeps `inlineByteLimit == nil` = unlimited inline (no regression, test-pinned). With a file-server configured a webpage capture routes exactly like any text attachment (dual / `serverFile`); fence hygiene (`ConverseRequest.safeFence`, untrusted-content label) is unchanged.

**Known capture limits (accepted V1):** lazy-load / infinite-scroll content not yet in the DOM is missed; virtualized lists + collapsed messages are absent; canvas apps (Google Docs/Sheets) extract ~nothing; cross-origin iframes + closed shadow DOM are inaccessible; web-app DOMs are noisy (no Readability extraction in V1). Capture code logs NOTHING content-derived (titles / URLs / selections / text) — Privacy §5.

### `SharedInboxManifest`
`Models/SharedInboxManifest.swift`, dependency-free Foundation. Fields: `v` · `uuid` · `createdAt` · `caption` · `conversationID?` (existing-chat pick) · `newConversationGatewayRef?` (New-conversation pick → mint on this ref) · `selectedBackendRef?` (bound-gateway hint for an existing pick, used at mint-fallback) · `items[]` (`relPath`, `originalName?`, `mimeType?`, `utTypeIdentifier?`, `sequence`, `sourceKind?` — `WebPageCapture.sourceKindWebpage` (`"webpage"`) marks an appex-SYNTHESIZED Safari capture, drives the drainer's webpage-only display-name + no-server clamp; `nil` for every user-shared file, OMITTED from the wire when nil) · `urls[]` · `shouldAutosend` (wire-contract field, always `true`).
- **`encoded()`/`decode()` pin `.iso8601` + `.sortedKeys`** — both binaries (appex writer, drainer reader) MUST route through these — Why: an ad-hoc coder silently fails to carry `createdAt`.
- **Tolerant decode**: only `uuid` is required; every other field default-fills — Why: a newer-appex envelope still decodes across a staged rollout instead of poisoning the inbox.
- **Each appex compiles a verbatim self-contained MIRROR** (no cross-target membership) — Why: separate compile modules keep the 120 MB-capped appexes self-contained. The three copies (canonical + both appexes) are byte-identical below their headers, drift-guarded by a 3-way frozen-wire test.

### `ShareTargetsSnapshot`
`Models/ShareTargetsSnapshot.swift`, verbatim-mirrored into BOTH appexes: `{schemaVersion, generatedAt, gateways:[{ref, displayName, colorHex, monogram, configured}], recentConversations:[{id, label, backendRef, lastActivityAt}]}`. `ShareTargetsSnapshotWriter` (`actor`) regenerates on `.conversationsDidChange` + `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` + at launch, atomic temp→rename to `<AppGroup>/Application Support/share-targets.json` (the appex never sees a partial file). Gated `#if os(iOS) || os(macOS)` — watchOS lacks the `CarPlayConversationLabel` dep. `recentConversations` is filtered to backends still in `configuredRemoteAgentRefs()` so the picker never offers a dead-gateway target.

### `SharedInboxDrainer` (`Services/SharedInboxDrainer.swift`, `actor`, `static let shared`)
Drains on app-active (iOS foreground, macOS `applicationDidBecomeActive`, notification tap). **macOS cross-process WAKE (load-bearing):** the menu-bar app is a persistent accessory; a share made while it's INACTIVE never foregrounds Conduck (focus returns to the source app). `ShareInboxWatcher` (macOS-only, owned by `AppDelegate` for app lifetime) arms a `DispatchSource` vnode source (`O_EVTONLY`, mask `[.write,.delete,.rename,.revoke]`) on `SharedInboxDrainer.productionInboxBase` (the SAME dir the actor drains, single-sourced); the appex's atomic publish modifies the inbox vnode → `drain(trigger: .shareWake)`, plus a cold-start `.launch` drain for envelopes queued while quit. **Why a vnode file-watch, NOT a Darwin notification:** Darwin notifications are stateless/edge-triggered/coalescing and delivered via the MAIN RUN LOOP — an inactive accessory app (no window) is a prime App Nap target with no servicing run loop, so the wake is silently DROPPED; the vnode source fires on a dispatch queue, not gated by app-active state.

`drain()` drains ALL published envelopes; `drainAndResolve(envelopeID:) -> UUID?` drains ONE and returns its resolved/minted `conversationID` so a notification tap lands the user in that chat. Per envelope: **claim** (atomic move → `processing/`) → **resolve-or-mint** → classify + build each attachment → `appendMessage(id:)` → assemble (current + retained prior-turn images) → mark `submitted` → dispatch. **Double-dispatch guard:** the atomic move-claim guards the initial claim; an in-flight set (`inFlightEnvelopes`) guards the foreground-`drain()` vs notification-tap-`drainAndResolve()` reconcile window — upholds "never double-hit the user's gateway."

### Converse dispatch — transport-split by platform
`LiveConverseDispatcher` behind the `ShareConverseDispatching` seam, mirroring the in-app composer's `#if os(macOS)` split:
- **iOS:** `BackgroundRemoteAgent.send` (background `URLSession`, survives appex exit + app suspension); its delegate lands the reply.
- **macOS:** the FOREGROUND in-process `RemoteAgentClient.send`, then the drainer lands the reply itself via the shared `BackgroundRemoteAgent.recordReply` BEFORE `dispatch` returns. **Why:** a background `URLSession` for an INACTIVE menu-bar (accessory) app has its completion delegate DEFERRED by `nsurlsessiond` until re-activation — the reply lands at the network layer but never persists (menu-bar dot never lights). An in-process session is not gated by app-active state.

**At-most-once consequence (macOS):** no daemon-owned converse task exists, so the relaunch reconcile's `hasLiveConverseTask` is always false there — a mid-send app QUIT leaves a `submitted` envelope with no live task → terminal **turn-exists failure**, NEVER auto-resend (which would double-hit the user's gateway). The share reply notification is RETAINED on both platforms — the menu bar is hidden in full-screen / other apps, so the notification is the only surface with reach.

### Per-type transport (mirrors the in-app composer)
| Shared item | Transport | File-server? |
|---|---|---|
| Image | `ImageProcessor` → inline base64 vision **+ eager upload of the ORIGINAL raw bytes when a file-server is configured** (dual, `spliceImageServerRefs`); inline-only otherwise; upload failure → inline-only (the turn still sends) | optional (dual when present) |
| Plain text / web URL | inline user-turn text | no |
| Text/code file | `TextFileExtractor` → inline fenced block | no |
| Safari page-text capture (synthetic `.md`, `sourceKind:"webpage"`) | text route (dual with a file-server, inline without) — but a server-LESS gateway CLAMPS the inline copy to 32 KB + honest note (`WebPageCapture.truncatedForInline`); display name = `"Captured Page — <title>.md"`. See Safari page-text capture | optional (dual when present) |
| Binary (PDF/docx/zip…) | upload → "saved as `<storedKey>`" ref | yes (per gateway) |

A binary routed to a gateway with **NO file-server → nothing sent; a "needs file server" notification fires and the envelope is dropped** (a no-turn failure) — NEVER partial-send the image/text subset, which would change the meaning of the user's share without consent. The agent wire physically can't carry a binary inline — the BYO file-server is the only transport.

### Routing = `SharedInboxRouting.resolveOrMint`
The picker stamps the target into the manifest; the drainer resolves by first-match-wins precedence — preserves the no-silent-reroute invariant (a gone target is a no-turn failure, NOT a silent reroute to default):
1. `conversationID` set & row exists → **append**, route by its stored backend.
2. `conversationID` set but row gone → **mint on `selectedBackendRef`** if still configured, **else no-turn failure** (no fall-through to default).
3. `newConversationGatewayRef` set & configured → **mint a new chat** on that ref.
4. `newConversationGatewayRef` set but gone/unconfigured → **no-turn failure**.
5. neither (legacy/no-target envelope) → the pointer-driven quick-capture path (`resolveQuickCaptureConversation`: TTL + default-gateway re-check), else **default gateway** (back-compat).

**Share drains are READ-ONLY against the quick lane** — a share may passively land in the quick-capture thread (precedence #5) but NEVER stamps/extends/retargets the per-device pointer — Why: sharing is an explicit-target act, not a quick capture. Actors are reentrant across `await`, so a rare double-mint is ACCEPTED (worst case two near-identical conversations — annoying, not data loss); no coordinator actor.

### Exactly-once = at-most-once dispatch + idempotent uploads (two crash windows)
- **Converse**: the manifest `uuid` **is** the user `Message.id` → `appendMessage(id:)` re-append is a no-op (dedupe key, no new Core Data attribute); `submitted` is durably marked BEFORE `task.resume()`; on relaunch, reconcile by `shareEnvelopeID` (`hasLiveConverseTask` over `session.allTasks`) — alive → leave, completed → delete, **submitted-but-no-task → turn-exists failure, never auto-resend** — Why: a silent re-send duplicates the request + side effects on the user's own gateway; true exactly-once needs gateway idempotency keys (unavailable), so at-most-once is the right trade.
- **File-server upload** (chained hop): deterministic per-attachment key `<uuid8>-<seq>__<sanitized>` (`FileServerClient.deterministicStoredKey`) → idempotent re-PUT (WebDAV PUT to the same path overwrites identical bytes, no orphan); replay inspects live transfer tasks (`hasLiveUploadTask`) — alive → defer, none → re-PUT. The drainer hands `uploadFile` a throwaway COPY (it deletes its input) and keeps the inbox original for replay.

### Failure surfacing — the CONVERSATION is the single recovery surface, no settings graveyard
Every terminal share failure is one of two shapes (`failWithTurn` / `failNoTurn`), both ending in envelope DELETE (at-most-once holds — neither re-dispatches):
- **Turn-exists failure** (dispatch threw after the user turn was appended; relaunch `submitted`-no-task): flip THE EXACT turn `failed` via `markPendingUserTurn(messageID:to:)` — NOT the conversation-wide variant, which aliases sibling in-flight turns → inline failed bubble + Retry (re-sends fully from Core Data, no envelope) → `.remoteAgentTurnDidFail` (drives the macOS menu-bar red dot) → `postFailureNotification` (deep-links into the thread; identifier prefix `remoteAgent.failure.` EXCLUDES it from reply auto-speak) → delete.
- **No-turn failure** (no user turn yet: `needsFileServer`, corrupt/unroutable envelope, gone target, pre-append upload failure): a "couldn't send" notification (no deep-link, `conversationIDKey: ""`) → delete. The shared bytes are dropped (accepted trade; the notification names the fix). Nothing is ever sent partially or silently.

The same model unifies in-app / headless / CarPlay failures: a failed turn lives in its thread with inline Retry; `ConverseIntent` pre-dispatch failures also post `.remoteAgentTurnDidFail` + the failure notification.

### Always-send + queue-and-drain handoff
Sharing ALWAYS sends on the picked target — there is NO "auto-send" toggle. Why: an OFF position needs a composer-draft staging path that doesn't exist, and "always send" matches the explicit-target picker. `manifest.shouldAutosend` stays in the wire contract (always `true`).

**HARD PLATFORM CONSTRAINT — a Share Extension cannot open its host app on iOS.** Apple blesses `NSExtensionContext.open` for Today widgets ONLY; the responder-chain `openURL` hack is unsupported / App-Store-risky and is NOT used. So there is no instant foreground into the chat from the share sheet — the experience is **always-send-on-share + land-in-chat on the confirmation-notification tap or next app open** (WhatsApp's share behavior). This is WHY the send seam stays **queue-and-drain**: the appex queues + writes the manifest + exits; the main app drains on next active. The confirmation `UNUserNotificationCenter` notification carries `shareEnvelopeID`; on tap, `NotificationDelegate` calls `drainAndResolve(envelopeID:)` then posts `.openConversationDeepLink` to open the resolved/minted chat. A plain next-app-open (no tap) drains everything via `drain()` without yanking the user anywhere.

---

## Per-Surface Behavior

### iPhone / iPadOS — `ConversationThreadView` shell

**Root gate.** `RootView` chooses onboarding-vs-app by **conditional rendering + `onComplete:` callback**, NOT `.fullScreenCover` — Why: `.fullScreenCover(isPresented:)` with `@State` seeded `true` in `init()` never fires on iOS 26. Onboarding flag is sync-seeded from the App-Group `UserDefaults` in `init()` to avoid launch-flash. DEBUG-only overrides (`QA/DebugFlags.swift`, read-only, no defaults write): `-ConduckShowOnboarding` forces the wizard every launch; `-ConduckSkipOnboarding` skips it without QA-mode's other side effects. **Precedence:** explicit skip intents (`QAMode` / `-ConduckSkipOnboarding`) WIN over `-ConduckShowOnboarding` — Why: an automated QA launch must never be trapped on first-run.

**iPhone thread.** `ContentView` is the conversational thread: `NavigationStack` → `ScrollViewReader` of role-tagged bubbles backed by `ConversationStore` (see Conversation Store), `PendingRetryCard` above thread, bottom type-or-talk composer. Toolbar: leading opens the conversation list as a **`.sheet`** (time-grouped, swipe-delete); trailing **New conversation** clears the visible selection (`currentConversationID = nil` → empty state → next send mints fresh; never touches the per-device quick-capture pointer). Per-message: Copy + Speak (via `ThreadSpeaker` → `ReplyVoice`, see Text-to-Speech) — a **play→pause→resume toggle that resumes from position with no re-synthesis**. **Copy conversation** (whole thread → clipboard as plain text): a single `.primaryAction` toolbar item declared INSIDE the shared `ConversationThreadView` (merges into all three host nav bars — iPhone/iPad/macOS — one impl, zero per-host toolbar edits; hidden on an empty thread); `ConversationCopyFormatter` (pure, unit-tested) emits role-labeled turns (`You:` / bound-backend name) with agent Markdown verbatim and attachments as bracket placeholders (`[Image attached]` / `[File attached: name]`) — NEVER bytes, `extractedText`, or `storedKey`; `failed` turns annotate `[Not sent]`. Watch/CarPlay/menu-bar popover excluded. `sourceDevice` chip on user turns only (agent replies have no originating device). In-flight **answering indicator** is borderless, left-aligned: spinner + "{bound backend} is answering…" + elapsed clock after ~3s; cancellation is the composer's Stop morph (no inline Cancel). **Unconfigured** → empty state pointing to Settings AND the composer position is replaced by a gated `LockedComposerBar` CTA routing to Settings → Personal AI — Why: the agent feature is the home screen, two distinct affordances toward the same setup, not a buried entry.

**iPad sidebar** mirrors the macOS shell: a pinned `.safeAreaInset(.top)` header with the prominent New-conversation button atop the shared `SidebarSearchField` (same component macOS uses); the native `.searchable` + large title are SUPPRESSED — Why: iOS forces `.searchable` ABOVE any pinned header, wasting a title band and putting search above New; the list filters off the host's search binding instead. Delete-All is a bare destructive trash button; a fallback compose item appears in the detail toolbar when the split is `.detailOnly`-collapsed. Settings renders as a toolbar item on iPhone, a footer row on iPad. iPhone composer is the edge-to-edge `.ultraThinMaterial` bar; thread/composer cap = `Constants.Layout.chatContentWidth` (720pt).

**Nav-title backend + gateway picker.** Nav title (and answering-indicator name) name the conversation's bound-backend `displayName`, fallback `"Personal AI"` only when nothing is configured (refreshed on launch / scenePhase / settings-dismiss / `.settingsDidChangeRemotely`). The title becomes a `Menu` gateway picker IFF `configuredRemoteAgentRefs().count >= 2` AND the conversation is new/empty (`currentConversationID == nil`) — selecting the backend for the to-be-minted conversation. Per-conversation binding locks once the thread has ≥1 turn → switch = Clone (see Architectural Invariants); past that the title is a static bound-backend label, tappable (chevron) when clone-eligible to open the lock/clone sheet. **Clone sheet, two states:** State A (still locked) gates clone targets behind a deliberate "Clone to another gateway…" reveal — Why: a curious title tap must not fork the thread by accident; State B (`!boundGatewayAvailable`, gateway gone) shows targets directly as the only recovery. macOS surfaces the same picker via `gatewayToolbarContent` (see macOS section).

**Composer (`iOSMessageComposerBar`, `.safeAreaInset(.bottom)`, `.ultraThinMaterial`).** Shared by iPhone `ContentView` + iPad detail column; adapts to `horizontalSizeClass`. Compact (iPhone) = `[growing TextField] [morphing trailing button]`; the trailing button MUST be a SINGLE `Button` wrapping a SINGLE `Image` with `.contentTransition(.symbolEffect(.replace))` — Why: an if/else over two Buttons gives each its own identity and the swap snaps. Morph **priority**: in-flight Stop (neutral, cancels via `cancelInFlight()`) → recording stop (red) → processing → send (amber, draft present) → mic (amber, idle); recording stays a stop-mic even mid-typing (`showsSendControl` false while capturing/in-flight). Regular (iPad) = `[mic] [field] [send]` + ⌘Return = send / ⌘N = new via zero-size `keyboardShortcut` buttons. Field = `TextField(axis:.vertical).lineLimit(1...6)`, **NOT auto-focused on thread open** (voice-first), + `.scrollDismissesKeyboard(.interactively)` (the sole keyboard-dismissal path). Hardware Return read via `.onKeyPress(keys:[.return])` — ⌘Return → send; plain/Shift Return → explicit `draft += "\n"` — Why: `.onSubmit` is unreliable with `axis:.vertical` and `.ignored` does NOT insert a newline on a vertical TextField; soft-keyboard Return is unaffected (text-input protocol, not `.onKeyPress`). First typed turn is sendable with no conversation (host mints it). Typed + spoken converge on `ConversationDetailViewModel.sendUserTurn(_:modality:)`.

**Attachments.** Leading `paperclip` → device-aware `Menu` (Take Photo / Photo Library / Choose Files…, + a file-server setup entry iff the bound gateway has no snapshot). Take Photo is REMOVED (not disabled) when `UIImagePickerController.isSourceTypeAvailable(.camera)` is false. Files = ONE unified `.fileImporter` accepting any file (routing → Agent File Transfer). Staged thumbs strip sits above the field; with attachments staged and an EMPTY caption the trailing button stays the mic (voice-caption) and a separate subdued Send appears. NO per-message/size/count caps (user pays their own bill); inline vision images are a fixed `1568`px long-edge EXIF/GPS-stripped JPEG at q=0.7, file-transfer uploads raw original bytes. Off-main pipeline: `ImageProcessor` (actor) + `DataURIBuilder` + `TextFileExtractor`. **macOS** also drops via raw `NSItemProvider` `.onDrop` — Why: Safari drags are file-promises that typed `.dropDestination` silently drops. Watch/CarPlay never attach.

**Triggers.** iPhone 15 Pro+ Action Button / older Back Tap / iPad Control Center → bundled `Conduck` Shortcut (entry 1); foreground in-app mic (entry 2). Both feed the same terminal contract (see Trigger Architecture).

**Entry 1 (Shortcut headless): `ConverseIntent`.** Bundled `GigaAction.shortcut` runs `CheckNetworkIntent` first (fast-fail offline) → Record Audio → `ConverseIntent` (single intent — fewer App Intent cache headaches). `perform()`:
1. Fetch URL + token + backend via `SettingsManager.activeSTTSnapshot()` (missing → `remoteAgentNotConfigured`, deep-link to Settings).
2. Resolve-or-mint conversation via `SharedInboxRouting.resolveOrMint` (TTL-gated pointer + default-gateway re-check; a pointer bound to a no-longer-default gateway mints fresh on the default); load trimmed history.
3. Write audio to temp. **Arm `PendingRetryGuard`** — preempt-save audio + 90s deferred local notification in case the OS kills `perform()`; `.completeFileProtection`. Covers the audio→STT hop ONLY (the agent hop relies on background-URLSession re-launch).
4. `STTClient.transcribe` **foreground** (Shortcut holds intent lifetime) → `appendMessage(role:.user)` → pre-dispatch `recordActiveConversation` crash-guard stamp (the intent IS the implicit quick-capture lane).
5. **FIRE-AND-FORGET** POST `text` (+ optional inline screenshot) to `<url>/v1/chat/completions` via `BackgroundRemoteAgent.send(awaitReply:false)` over the **background** `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.converse` session — Why: replies take 30s–minutes and foreground would suspend. `perform()` returns `.result(value: transcript)` once the upload is enqueued so the Shortcut does NOT spin. Delegate cleans the request-body tmp per the audio-cleanup mandate.
6. **Disarm discipline:** retryable STT error → leave armed; non-retryable → disarm. `CompletionFeedbackPlayer.play()` on success.
7. The background-session delegate receives the reply (possibly post suspend+relaunch), **independently of `perform()` having returned** → `appendMessage(role:.agent)` (persisted, synced) → re-stamps the pointer iff `RemoteAgentBackgroundMetadata.stampsActiveConversation` (intent sets `true`; idempotent with step 4; in-app turns ride the same delegate with `false`) → posts the reply notification (~200-char body) on success, OR a failure notification on error. **The failure push fires ONLY when no in-app caller is awaiting** (the headless lane) — Why: the in-app path surfaces errors in-thread with no duplicate push. Tap deep-links to the thread (store holds it; no ephemeral cache).

**Headless launch.** SwiftUI scene modifier `.backgroundTask(.urlSession("ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.converse")) { await BackgroundRemoteAgent.shared.handleBackgroundEvents() }` — there is NO `UIApplicationDelegate` background-session method and no upload-coordinator class.

**Notification "Reply" action.** iOS forbids background mic activation outside a foreground session/CallKit, so Reply foregrounds the app into the thread (entry 2) with the mic focused but NOT auto-recording; user taps to begin. A notification-tap open never stamps the quick-capture pointer (explicit surface).

**Spoken Replies on notification open** (toggle, default OFF — see Text-to-Speech → Spoken Replies). Reply notifications post under `NotificationDeepLink.replyIdentifierPrefix + UUID` (`"remoteAgent.reply.<uuid>"`; failures `"remoteAgent.failure.<uuid>"`) — a shared constant used by both post sites and the speak decider's discriminator. `NotificationDelegate.didReceive` (`#if !os(macOS)`) reads the toggle via the synchronous App-Group static `SettingsManager.speakReplyOnNotificationOpenAtTap()` (`didReceive` is nonisolated — no actor hop) and on a speak verdict stages `AutoSpeakMailbox.shared.request(...)` BEFORE posting `.openConversationDeepLink` — Why: set-then-post kills the consume-before-set race. `ConversationThreadView` (`#if os(iOS)`) drains it via `attemptAutoSpeak()`.

**Entry 2 (in-app composer).** Typed turns skip STT/`InAppAudioRecorder` → straight to `sendUserTurn(_:modality:.text)`. Spoken path: `InAppAudioRecorder` (`@MainActor @Observable`) composing `AudioRecorder` + `AudioCompressor` + `STTClient.transcribe`; on success the transcript **POPULATES the composer draft via `appendingTranscript` (appends at end, never replaces) and does NOT auto-send** — Why: a misheard word stays fixable AND staged attachments ship WITH the spoken caption. Only headless lanes (Shortcut / Watch-headless / CarPlay / macOS popover) auto-send. State: `idle`/`recording`/`processing`/`error`; all send paths disabled while recording/processing; duration-cap auto-stop stages (never sends). **Reactive `PendingRetryStore.save` on retryable errors only — NO pre-record `PendingRetryGuard.arm`** — Why: recording is foreground/user-initiated, pre-arm has no termination-protection benefit (distinct from Entry 1, which DOES arm before the STT hop). "Continue" a past thread = open it and append (view-state only — in-app turns never stamp the quick-capture pointer); the client re-sends that conversation's stored history, so resumption works entirely from the local store.

### macOS — Dock app (unified window + menu bar)

**Unified shell.** ONE main window (`MainWindowView`, `Window("main")`) — a `NavigationSplitView`: sidebar = New-conversation button + shared `ConversationListView` (`.searchable`, time-grouped, swipe-delete; `showsToolbarActions: false`) + a bottom identity-footer Settings button (identity = Keychain UUID, no logged-in user); detail = the `.principal` gateway toolbar item (`gatewayToolbarContent`, picker/static per the Nav-title rule above), the reused thread, and the **always-mounted** `MessageComposerBar`. An empty chat shows a shuffle-bag mascot (`EmptyStateMascot`, shared iPhone/iPad/macOS — see §iPhone/iPadOS). The composer takes an OPTIONAL VM and routes its first typed turn through `MenuBarCoordinator.handleTypedText` (the explicit `windowViewModel` lane — mints on send, bound to the picker's pending/default backend). **Settings = full-window mode swap:** `MacSettingsView` REPLACES the split view via a top-level `if showingSettings` branch (NOT a sheet — gives dense setup screens the whole resizable window); exit is an `onDone` callback, NOT `@Environment(\.dismiss)` (wrong abstraction in a non-sheet root). **Deferred-present seam:** every settings entry point sets `MenuBarCoordinator.pendingShowSettings` + opens `main` first; `onAppear` (window-closed) and the live `.onReceive` (window-open) both read & clear it.

**Window mic ≠ menu-bar mic — two independent recorders.** The main window owns its OWN `@State windowRecorder = InAppAudioRecorder()`; `handleWindowVoiceResult` POPULATES the shared `composerDraft` via `appendingTranscript` (review-then-send, matching iOS Entry 2 — NOT auto-send). `DictationService` is the menu-bar / ⌘⇧1 quick-capture path ONLY (always direct-send) and carries NO transcript-destination latch — the two surfaces can't cross-deliver because they share no service.

**ConduckApp scenes (`NSApplicationDelegateAdaptor(AppDelegate)`).** `Window("main")` `.automatic` + `Window("onboarding")` `.automatic` (NOT `.suppressed` — Why: a suppressed Window is never created at launch, so `openWindow` can't foreground it) + `.restorationBehavior(.disabled)` (Why: a restored stale onboarding window traps the user back in setup); both `.defaultPosition(.center)` with an on-appear re-center if the restored frame isn't on any `NSScreen.visibleFrame` (macOS restoration drifts off-screen from a removed external display). macOS onboarding is posted via `NotificationCenter` from `AppDelegate`, NOT iOS `RootView` conditional rendering (different Scene lifecycle; locked). **Dock presence = activation policy, the ONLY lever** (macOS couples Dock-icon + top app-menu; `NSStatusItem` is policy-independent + always shows). `Show in Dock` → device-local App-Group Bool `Constants.showDockIconKey`, NOT KVS (per-machine window-manager choice). `applicationWillFinishLaunching` forces `.accessory` FIRST (before the scene graph) so `main`'s `.automatic` Window is instantiated-but-NOT-foregrounded (quiet launch; its `.onReceive` subscribers stay live so the duck opens it on demand). Launch fully determines policy: ON → `.regular` in `didFinishLaunching` (Dock icon, no window auto-open, no `activate` — Launch-at-Login shows the icon without stealing focus); OFF → `.accessory`. `AppDelegate.applyDockVisibility` is the live-toggle path. `applicationShouldTerminateAfterLastWindowClosed = false` keeps the menu-bar app alive after the window closes; `applicationShouldHandleReopen` reopens `main` on Dock-icon click.

**`TitlebarBandRepair` (AppKit workaround, activated in `didFinishLaunching`).** macOS 26 mis-lays-out the split view's `NSTitlebarBackgroundView` instances (the titlebar's `NSScrollPocket` glass hosts) when the main window lays out behind another frontmost window — first-run onboarding over a restored main window — leaving a toolbar-height translucent band compositing partway down the transcript that AppKit never self-heals (verified by instrumented capture: `needsLayout`/`layoutSubtreeIfNeeded` recomputes the same stale frames; only an explicit frame correction or a sidebar collapse/expand fixes it). The repair sweeps titled visible windows after launch + after titled-window closes and `setFrameOrigin`s any `NSTitlebarBackgroundView` whose top edge is off the window top (exact-class-name match, |offset| > 4pt, public API only — no-op when healthy). SwiftUI `scrollEdgeEffectHidden` CANNOT fix this (the views are window chrome outside every app subtree — two leaf opt-outs were tried and falsified). Diagnostic: `-ConduckDumpHierarchy` launch arg → `DebugHierarchyDump` (DEBUG, `QA/`) writes full view/layer-tree dumps to the app container tmp.

**`MenuBarController`** (raw `NSStatusItem`, NOT `MenuBarExtra` — too constrained for state-machine icon swap; locked). 6 icon states: idle (static dot) / recording (`record.circle.fill`) / processing-STT (`ellipsis.circle.fill`) / replying (`sparkles`, driven by `coordinator.quickViewModel?.isAwaitingReply` — the QUICK lane, since the icon narrates the hotkey capture flow, not whatever thread the window browses) / error (`exclamationmark.triangle.fill`). `statusBarButtonClicked` routes by mouse button: left → `showPopover`, right/control → the context menu (which includes **Screenshot & Ask…**). **`showPopover` ACTIVATES the app** (`NSApp.activate(ignoringOtherApps: true)` THEN `makeKeyAndOrderFront`) — Why: keyboard events (notably Esc, caught by a local key monitor) only reach the popover when Conduck is the active app; a background app's windows can't become key. Accepted tradeoff: a ⌘⇧1 from another app brings Conduck forward.

**`DictationService`** state machine: `idle → recording → processing → error(message:isRetryable:)` — NO terminal `.done`; STT success hands the transcript to `onTranscript` → coordinator → VM `sendUserTurn`, then the service returns to `.idle` (the reply + in-flight UX live on the VM). `processAudio` → `STTClient.transcribe` → agent via **foreground** `URLSession` (the menu-bar app stays running — no background session). `retryLast()` against `PendingRetryMetadata`; reactive `PendingRetryStore.save` on STT-retryable error ONLY, NO pre-record arm (in-process main-actor — no OS-kill risk between start and stop). **No VAD on Mac** — tap/hotkey-to-stop (VAD is a CarPlay constraint).

**`DictationPopoverView`** = glance-and-dismiss **reply-only** surface (full browse lives in `Window("main")`). Header destination control: New chat (a gateway **submenu** when `quickConfiguredRefs.count >= 2`, else a single button) · the automatic quick thread · up to 5 recents (`fetchRecentForPicker(limit: 6)` then drop the active quick thread + `prefix(5)`) · Open window…. ONE status/answer slot resolves by priority + crossfades (keyed on a discrete phase Int so the thinking clock's per-second ticks don't re-trigger it): STT processing → "Transcribing…"; `isAwaitingReply` → a Heard line + inline thinking indicator; else the retained quick-lane reply as Markdown + the Heard line; else the start hint. **Quick-lane provenance gate:** the reply slot renders ONLY the reply to the most recent menu-bar/hotkey capture (`ConversationDetailViewModel.lastPopoverReply`, the retained agent-reply record snapshot — set on the macOS foreground success branch iff the send rode `surfacesInPopover: true`, a per-send latch mirroring `speaksReply`/`stampsQuickPointer`, inherited on `retry` via `popoverReplyMessageIDs`; a self-contained snapshot so it renders without a `messages` lookup or a reload-timing race). It NEVER shows a window-typed or iPhone/Watch reply that merely landed in the shared registry VM's conversation via CloudKit — the menu bar must not claim authorship of a turn its lane never initiated (the spoken-reply path is already scoped this way; this makes the visible reply match). A cross-thread unread reply surfaces instead via the unread dot + the read-only dot-click override (`popoverOverrideViewModel`), whose reply slot DOES show that thread's latest agent message (an explicit peek). `lastPopoverReply` is in-memory (macOS uses a foreground URLSession — the reply lands in-process, no relaunch resume needed): on a cold launch the reply slot shows the "ask" hint until the first in-process menu-bar/hotkey capture, even under `OnLaunchMode.resumeLastConversation` (which binds the quick VM to the last thread for the WINDOW's benefit) — a quick-capture HUD surfacing the last session's reply would misrepresent authorship, so this is intended, NOT a gap to close by widening the gate back to "last agent message". The in-flight `Task` is held by the long-lived coordinator-owned quick-lane VM, so a `.transient` dismiss mid-wait does NOT cancel it; reopening rebinds the same VM AND re-shows the retained reply. **Lifecycle:** `.transient` idle · `.applicationDefined` ONLY during recording (default `.transient` dismisses on outside click → orphaned audio session) · `.transient` during the agent wait. **`.preferredColorScheme(.dark)` pin** — a raw `NSHostingController` does NOT inherit the app scheme, so without the pin system-derived colors render black-on-dark in Light Mode against the hardcoded-dark `AppColors` gradient.

**Menu-bar input mode (`MenuBarInputMode`: `voice` | `text`).** Device-local App-Group raw string `Constants.menuBarInputModeKey`, NEVER KVS (per-machine, mirrors `showDockIconKey`). **Voice (default)** = the auto-record flow. **Text** = a compose surface instead (multi-line `TextField` + destination control + optional staged-screenshot thumbnail): Return sends, Shift+Return inserts a newline at the cursor (popover-scoped key monitor gated on the popover window being KEY so it never swallows main-window keys); no mic affordance (founder-locked). ⌘⇧2 stages a region + opens the compose surface (mic preflight skipped). The draft is COORDINATOR-owned (`quickDraft`) and survives an IMPLICIT click-away dismiss ONLY. **Esc + every explicit Cancel/Discard route through the universal `cancelActiveCapture` teardown** — discards the whole composition (screenshot + draft + stash), cancels any in-flight reply, clears `.error → .idle`, and releases the destination latch, in every mode/state (Why: explicit bail = clean slate, so nothing spills into the next capture; only an accidental click-away preserves work).

**Quick-lane / explicit-lane split (`MenuBarCoordinator`).** TWO bound VMs, never one shared slot: `quickViewModel` (popover + global hotkeys; sends pass `sendUserTurn(stampsQuickPointer: true)`) and `windowViewModel` (the main window; never stamps). Both come from a VM registry keyed by `conversationID`, so a conversation shown in both surfaces shares ONE instance (one in-flight state machine, no dueling appends). Each capture LATCHES a `QuickDestination` snapshot at capture START: `automatic` (the SCP/pointer-resolved quick thread or a fresh mint) and `explicitNew(RemoteAgentRef?)` (the picker's New chat; nil = persisted default, the submenu sets it) both STAMP the pointer; `explicitConversation` (a picked recent) is a one-shot detour that NEVER stamps (an explicit pick must not become the next automatic target). A `.explicitNew` mint on a NON-default gateway stamps, but the next bare automatic capture's default-gateway re-check resolves that non-default pointer to nil → fresh mint on the default (the non-default thread stays reachable via Recents). A capture against a busy (in-flight) or deleted target surfaces a **visible error with Retry** — never a silent swallow or reroute.

**Quick-lane Spoken Replies** (toggle, default OFF — TTS → Spoken Replies). `handleQuickSend` (the shared quick-lane send for voice transcripts AND text-mode typed turns) reads `getSpeakQuickLaneReplies()` ONCE at send and passes it as `sendUserTurn(speaksReply:)` — a per-SEND latch, NOT per-VM state (Why: the VM registry shares one instance popover↔window; a per-VM flag would leak window sends into speech; latch-at-send also matches `stampsQuickPointer` semantics). `speakMessageIDs` mirrors `quickStampMessageIDs` so `retry()` inherits the original turn's verdict (fail-safe silent on unknown provenance). On success the VM's `replySpeaker` seam enters via `SpeechExclusivity.claimForAutoSpeak` (refused while any mic records) then fires `ReplyVoice.shared.cancel()` THEN `.speak(reply, sanitize: true)` — cancel-FIRST is load-bearing: `claim` excludes the claimant and `speak` alone cancels a pending fetch, not live playback on the shared instance, so consecutive quick replies must supersede explicitly. `handleTypedText` (window lane) never speaks.

**TTS markdown-strip mandate** (canonical — Watch + CarPlay cross-reference this). Pipe the reply through shared `ReplySanitizer.spoken(_:)` before any speak boundary (`ReplyVoice` / `AVSpeechSynthesizer`) — raw LLM output would otherwise speak "asterisk asterisk bold". Strips emphasis/inline-code/links/list-markers/headings/blockquotes/emoji; fenced blocks → "[code block]". Visual surfaces render full Markdown; the strip is TTS-only.

**Global shortcuts + Screenshot & Ask.** `KeyboardShortcuts` SPM (Carbon hotkey, sandbox-safe — Event Tap is sandbox-hostile). TWO names: `.toggleVoiceCapture` (default ⌘⇧1) + `.captureRegionAndVoice` (default ⌘⇧2), user-configurable in Settings → General. **The `KeyboardShortcuts.Name` identifiers keep their voice-era names + the catalog key stays `…shortcut.quickCapture.label`** — renaming would orphan users' stored custom bindings / dead-end the catalog. **⌘⇧2 = region capture + ask** (macOS sibling of the iOS `Screenshot & Voice` entry; also the context-menu item) → `RegionCaptureController.captureRegion(requiresMicrophone:)` → up-front permission preflight BEFORE any overlay (Screen Recording always; mic only in voice mode — text mode types its question and never records; Screen Recording is RATIONALE-FIRST: TCC keys consent to the code SIGNATURE and it is resettable — a re-signed install, `tccutil reset`, or removal in System Settings makes the system consent dialog appear again — so no persisted flag can predict whether it will show; Conduck's rationale `NSAlert` (**Continue**/Cancel, body carries turn-it-off-and-on-again guidance for a grant recorded under a previous signature) therefore ALWAYS precedes `CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess()` (returns immediately; the first request for this TCC identity since the last reset also pops the system dialog), keeping the dialogs strictly sequential, never simultaneous. Request returns true = grant recorded but unadopted by this process → "relaunch needed" alert with a one-click **Quit Conduck** (`NSApp.terminate`, manual reopen — auto-relaunch is sandbox-hostile); returns false → the `Privacy_ScreenCapture` Settings deep-link opens unconditionally (a prior denial's repair; on a fresh ask it merely pre-opens the pane the system dialog's own button leads to). App-group `Constants.screenRecordingCaptureAttemptedKey` is ONLY the Diagnostics relevance gate, written when the preflight passes or right after the request fires — i.e. once Conduck exists in the Settings pane the row deep-links; cancelling the rationale leaves it unwritten) → a dimmed crosshair `NSWindow` overlay on the display under the cursor (single-display v1; Esc / <4 pt drag / outside cancels; mouse handling on the view, NO `CGEventTap` = sandbox-hostile; overlay torn down BEFORE capture) → `ScreenCaptureKit` (`SCScreenshotManager`, Conduck's own windows excluded, AppKit↔SCK coordinate flip, Retina scale) → **in-memory PNG, never written to disk, never logged**. `handleQuickSend` threads it as a `PendingAttachment.image` onto the SAME turn → `ImageProcessor` normalizes to the 1568px/q0.7 EXIF/GPS-stripped JPEG data-URI (the shared inline-vision path); inline-only, no file-server (see §Agent File Transfer). RETAINED on STT failure for the recovery footer. No new entitlement / Info.plist key (TCC is runtime via `CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess()`; the existing mic usage-description covers recording).

### Apple CarPlay

CarPlay is a **multi-turn voice conversation** surface — the model the `voice-based-conversation` entitlement category exists for (Siri / phone calls / ChatGPT / Perplexity). One tap arms a hands-free session; follow-ups are voice. CarPlay lives in the **iOS app target** (`CPTemplateApplicationScene`, same process as the iPhone app, reuses iOS-target shared types directly) and owns the `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.carplay.converse` background session alongside the iOS `.converse` one. Entitlement posture: team grant, adoption = portal toggle + profile regen, divergence = entitlement update not new application.

**Root = conversation picker (`CPListTemplate`, permanent root).** Gate = `SettingsManager.configuredRemoteAgentRefs().isEmpty` (refs, not the built-in-only `configuredRemoteAgentBackends()` forwarder — that shim excludes custom gateways): empty → single setup-hint row. Else section 1 = one New-voice-chat row; section 2 "Recent" = conversations to *continue*, most-recent-first, capped at `CPListTemplate.maximumItemCount − 1` (~10), no pagination. Fed by `ConversationStore.fetchRecentForPicker(limit:)` (one-actor-hop, gated `#if os(iOS) || os(macOS)`, returns `RecentConversation` snapshots). Row label (`CarPlayConversationLabel`) = `title` ?? first-user-turn snippet ?? floor. **Thread message text is NEVER displayed** (Apple driver-safety rule for this entitlement category — titles + dates only; voice resumes the thread; browse-to-continue YES, content NO). When ≥2 gateways are configured: each recent row gets a leading color-coded `GatewayBadge.image(for:ref,customs:)` — the ONLY way thread-ownership is conveyed when content text is forbidden — and the idle picker shows a leading **gateway switcher** button (session-effective gateway `displayName` → pushes a chooser `CPListTemplate`).
