**Gateway chooser = SESSION-LOCAL override, NOT a global-default writer.** It sets a per-connection `sessionDefaultRefOverride` (`RemoteAgentRef?`) on the CarPlay scene, cleared on disconnect/teardown; it NEVER calls `setDefaultRemoteAgentRef`/`setDefaultRemoteAgentBackend` and NEVER touches any active-conversation pointer — Why: writing the global default would silently re-point iPhone/iPad/Mac and clear their device-local quick pointers. Do not "simplify" it to the global setter. Session-effective ref = `sessionDefaultRefOverride ?? defaultRemoteAgentRef()`; new sessions mint on it, existing recents keep their bound `Conversation.backend`. Pre-session/idle-only (car stationary); no per-send picker during a live session.

**Permission-blocked branch.** `service.state == .permissionBlocked(.undetermined/.denied)` drives a single mic-permission picker row + spoken `speakPermissionInstruction(reason)` (CarPlay can never prompt for TCC). `AppleSpeechPreparer.prepareStandardIfAuthorized()` warms the on-device model on scene activate.

**Voice template presented MODALLY over the permanent root.** `setRootTemplate` ONCE in `didConnect`; each session `presentTemplate`s `service.voiceControlTemplate` (`CPVoiceControlTemplate` is modal-only — not pushable, non-idiomatic as a root), session end `dismissTemplate`s back to the picker. `applyState` chokepoint: in-session states (`.recording`/`.processing`/`.speaking`/`.muted`) → `ensureVoicePresented`; `.idle`/`.error`/`.permissionBlocked` → `ensureVoiceDismissed` + refreshed picker. **`templateDidDisappear(voiceTemplate)` is the AUTHORITATIVE teardown trigger** (not diagnostic), guarded `=== service.voiceControlTemplate` — Why: `CPVoiceControlTemplate`'s modal lifecycle is empirically unreliable, so a system/user dismiss can bypass the End handler; this ends the session + deactivates DIRECTLY (modal already gone → no dismiss completion fires). No-ops on End/timeout/error/background (they already flipped `sessionActive` false). The template never disappears between turns (re-arm goes `.speaking→.recording` directly), so it fires only on genuine session end.

**Picker tap starts the session** (not cold-connect — multi-turn sessions are user-initiated). Tapping any row seeds the per-session in-memory pin via `CarPlayRecordingService.beginSession(conversationID:defaultRef:)` (`sessionConversationID`; New → nil, first turn mints fresh on `defaultRef` = the session-effective gateway), presents the voice modal, starts the session — no intermediate screen.

**Multi-turn loop = per-SESSION state machine** (`CarPlayRecordingService`, not per-turn). Per turn: **listen** (`AVAudioEngine` + Silero VAD, CarPlay preset) → end-of-speech → **STT foreground** (inside `beginBackgroundTask`) → append `user` turn (`sourceDevice "carplay"`) against `sessionConversationID`, assemble client-owned prior turns → **think** (background converse hop) → reply lands (foreground + voice-root + matching in-flight `turnToken`) → append `agent` turn (append-once) → `ReplySanitizer.spoken` → **speak** — the voice template stays on the THINKING visual until FIRST AUDIO (`speakAgent`'s `onFirstAudio`, guarded to `.processing`, flips to `.speaking`): head-chunk cloud synthesis takes seconds, and a "Replying" label over that silence reads as a hang — and a HUNG synth can't pin it there (`ReplyVoice`'s 45 s first-audio watchdog hands the reply to Apple); on finish wait `carPlayHFPSettleDelay` (~300ms, HFP route settle) then auto re-arm. `reArmAfterSettle` gates on a scene-injected live-`presentedTemplate` closure — if the modal vanished without a teardown signal it ends instead of listening behind the picker (nil closure → assume presented, never false-kill).

**Session ends** (→ `.idle` → dismiss → deactivate in dismiss completion) on: **End** button (any state); a non-End modal dismiss (caught by `templateDidDisappear`); zero-input follow-up timeout (`carPlayFollowUpSilenceTimeout` ~5s → brief sign-off + end); unrecoverable `remoteAgent*` error (error TTS + end); app backgrounded (in-flight converse still completes + syncs via background session, no TTS). **`endSession` cancels in-flight reply TTS at its top** (`CarPlaySpeechService.cancel()`) — every terminal path funnels through it, and the chunk queue would otherwise keep fetching tail chunks and STARTING per-chunk players after a background/disconnect deactivate (unsolicited audio + implicit session re-activation); the sign-off/error `speak` arm runs after the cancel, so terminal TTS still plays. **No acoustic barge-in** (hot mic during TTS unreliable across vehicle HFP/echo hardware) — **End** is the stop control. Do NOT truncate replies; verbose TTS is wanted.

**Mute = mic-mute, call-style** (`toggleMute`): stops capture (discards partial), parks the session on `.muted` — session stays LIVE, HFP route HELD, never signed off for silence. An in-flight reply still lands/persists/syncs/**speaks** (output unaffected); `unmute()` re-arms with no re-activation. Re-sync the Mute button at `startSession` BEFORE the next present — Why: mutating `trailingNavigationBarButtons` in `applyState(.idle)` is a post-dismiss template mutation = CarPlay assertion source. The stable End+Mute `CPBarButton` pair is installed ONCE in `didConnect` before first present (CarPlay does not reliably render nav-bar buttons swapped in after a template is presented).

**Session isolation.** `sessionConversationID` is in-memory only, seeded by `beginSession` and cleared on `endSession`/`teardown`; CarPlay never reads or writes the shared per-device quick-capture pointer — Why: a drive must not retarget the Action-Button/menu-bar thread; a post-drive phone capture behaves as if the drive never happened. In-memory by design (the relaunch reply path rides `RemoteAgentBackgroundMetadata.conversationID`; a relaunched process can't resume a live voice session).

**Audio-session lifecycle (deactivate-once OWNER).** Activated ONCE at session start, held across all turns + TTS + re-listens, deactivated **EXACTLY ONCE** at session end via the single idempotent `deactivateAudioSession()` chokepoint (`.notifyOthersOnDeactivation`) — Why: else the head unit stays locked in "call" state and the driver loses radio/nav. Foreground terminal paths we initiate (End/timeout/error) free it INSIDE the `dismissTemplate` completion (after the modal is gone — a synchronous-deactivate-then-async-root-swap race drops the app to the dashboard); background/disconnect AND a `templateDidDisappear` system dismiss free it DIRECTLY. `endSession(speak:)` never deactivates inline; both guards (`sessionActive`, `audioActivated`) are idempotent. `SpeechPlayer` (behind `ReplyVoice`, the single speak boundary with exactly-once completion) pins `AVSpeechSynthesizer.usesApplicationAudioSession = true` and NEVER calls `setCategory`/`setActive` — synth and every cloud-mp3 `AVAudioPlayer` (incl. `SpeechChunkQueue`'s per-chunk players; long replies chunk on CarPlay like iPhone) ride the single held session; `false` would create/teardown its own session, breaking deactivate-once + dropping the HFP route between turns.

**Engine start MUST run in the `presentTemplate` completion, ONLY on a successful present** — Why: races voice-template attach vs `AVAudioSession.setActive`; synchronous `engine.start()` fails FourCC `'!obj'` (560947818), and a failed present must never leave a session recording behind a missing modal. A fresh session's first listen waits `carPlayColdStartSettleDelay` (~0.6s, longer than in-session re-arm) after `setActive(true)` — a cross-session cold start reacquires RemoteIO after the prior session's HFP teardown, else `engine.start()` is refused FourCC `'nope'` (1852797029), a *wedged* state a longer wait alone does not clear. `startCaptureEngineWithRetry` (3 attempts, fresh engine+tap each) does a HARD recovery on attempts 1+: `CarPlayAudioSession.deactivateForRecovery()` uses `options: []` NOT `.notifyOthersOnDeactivation` — Why: it is an internal deactivate→reactivate WITHIN a live session, not a release, so it must not wake competing apps (the once-per-session terminal release is unchanged). **On exhaustion or any `startListening` setup bailout, end SILENTLY (`endSession(speak: nil)`)** — NEVER speak over a wedged session: that escalates a recoverable `'nope'` into a `mediaserverd` `Connection invalidated` + CarPlay-scene drop. A `mediaServicesWereResetNotification` observer disposes the capture engine + speech objects, clears `audioActivated`, and ends silently (every audio object is a zombie after a reset and must be rebuilt).

**Audio interruption** (`handleInterruption`): `.began` while `.recording` → upload the partial (truncated transcript beats a discarded one); `.began` while `.speaking` → let the system drive (`AVSpeechSynthesizer` auto-pauses, completion fires on its didFinish/didCancel); `.ended` → no auto-resume.

**VAD = FluidAudio `VadManager`** (Silero CoreML, bundled `.mlmodelc`), NOT Apple `SpeechDetector` — Why: `SpeechAnalyzer` requires pairing with `SpeechTranscriber`, forcing a TCC prompt + per-locale model download. `EndOfSpeechDetector` uses `carPlayVADThreshold` (~0.65, higher to reject road/cabin noise) + `carPlayVADMinSilence` (~0.8s); `Constants.maxAudioDuration = 300` hard cap; no warning UI (driver safety). Pure-VAD endpointing matches ChatGPT/Perplexity; no manual force-send.

**`CarPlaySettings`** `@MainActor` STT cache (`activePresetID` + `sttAPIKey` + `preferredLanguage` + `customModel`, all from one atomic `activeSTTSnapshot()` hop so a turn never observes a torn preset/model pairing) — `customModel` is threaded into `transcribe(customModel:)` so a Gemini/Qwen override applies in-car. The BYO `customConfig` is intentionally NOT cached (CarPlay routes only cloud + Apple providers at V1; a custom-active preset falls through to the unconfigured copy). `refreshFromSettings()` runs at launch + on `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` — avoids Keychain hops in the audio hot path.

**Converse hop = background URLSession `…carplay.converse`** (`CarPlayConverseUploader`, 300/600s timeouts — see Remote Agent Round-Trip): a different mechanism from `beginBackgroundTask`, survives suspension when the driver navigates to Maps. On 2xx: decode `choices[0].message.content`, append `agent` turn (single owner), no quick-capture pointer stamp. Route on scene state: foreground + voice-root + matching active turn → speak; else persist + sync only, no TTS (Apple forbids unsolicited audio from a backgrounded app). `defer`-removes the request-body temp file on every path.

**Audio-pipeline gotchas** (fixed 16kHz mono tap; `AVAudioConverter` `.noDataNow` never `.endOfStream`; single engine, no parallel `AVAudioRecorder`; `computeUnits = .cpuOnly` on Simulator; observe `AVAudioEngineConfigurationChange` AFTER `engine.start()`; `.voiceChat` mode; no `audio` in `UIBackgroundModes`). Session category `.playAndRecord / .voiceChat / [.defaultToSpeaker, .allowBluetoothHFP]`, no mid-session swap. No `.duckOthers`/mix option — Why: the held session INTERRUPTS other audio (music/podcast/radio pauses the instant listening starts, resumes at session end via `.notifyOthersOnDeactivation`), so a ducked-but-still-playing source can't bleed into the cabin mic and false-trigger the VAD as never-ending speech.

**Scene-manifest defense:** BOTH `INFOPLIST_KEY_UIApplicationSceneManifest_Generation = NO` (Debug + Release) AND hand-authored `Info.plist` via `INFOPLIST_FILE` — Why: Xcode auto-gen silently drops the scene key; template refresh re-introduces `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE = YES`. Symptom if violated: assertion crash on CarPlay external-display connect.

### Apple Watch + Widget

**`RecordNoteControl`** (`ControlWidgetButton`, `kind="ai.gigaduck.AgentRelay.watch.RecordNoteControl"`) → **`RecordNoteIntent`** (`.foreground(.immediate)`) → `WatchRecordingCoordinator.shared.requestStart()`. `WatchRecordingCoordinator` (`@MainActor @Observable` singleton, touched in `WatchApp.init()` so it exists before any intent fires) and `RecordNoteIntent` are **duplicated files in BOTH the watch-app and the widget-extension target** (Approach B). Any rewrite scoped by filename misses the widget copy, whose labels surface in Control Center / Smart Stack / Action Button — mirror edits to both. **`WatchAppShortcuts`** (`AppShortcutsProvider`) exposes `RecordNoteIntent` to force the `appintentsd` install-time index — without it the first ControlWidget tap fails on a fresh install.

**`WatchRecordingService` state machine:** `idle → arming → recording → uploading → waiting → error` (no terminal reply card — the reply lands as a thread bubble). **`.arming` withholds the `.start` haptic and recording visuals until `AVAudioRecorder` is genuinely live** — the overlay renders during `.arming` but the user must not be told to speak before the recorder exists. Before activating it **polls `WKApplication.applicationState == .active`** (≤1 s, 50 ms intervals) — ControlWidget cold-launch leaves state `.inactive` briefly and the mic fails to activate otherwise. Session activation (`setCategory(.record)` + sync `setActive(true)` — valid for a record session; only PLAYBACK sessions need the async activate API) is issued **off the main actor** through the `WatchAudioSessionCoordinator` config lane (see Audio-session arbitration below), with `captureGeneration` + `state == .arming` re-checked after the hop — a cancel landing mid-IPC bails silently (`capture.staleArm`), never an error over the user's own cancel. `AVAudioRecorder` is constructed on the main actor AFTER activation (non-Sendable — never crosses the `@concurrent` hop). Active TTS is stopped before the mic activates; recording is driven from the SERVICE independent of navigation (never gated on the thread view finishing its load).

**Capture-in-thread (unified surface).** Both quick-capture triggers land in the SAME `WatchConversationThreadView`. `WatchNoteView` is launchpad + nav host; a `WatchRoute.capture(WatchCaptureTarget)` pushes a (possibly draft) thread that auto-starts recording inside itself. **`WatchCaptureTarget = .existing(UUID) | .new(backendRef:)` is resolved at TRIGGER time** (existing-vs-new + the gateway ref captured the moment the trigger fires) so a pointer / default-gateway change mid-recording cannot silently reroute the turn. **Persistence is LAZY:** a `.new` target mints NOTHING up front (draft shell, no `conversationID`); the conversation is minted at the first non-empty transcript in `resolveActiveConversationAndBackend()` and the draft adopts the real id via `inFlightConversationID`. **No eager-mint / no delete-on-exit** — view-disappear / wrist-down / suspension are NOT cancellation boundaries on watchOS, so a cancel before any transcript leaves no conversation behind. The capture overlay dominates the thread during `.arming`/`.recording` (history dims, composer/scroll/Speak/back-nav suppressed) and carries its own `isLuminanceReduced` branch so recording state survives wrist-down/AOD. The only exit while capturing is an AOD-gated Cancel (top-leading, `!isLuminanceReduced`) — a 44 pt hit target with visible circular chrome. **Stop is the `Tap to Stop` BUTTON alone — never a whole-surface tap-to-stop gesture:** a full-screen stop target sits underneath the cancel-X and converts every near-miss on it into a stop-and-send, shipping the audio the user meant to discard. A tap that hits neither button does nothing — the destructive direction here is sending, not discarding. **Draft pop-on-discard:** a `.new` draft whose capture retires un-minted has nothing to render (its nil-id load never runs — a stranded forever-spinner), so the thread view pops itself (`popDraftIfNeeded`; `.existing`/adopted-id threads never pop) — driven by the service's `captureDiscardCount` outcome counter (bumped ONLY in `cancelRecording()` entered from an active state + the empty-transcript reset) plus direct pops on the two user taps (cancel-X, error-banner dismiss). **Never infer the pop from `state == .idle` + nil id** — observation coalesces across suspension (a backgrounded mint→reply resumes as `.idle`+nil and would pop a REAL conversation); a counter increment survives coalescing and the minted path never touches it. **`dismissError()` must never bump** — it doubles as the internal error-supersede (new-attempt entry points, relay-success auto-clear), where a bump pops a live draft mid-mint; user abandonment pops view-locally instead. `WatchCaptureDiscardOutcomeTests` pins the contract.

**STT dispatch.** Apple branch (`activePresetID == "apple-on-device"`): enqueue-FIRST to `AppleRelayPendingQueue` (durable, queue owns the audio) then ship via `AppleSpeechRelayCoordinator.relay(...)` — full lifecycle at Apple On-Device STT § Watch Option B audio relay. Cloud branch: `AVAudioRecorder` records M4A 48 kHz mono AAC, **compressed to 16 kHz mono before upload** (~4–5× smaller; the 300 s hard cap keeps even a maximal recording well under the 15 MB server bound, so compression runs unconditionally — a sub-2 KB husk is discarded pre-compression as too short) → foreground upload via `WatchNetworkClient.uploadSTT(...)` (`URLSession.shared`, 2-min frontmost window) → on timeout/suspend, fall back to background `WatchAudioUploader.uploadSTT(...)` on the `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.watch.stt` session.

**In-app "Ask" = ALWAYS-new + gateway choice** (distinct from the headless ControlWidget/Action-Button path). ≥2 configured gateways → `.confirmationDialog` picks the backend; one → push straight in. Either pushes `.new(backendRef:)` and stamps the chosen ref as a one-shot hint via `setPendingInAppNewConversationBackend` → **App-Group UserDefaults, never iCloud KVS** (device-local transient that must not sync), consumed FIRST in `resolveActiveConversationAndBackend()` (returns a gated `(id, ref, stampsQuickPointer)` triple). Persisted (not in-memory) because the converse hop can re-enter in a relaunched process via the background-STT-fallback delegate. **Headless triggers never use the Ask picker** — `resolveHeadlessCaptureTarget()` returns `.existing` iff the per-device pointer is SCP-TTL-fresh AND that thread still exists AND it is still bound to the CURRENT default gateway (this default-gateway re-check is mirrored at trigger time because the bound branch deliberately never re-checks a pinned thread); any miss → `.new(defaultBackendRef)`. The Ask hint is cleared at the headless entry and whenever an `.existing` capture starts — the only incorrect consumer is a headless turn, so clearing there is airtight against every STT-stage error exit.

**In-thread composer (continue an open conversation).** `WatchMessageComposerBar` mounts as a floating bottom `.overlay` (scroll-to-hide; NOT `.safeAreaInset` — that would resize the ScrollView mid-scroll on toggle). It uses a NATIVE single-line `TextField` (no custom capsule / `.textFieldStyle(.plain)`: on watchOS `.plain` does NOT suppress system field chrome, double-stacking into a visible artifact) + the single-`Image` morphing trailing button (`.symbolEffect(.replace)` — a single Button wrapping a single Image, same identity trick as iOS). Both paths pin to the OPEN `conversationID` on its stored `Conversation.backend`. Two service entry points: `startRecording(boundTo:)` (voice) and `sendTypedText(_:into:)` (STT-free — bypasses `AVAudioRecorder`/session, calls `startConverseHop` directly); both set `pendingConversationID` and `resolveActiveConversationAndBackend()` short-circuits on this pin FIRST without consuming the always-new Ask hint (a parallel Ask survives a bound send). The bound branch returns the verdict the pin site set (`pendingPinStampsQuickPointer`): **composer / deferred-drain pins are EXPLICIT → `stampsQuickPointer: false`** (never write the wrist's per-device pointer — replying in an old thread must not hijack where the next headless capture lands); a `.existing` headless continuation is IMPLICIT → `true` (pointer TTL extends). User turns stamp `sourceDevice` `watch-text`/`watch-voice`. The in-flight indicator is an **agent-side, left-aligned in-thread row at the bottom of the message list**, gated to the open thread (`inFlightConversationID == conversationID`) by recording state — NOT a centered banner; subdued under `isLuminanceReduced`.

**Agent hop = ALWAYS background URLSession on Watch — no foreground branching.** `applicationState == .active` can drop the moment the user lowers their wrist mid-think, so branching on it is a race. `uploadConverse(...)` is invoked from the STT upload's `urlSession(_:task:didCompleteWithError:)` — two-hop chaining inside the Hop-1 delegate — on the `ai.gigaduck.agentrelay.watch.converse` session (300/600 s timeouts, see Remote Agent Round-Trip). The `stampsQuickPointer` verdict rides the task metadata (`stampsActiveConversation`); the reply delegate stamps the per-device pointer only when `true`, so a pinned-composer reply landing post-relaunch can't retarget the quick lane. Watch may relaunch twice (one per hop); **`sessionSendsLaunchEvents = true`** on both background sessions covers each. `ConduckWatchApp` registers TWO `.urlSession` handlers and the delegate routes by session identifier. Hard-kill between hops → turn dropped, user re-speaks (acceptable degradation; a durable cross-process resume state machine is over-engineering). Audio cleanup (audio-cleanup mandate; see STT Wire Formats) is non-negotiable — Watch storage is constrained.

**Reachability is wire-level (no pre-flight probe).** No `HEAD`/`GET /v1/models` before arming the mic — local network-state caches drift on every transition (wrist drop, gateway restart, Tailnet reconnect, cellular handoff, BT↔Wi-Fi route change) and report stale reachability. The upload fails with the appropriate `AppError` and the user re-taps; Settings "Test Connection" is the user-initiated reachability surface. **The launchpad carries NO reachability warning** — a pre-flight claim would rest on two inputs that are least reliable at exactly that moment: `WCSession.isReachable` is the counterpart APP's status, not the physical iPhone or the OS companion route (see Companion-routing trap), and `activePresetID` can still be the cold-start `apple-on-device` default, indistinguishable by value from a real user selection (`lastEnvelopeTimestamp == 0`). The relay's own enqueue-first deferral notice at the reply-wait timeout is the honest surface — it fires on an ACTUAL running relay, never on settings or reachability.

**Companion-routing trap (watchOS platform reality).** When the paired iPhone is powered ON + in Bluetooth range but offline (Wi-Fi off / Tailnet down / no signal), watchOS routes the Watch's OWN outbound HTTPS THROUGH the iPhone (`"prefer companion"` / NECP policy) and won't fall back to the Watch's Wi-Fi — so even a PUBLIC turn (OpenRouter) fails from the nearby iPhone's dead network; only iPhone fully OFF / out of range frees the Watch radio. (The SAME OS routing is why a tailnet-only gateway IS reachable from the wrist when the iPhone is nearby + online — the Watch's request rides the iPhone's Tailscale; only STANDALONE does the lack of a watchOS Tailscale client bite.) **No public API bypasses it** (Network.framework interface-pinning is unsupported for `URLSession` on watchOS, TN3135; no `URLSessionConfiguration` / workout-session / cellular / entitlement lever selects the interface) and the Watch CANNOT prove the iPhone is the cause (WCSession reachability is app-level, ≠ the OS companion link) → the only remedy is honest hedged copy. `WatchNetworkFailureCopy.transportFailureMessage` maps the transport `URLError`: `.notConnectedToInternet` (-1009) → an iPhone-aware "your watch may be using its connection" hint; `.timedOut`/`.cannotConnectToHost`/`.networkConnectionLost` → a generic connection hint; everything else (DNS codes + all HTTP/body gateway errors, which take the status-map path) → the call site's existing copy, so the hint never fires on a real gateway failure. **Don't try to "fix" the failure itself** (no transport workaround exists; relaying the converse through the iPhone is the wrong reflex — see Remote Agent Round-Trip Reachability).

**Reply surface = the thread bubble (no transient card).** On reply (background delegate, app possibly suspended): decode → `appendMessage(role: .agent)` to the `ConversationStore` (syncs to all devices; the Watch reads the local synced store, no remote API) → `UNUserNotificationCenter` (200-char body, `userInfo` carries `conversationID` + backend ref) → `.success` haptic. Open thread: `.conversationsDidChange` → `refreshThread` with scroll-to-bottom. **Suspended-reply deep-link:** a notification tap fires `WatchNotificationDelegate`, which reads `conversationID` and hands it to `WatchReplyDeepLinkCoordinator` (singleton touched in `ConduckWatchApp.init` so a cold-launch tap isn't lost); `WatchNoteView` pushes the BROWSE route `WatchRoute.thread(id)` — browse never `.capture(...)`: a notification tap must NOT auto-start the mic (there is no `onOpenURL` on watchOS).

**TTS on Watch.** Replies arrive **silent by default**; the per-Watch "Speak replies aloud" toggle (`watch.readRepliesAloud`) opts into auto-speak. **Delivery is WCSession-primary:** the iPhone includes the bool on `assembleSettingsPayload()`, the Watch persists it to its App-Group mirror, and `WatchSettingsReader.readRepliesAloud()` reads that mirror FIRST; iCloud KVS is the cold-launch fallback only. **Why not KVS-alone:** KVS is unreliable/laggy on watchOS (`ubiquityIdentityToken` always nil there), so the flag frequently never arrives. `WatchRecordingService` latches a `WatchCaptureSource` at every capture entry; the VOICE sources (`.headless`/`.ask`/`.composer`) are auto-speak-eligible, while TYPED (`.composerText`) and the deferred Apple-relay drain (nil — temporally detached from the wrist-raise) stay silent → **"voice speaks, text doesn't"**. `handleBackgroundReply` computes the eligibility verdict at the TOP (before `clearInFlight` wipes the tag) and — **regardless of wrist state** — stages `AutoSpeakMailbox.shared.request(...)` for the open thread's `attemptAutoSpeak()` to consume. `attemptAutoSpeak` is the separate **play gate** (speaks only while the scene is `.active`, checked BEFORE the destructive `consume`) and re-fires on the wrist-raise (`.onChange(of: scenePhase) == .active`) — so a reply that landed wrist-down speaks when the user raises their wrist, within the mailbox freshness window. A reply-notification tap-open speaks source-independently; a process relaunch loses the in-memory tag → the arrival-stage can't fire, but the notification tap still speaks. The per-message Speak control is the shared cross-platform `ThreadSpeaker` state machine (driven by `WatchReplySpeaker`, a `SpeakEngine`) — behaves identically to iOS: idle→loading→playing→paused, tap-to-pause / resume-from-position, supersede on a different reply, agent bubbles only. Cloud TTS active (synced via the envelope, see Cross-Device Sync) → `WatchReplySpeaker` fetches mp3 via `WatchTTSClient` then plays an `AVAudioPlayer`, with `AVSpeechSynthesizer` fallback on throw (voice locked `.default` — no `.enhanced`/`.premium`/Personal Voice on watchOS); a per-turn **20 s first-audio watchdog** (the `ReplyVoice` pattern, wrist-tuned: only frozen cloud vendors reach the wrist and the `.wristConservative` head is ≤ 220 chars, but the BT→paired-iPhone relay is jittery) bounds a HUNG synth — expiry cancels the stalled leg without firing, then Apple speaks the whole reply through the same exactly-once funnel — and the chunk queue's **10 s seam-stall grace** (`SpeechChunkQueue.seamStallTimeout`, Watch-only; nil on iOS/CarPlay) Apple-speaks the unplayed remainder when a TAIL fetch wedges after audio started; supersede-safe like `SpeechPlayer` (one-shot `fireCompletion` + `generation` token + per-object `ObjectIdentifier` guards). `ReplySanitizer.spoken(_:)` markdown-strip runs first (Watch is highest-stakes — BT-route TTS with hands occupied).

**Built-in-speaker dim-cut handling.** watchOS suspends built-in-speaker audio the instant the always-on screen dims (a platform limit — background audio survives only on a **Bluetooth** route; `.longForm`/route-picker is deliberately NOT used for glanceable replies), and fires no terminal delegate. **PRIMARY: own the cut at the dim edge.** The thread view's DIM-direction hooks (`isLuminanceReduced` flipping true; `scenePhase` leaving `.active`) call `ThreadSpeaker.systemDimPause()` — a proactive, position-preserving `engine.pause()` that flips `.playing` → `.paused` and marks `pausedBySystem`, executed while the ambient transition still gives the app runtime. Route-gated by `WatchReplySpeaker.outputRouteSurvivesDim` (a Bluetooth route survives the dim — AirPods playback is never interrupted); guarded to `.playing` (a USER-paused reply never gains auto-resume eligibility). Owning the pause also parks a chunk queue caught between chunks (a landing fetch can't blare under the dim). **Why proactive, not reconstructive:** the suspension can freeze `AVAudioPlayer` with `isPlaying` still reading `true`, so any after-the-fact status probe sees `.active` and the stuck-`.playing`/two-tap bug survives every raise. **FALLBACK: reconcile on the raise.** `WatchReplySpeaker.playbackStatus` (`SpeakEngine`, default `.active` off-Watch) exposes the engine's real state (`active`/`pausedResumable`/`inactive`); `ThreadSpeaker.reconcileSystemPauseIfNeeded()` flips a stuck `.playing` to `.paused` (marking `pausedBySystem`, or `silentReset()` if truly dead). Invoked at the same-message tap preflight (top of `speak`) AND on the wrist-raise (`scenePhase == .active` + the `isLuminanceReduced` un-dim edge — luminance is the extra edge since the ambient dim may keep the scene `.active`). A **SYSTEM**-paused reply (never a user-paused one — `pausedBySystem` gates it) **auto-resumes on wrist-raise within a 30 s window** via `autoResumeIfSystemPaused()`; a later raise clears the mark (stays paused + tappable, one-tap resume, no jump-scare).

**Audio-session arbitration (`WatchAudioSessionCoordinator`, `@MainActor` singleton).** One shared `AVAudioSession`, three reconfigurers — the capture arm, the interruption resume, and BOTH `WatchReplySpeaker` instances (the service's + the thread view's `ThreadSpeaker` engine) — and every config call is audio-server IPC (hundreds of ms routinely; seconds under a distressed daemon), so none may run on the main actor or land on behalf of a dead turn. Two mechanisms. **Ownership claims** (monotonic token; a new claim supersedes, staling the old): the recorder claims `.recording` synchronously at arm — deliberately BEFORE `speech.stop()`, so the yielding speaker's terminal release is stale and cannot schedule a teardown under the mic — and each `speak()` turn claims `.playback`. **Serialized config lane:** all `setCategory`/`setActive` IPC routes through `runConfig(for:)` — strictly FIFO, claim re-checked at ISSUE time (a superseded turn's still-queued config is skipped, never issued onto the new owner's session), IPC `@concurrent` off-main. **Terminal deactivation = the duck-restore:** speaker terminals (`fireCompletion`/`cancel`/`stopInFlight`) release-and-deactivate — `setActive(false, .notifyOthersOnDeactivation)` after a 200 ms grace (an immediate re-claim — next bubble tap, reply auto-speak, capture arm — cancels it, avoiding deactivate→reactivate churn) riding the same FIFO lane with an unclaimed re-check, so the user's ducked podcast/music resumes at every spoken-reply end. **Recorder release posture is split:** a tenure whose `.record` config COMMITTED releases plain (the record session staying active is deliberate — the next speak re-categorizes it); a tenure that died PRE-config (mic denied, cancel during arming, activator throw) releases-and-deactivates, because it may have superseded live playback and would otherwise strand an active `.duckOthers` session that ducks the user's audio forever. Interruption `.ended` re-activates via `setActive(true)`-ONLY through the same lane (never re-stomps category), gated on the live claim + `captureGeneration` + `state == .recording`, logged `capture.interrupt.*` (WatchLog, metadata-only). The speaker's category write is chained through the lane too — `speak()` never blocks the main thread on session IPC — and its 20 s first-audio watchdog is armed before the chained config, so a wedged category IPC is bounded by the same Apple-voice fallback.

**Identity & distribution.** Watch Keychain (cache) ← `WCSession.updateApplicationContext` ← iCloud KVS; the Watch never generates a UUID, and its Keychain is NOT shared with the iPhone (STT key + gateway token arrive via `WCSession.transferUserInfo`/`updateApplicationContext`). `WatchSettingsReader` reads synced settings from iCloud KVS (App-Group defaults don't sync between devices). **Exception — the active-conversation pointer is Watch-LOCAL:** App-Group UserDefaults shared with the widget-extension process for cold ControlWidget launches, NEVER KVS — a Mac/iPhone write must not steer the wrist thread. The `SessionContinuationPolicy` it gates is read App-Group-first (couriered in the multi-gateway broadcast envelope's `sessionPolicy` slot), with a one-time legacy KVS seed on the first cold launch post-update (see Settings & Storage). `updateActiveSTT(presetID:apiKey:timestamp:)` is an atomic 3-field update that rejects older timestamps; `apiKey: String?` and Apple skips the Keychain write. Token rules: token → `WCSession`, persisted in Watch Keychain `remoteAgent.token.<rawValue>` (`kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlock`), never KVS; a pairing race (Watch foregrounds before the envelope) falls back to a KVS URLs-only read and disables the ControlWidget action until the token arrives; rotation overwrites the slot, in-flight requests fail naturally, next turn uses the new token.

**Watch does NOT:** edit URL/token/backend (iPhone-only setup); run a continuous mode; run an LLM/VAD pipeline; attach files. Attachments render per-attachment via the shared pure classifier `AttachmentRecord.watchDisplayClass` — ORDER load-bearing: server reference FIRST (a server-ref PNG is `isImage` AND `isServerFile`; classifying image-first would double-render placeholder + fallback), routed by its synced preview (see Agent File Transfer → Preview enrichment): `thumbnailData` → inline thumbnail; `hasTextPreview` (`previewKind=="text"`) → TAPPABLE row into the same text viewer, which lazy-loads the blob via `ConversationStore.fetchPreviewText` (the snapshot NEVER carries `previewData` — a file-rich thread must not fault 128 KiB blobs per row; nil blob = CloudKit asset not yet arrived → missing-state copy) and footers "Saved copy from when this reply arrived" (snapshot honesty — chip re-downloads on capable devices stay fresh); no preview → informative passive marker (filename/`Attached file` + `ByteCountFormatter` size + "On your iPhone, iPad or Mac" — no wrist download by design); image → small synced `thumbnailData` thumbnail (~50 KB, never full-resolution bytes) with `[Image attached]` fallback until it syncs; text/code with synced `extractedText` ≤ `watchViewableTextByteCeiling` (128 KB — a multi-hundred-KB `Text` layout is a wrist freeze) → TAPPABLE row (doc glyph + filename) pushing `WatchRoute.attachmentText` (IDs-only route, content-free) → `WatchAttachmentTextView`, a scrolling read-only viewer loading `extractedText` from the store (watchOS has no QuickLook; native back = dismiss; AOD dims by opacity only — no layout branch on luminance; missing row → passive "no longer available"); oversized text → passive filename + "Too large to view on Apple Watch"; nil `extractedText` (not-yet-synced) → passive `[File attached]`. Capture routes still win — the history's `.allowsHitTesting` suppresses the row during capture, and a capture push covers the stack. **Alert-collision guard:** the notification-permission `.task` skips the prompt while `pendingStart || isRecordingFlowActive` (the system queues the mic and notification prompts simultaneously and auto-dismisses one).

**First-run welcome (`WatchOnboardingView`).** A one-time full-screen takeover the FIRST time the watch app opens — gated by `WatchSettingsReader.hasSeenOnboarding()` (App-Group-local `watch.onboardingSeen`; never KVS / never couriered from the iPhone; reset only by a reinstall). Two jobs: a warm impression, and an honest expectation-set — the first one or two asks warm up before the wrist path settles, so a slow first turn doesn't read as broken (copy: "the first ask or two may need a moment to warm up. It settles after that."). Configures NOTHING (no wrist keyboard). The root gate in `ConduckWatchApp` renders it AHEAD of the identity gate but DEFERS to a pending capture (`WatchRecordingCoordinator.pendingStart || isRecordingFlowActive` — read live off the `@Observable` coordinator so an Action-Button capture arriving mid-welcome also wins), so a cold-launch into a recording is never interrupted; the seen-flag latches ONLY on the "Got it" tap (a killed first launch re-shows). Restrained to the wrist idiom: near-black background, a single one-shot opacity-led entrance (no looping animation), Reduce-Motion honored; `.onAppear` pokes the identity/settings courier so the read-dwell warms the first ask.

### iPhone-side Watch setup (guide)

Teaches putting Conduck on the wrist; the watch runs only the one-time first-run welcome above, never on-wrist setup. `WatchSetupGuideView` (iOS-only `.fullScreenCover`, mirrors `SetupGuideView` chrome) — **2 paged steps**: a value-prop welcome carrying an install note (Conduck auto-installs with the iPhone app; otherwise add it from the Watch app's Available Apps — so the guide never dead-ends for a not-yet-installed wrist) + a first-launch sync heads-up (the initial CloudKit import settles in a few seconds — sets expectations rather than reading as a hang) → **"Add GigaAction to your Watch"** (control display name `GigaAction`). Control Center and the Action Button are **two entry points to the SAME `RecordNoteControl`** — either alone suffices — so the step presents **Control Center as the default path** (works on every Apple Watch; opened via the side button — the press-and-hold-face gesture is pre-watchOS-10 and wrong) and the **Action Button as a collapsed Ultra-only "Alternatively…" disclosure** (NOT a forced third step — the iPhone can't reliably detect a paired Ultra, so the non-Ultra majority must never be marched through it). **Neither shared prerequisite is taught here:** no allow-mic step (`RecordNoteIntent` runs `.foreground(.immediate)`, so the first GigaAction press foregrounds the Watch app and the system shows the mic prompt in-context — self-correcting), and gateway reachability is owned by the gateway Guided-Setup disclosure and surfaced at failure by `.remoteAgentUnreachable` (repeating it on a UI-config screen is out of context). Built on the shared `onboardingStepLayout` scaffold (scroll body + pinned footer) so Done stays reachable when the disclosure expands at large Dynamic Type. Copy is chatbot-framed. Smart Stack / complications are out of scope (separate WidgetKit widgets).

**One entry point — `Settings ▸ Setup ▸ Apple Watch`** — **ALWAYS shown** (no pairing-state gate, so the user can always see Conduck runs on the wrist), **iPhone-only** (`userInterfaceIdiom == .phone` — a Watch pairs with an iPhone, not an iPad). It pushes `WatchSettingsView`, which renders FOUR sections: the **default-gateway picker** ("Follow iPhone" + each configured gateway → `setWatchDefaultOverrideRef`, reusing `DefaultGatewayPicker` with a prepended Follow-iPhone row); a **session-continuation-policy picker** (with a Follow-iPhone option → `setWatchSessionPolicyOverride` — the Watch-effective SCP gated at active-pointer resolution); a **"Speak replies aloud" toggle** (`watch.readRepliesAloud` → `setWatchReadRepliesAloud`); and a **"Set up Apple Watch"** button opening `WatchSetupGuideView` (`.fullScreenCover`). Watch setup is passive-discovery only — there is no proactive home-screen nudge.

---

## Notification Presentation

`UNUserNotification` banner per surface on agent reply, EXCEPT the macOS **in-app** reply path — which posts no notification and surfaces replies via a menu-bar unread dot. macOS **shares** are the exception-to-the-exception: they keep their reply + failure notification (reach when the menu bar is hidden) AND raise the dot. iOS/Watch presentation is decided at delivery via `willPresent`.

| Surface | Banner fires |
|---|---|
| iOS Shortcut headless (`ConverseIntent`) | Always — app suspended, `willPresent` never fires |
| iOS in-app composer (iPhone/iPad) | Iff app foreground AND target thread NOT in `ActiveViewTracker` |
| macOS in-app (popover + window) | NEVER — reply raises a brand-colored unread dot on the status item, cleared when the thread is viewed |
| macOS share (`SharedInboxDrainer` → foreground `RemoteAgentClient`) | Reply notification + unread (yellow) dot; send failure → failure notification + **red** failure dot → read-only popover glance |
| Watch ControlWidget headless | Always on suspended delivery (+haptic); TAP deep-links into the reply's thread (`userInfo.conversationID` → `WatchReplyDeepLinkCoordinator`); failure carries no id → no deep-link |
| Watch Apple-relay drain (`AppleRelayPendingQueue`) | Foreground: never. Suspended: native delivery (transcript or error) |
| Watch foreground (any) | Never (`WatchNotificationDelegate.willPresent` returns `[]`) — sessions short, no per-thread tracker |
| CarPlay | Never (no post site). Connected failure spoken via TTS; turn persisted for in-thread retry (see Per-Surface → Apple CarPlay) |
| Pending-retry (OS-killed `ConverseIntent.perform()`) | Always — `PendingRetryGuard` 90s `UNTimeIntervalNotificationTrigger`; never armed by in-app capture |

**Reply title.** `title` = bound gateway's `RemoteAgentRefMetadata.displayName` when `configuredRemoteAgentRefs().count >= 2` (who answered, before opening), else generic; body = the ≤200-char reply. **Resolve `displayName` from the RAW ref string** (`Conversation.backend` via `fetchConversation`, falling back to `backendRawValue`), NOT the recovered `RemoteAgentBackend` enum — that collapses every custom to `.openclaw`.

**Delivery-time gate.** `NotificationPresentationDecider.shouldSuppress(userInfo:viewedConversationIDs:)` — pure, module-internal in `ConduckApp.swift` (module-internal so the test target reaches it). Falls back to `present` on any missing/malformed payload — banner is the safer default than silent-drop. Driven by `NotificationDelegate` (one delegate class; the macOS and iOS `App` structs each hold their own instance, one branch compiles per build). `@MainActor`; `nonisolated` methods hop via `Task { @MainActor }` to read `ActiveViewTracker.viewedConversationIDs` (`@MainActor enum`, `Set<UUID>` — not optional, so iPad multi-scene + macOS multi-window track concurrently) fed by `.onAppear`/`.onDisappear` on the single `ConversationThreadView` host. The menu-bar popover hosts `DictationPopoverView`, not `ConversationThreadView`, so it uses `MenuBarCoordinator.popoverVisibleConversationID` for its cue instead. macOS posts no reply notification, so this gate is effectively iOS/Watch.

**iOS notification-tap auto-speak.** `NotificationDelegate.didReceive` re-posts `conversationIDKey` on the in-app deep-link bus to foreground+open the thread (iOS/macOS). On iOS/iPadOS only it ALSO auto-speaks the reply when `ReplyAutoSpeakDecider.conversationIDForAutoSpeak` returns non-nil: requires the request identifier to carry `replyIdentifierPrefix` (`"remoteAgent.reply."`), the `conversationIDKey` to parse, the device-local `speakReplyOnNotificationOpenAtTap()` toggle, AND `!CarPlayRecordingService.anySessionActive` (a live CarPlay route would be fought mid-drive). **Prefix test is load-bearing:** failure notifications (`remoteAgent.failure.<uuid>`) also carry `conversationIDKey` for tap-to-retry — without the prefix gate, tapping a failure would speak a stale prior reply. Stage the auto-speak request BEFORE the deep-link post (a host already mounted on this thread reacts on the same main-thread turn → set-then-post kills the consume-before-set race).

**macOS reply cue (menu-bar unread dot).** Both in-app success branches post `.conversationReplyArrived`; shares + any background-landed reply post `.remoteAgentTurnDidComplete`. The two are mutually exclusive per turn (in-app composer appends in foreground; share drain lands via `recordReply` — foreground `RemoteAgentClient` on macOS, background delegate on iOS). `MenuBarCoordinator` observes both → `noteReplyArrived(id)`, inserting into `unreadReplyConversationIDs`. **`.remoteAgentTurnDidComplete` fires from `recordReply` AFTER persistence — never from the drainer's success branch** (its awaiter resumes before persistence and would miss the iOS relaunch-reconcile completion; `recordReply` returns false on append failure → no post). `unreadReplyConversationIDs` is `private(set) var … Set<UUID>` and **MUST be STORED, not a computed view** over `unreadOrder` — a share reply flips only this property, and `withObservationTracking` does not reliably register a dependency on a computed view, so the dot wouldn't repaint. `MenuBarController` renders ONE brand-colored dot in the idle state only; precedence recording → processing → error → awaiting → unread-idle. Why a dot not a notification: a menu-bar app's own surface fits the async reply and saves the macOS notification permission (see Onboarding). **Window-visible pin:** `windowVisibleConversationID` mirrors the popover pin for the main window — fed by `MainWindowView`'s `WindowThreadVisibilityReporter` on the thread mount (INSIDE the `.id` boundary, so a sidebar switch remounts it per thread), gated on `\.appearsActive` so a thread mounted in a backgrounded/miniaturized window reports nothing (a reply landing there still raises the dot — the cue exists for replies the user isn't watching; this is also why `ActiveViewTracker` can't drive it: it counts background mounts). Deactivation drops the pin; unmount clears via `clearWindowVisibleConversation(ifCurrent:)` (the new thread's reporter can mount before the old one's `.onDisappear` — an unconditional nil would wipe the fresh pin). Re-activation re-reports and clears the thread's marks, so cmd-tabbing back onto a lit thread settles its dot.

**macOS send-failure cue (RED dot).** `MenuBarCoordinator` observes `.remoteAgentTurnDidFail` (carries `conversationID`) → `noteFailure(id)` into `failedConversationIDs` — STORED for the same Observation-repaint reason; ignores empty/absent id (a no-turn failure has no thread). Semantics = unviewed failure; idle precedence is **red over yellow** (failure outranks unread). `noteReplyArrived`/`noteFailure` skip `id == popoverVisibleConversationID` AND `id == windowVisibleConversationID` (either way the thread is on screen in front of the user); `clearUnread`/`clearFailure` sit together at every clear site (popover-show, window-thread-activation, reply-settles, `openConversation`) — clear-site parity prevents a stuck dot.

**macOS shared-reply glance (display override).** A share dot click opens the popover on `mostRecentUnreadConversationID`; when that thread isn't the quick-capture thread, the controller sets a read-only display override (`displayedPopoverViewModel = override ?? quickViewModel`) BEFORE showing, so the popover shows the shared thread WITHOUT mutating quick-capture state. Override suppresses the compose surface (it targets the quick lane — a different thread); long-form continuation routes to the window via `onOpenWindow`. Cleared on popover close and on any new capture (`armQuickCapture`) so fresh captures start in the quick lane. The popover is a GLANCE surface: a long/fenced-code reply (`ReplyLengthClassifier.isLong`) routes to the full window via `onOpenWindow`; Copy + Speak act on the full reply text.

**iOS/background failure notification.** `BackgroundRemoteAgent` posts a failure notification (generic title, identifier `remoteAgent.failure.<uuid>`) AND `.remoteAgentTurnDidFail` carrying `conversationID` — feeds the macOS red dot and supports tap-to-retry deep-link into the failed turn.

**Retry nudge is headless-only by construction.** `PendingRetryGuard.arm` is called ONLY from `ConverseIntent`; in-app recorders do NOT arm it (in-app failures are covered by the in-app retry card). The deferred notification fires only for the genuinely-headless case where the app is never opened.

---

## Audio Pipeline

| Surface | Capture | Cap |
|---|---|---|
| iPhone / iPad (entry 1) | Apple `Record Audio` Shortcut action (M4A) | `Constants.maxAudioDuration = 300` |
| iPhone / iPad (entry 2) | `AudioRecorder` (`AVAudioRecorder`, M4A AAC) | 300 s |
| macOS | `AudioRecorder` (`AVAudioRecorder`, M4A AAC) | 300 s |
| CarPlay | `AVAudioEngine` + `installTap` + service-owned `AVAudioConverter` (16 kHz Float32 → `AVAudioFile`) → post-record compress | 300 s |
| Watch | `AVAudioRecorder` (48 kHz mono M4A; the active mic session keeps the app alive on wrist-drop) | 300 s |

A soft warning fires `Constants.maxAudioDurationWarningOffset` (=60 s) before the cap.

**`AudioCompressor`** (unified iOS/macOS, no platform branch, never throws): AAC (M4A, 16 kHz mono) → on AAC-≥-original or encode failure, WAV fallback (`compressToWAV`, 16 kHz mono 16-bit PCM) → `.original` if the WAV is also ≥ original (already-compressed input). `CompressionResult.format ∈ {aac, wav, original}`.

Audio never persisted where Conduck controls — in-memory capture → STT provider → discard; every cleanup site uses `defer`.

**Read-aloud session ownership** (table above is CAPTURE; playback differs): spoken replies play on the *caller's* `AVAudioSession` — `CarPlayAudioSession` (`.playAndRecord`/`.voiceChat`, held per-session) on CarPlay, `ChatPlaybackSession` (`.playback`/`.spokenAudio`/`.duckOthers`, per-utterance, skipped while CarPlay is active) on the iOS chat path; macOS has none. `ReplyVoice`/`SpeechPlayer` never touch the session (see Text-to-Speech).

---

## Settings & Storage

### Keys

Provider-agnostic naming so V1.x extends additively. `SettingsManager` is an actor singleton: dual-write App Groups + iCloud KVS for cross-device settings (read `defaults` FIRST — KVS-only would silently drop the write + revert the selection when iCloud is unavailable), Keychain for secrets.

| Setting | Storage | Default |
|---|---|---|
| Active STT preset ID | App Groups + KVS `stt.activePresetID` (dual-write, read-defaults-first) | `apple-on-device` |
| Per-preset API key | Keychain `stt.apiKey.<presetID>`, synchronizable. **Cloud TTS reads this SAME slot** — one key per vendor, both directions, zero new slots (see STT Provider Registry) | none |
| Active TTS provider ID | App Groups + KVS `tts.activeProviderID` — independent of the STT pointer (picked separately) | `apple-tts` |
| Per-provider TTS voice / model override | KVS `tts.voice.<id>` / `tts.customModel.<id>` (non-secret; empty/absent → the provider's pinned default; model override = the 5 frozen cloud providers only, Apple + custom excluded) | nil → default |
| Custom-voice-endpoint roster | App Groups + KVS `stt.customVoiceEndpoints` — JSON `[{id,name}]`; cap `Constants.maxCustomVoiceEndpoints` (=5) | `[]` |
| Per-endpoint custom-voice config (`<uuid>`) | url `stt.custom.url.<uuid>` (KVS) · key `stt.apiKey.custom-openai_<uuid>` (Keychain, synchronizable, shared STT+TTS) · cert `stt.custom.certFingerprint.<uuid>` (App-Group local, per-device — NOT KVS) · auth `stt.custom.authScheme.<uuid>` (`bearer`/`none`) · STT model `stt.custom.model.<uuid>` · TTS model `tts.custom.model.<uuid>` (required, default `tts-1`); `https://` only | none |
| Preferred language | KVS `stt.preferredLanguage` (optional ISO 639-1 hint) | nil → auto-detect |
| Per-ref gateway URL | App Groups + KVS `remoteAgent.url.<ref>` (non-secret; `https://` only) | none |
| Per-ref bearer token | Keychain `remoteAgent.token.<ref>`, synchronizable + `AfterFirstUnlock`; Watch via `WCSession` (non-sync); NOT KVS/UserDefaults. Optional under `.none`, required under `.bearer` | none |
| Per-ref auth scheme | App Groups + KVS `remoteAgent.authScheme.<ref>` (`bearer`/`none`); default `bearer` — fail closed (see Architectural Invariants). Rides each Watch broadcast sub-envelope (always-encoded; decode defaults `bearer`). **Watch goes keyless ONLY on an explicit `.none`** — a `.bearer` envelope with a nil token PRESERVES the stored Watch token (a transient iPhone Keychain read failure must not delete it) | `bearer` |
| Per-ref TLS fingerprint | App Groups `remoteAgent.certFingerprint.<ref>` (SHA-256 hex; per-device — App Groups only, NOT KVS) | none |
| Per-ref model | App Groups + KVS `remoteAgent.model.<ref>` (non-secret). Written ONLY by a hosted-model built-in whose descriptor `model == .required` (OpenRouter); OpenClaw/Hermes never write it (`.unsupported`); customs keep their model on the roster. Rides the Watch envelope | none |
| Custom-gateway roster | App Groups + KVS `remoteAgent.customGateways` — JSON `[{id,name,model?,colorID?,monogram?}]`; cap `Constants.maxCustomGateways` (=5) | `[]` |
| Default-backend pointer | **DEVICE-LOCAL — App Groups ONLY** `remoteAgent.defaultBackend` (NOT KVS — each device owns its default; gateway *configs* sync, the *choice* is per-device). Stores a `RemoteAgentRef.rawString`; selects the ref new headless/CarPlay-seed/Watch-if-following conversations stamp. No KVS read fallback, no `handleiCloudChange` mirror — a late KVS write from another device is IGNORED (never re-globalizes). Orphan → falls back to a **built-in**, never another custom. First-gateway bootstrap (`saveRemoteAgent`) writes it, category-agnostic (incl. OpenRouter); a sync-only device with no local default lazily adopts the first configured ref (`defaultRemoteAgentRef()`). NEVER clears the active pointer; NEVER gates on `kvsSchemaVersion` | first configured ref |
| Apple Watch overrides | App Groups ONLY, iPhone-written — **NEVER KVS** (must not leak to iPad/Mac): default gateway `remoteAgent.watchDefaultBackend` + session policy `watch.sessionContinuationPolicyOverride`; each a `rawString`/`rawValue`, or ABSENT = "Follow iPhone". The default-override self-heals a dangling ref (`deleteCustomGateway` clears it eagerly); both ride the broadcast envelope (`defaultBackendRef` / `sessionPolicy`) | nil → Follow iPhone |
| Session continuation policy | App Groups `remoteAgent.sessionPolicy` (`SessionContinuationPolicy`); **PER-DEVICE — NEVER KVS** (see Active-conversation pointer lifecycle); Watch follows via the broadcast envelope's `sessionPolicy` slot | `.minutes30` |
| Spoken-Replies toggles | All device-local App-Group: `speakReplyOnNotificationOpen` (iOS notif-open) · `speakQuickLaneReplies` (macOS quick-lane) · `watch.readRepliesAloud` (Watch) = **WCSession-primary** (Watch persists to its App-Group mirror, reads that FIRST) + iOS-KVS cold-launch fallback + on the settings payload; reads `object(forKey:) as? Bool ?? false`, never bare `bool(forKey:)` | `false` |
| Menu-bar input mode (macOS) | App Groups ONLY `menuBarInputMode` (`voice`/`text`; device-local per-machine ergonomic — NEVER KVS) | `.voice` |
| Cold-launch landing mode | App Groups + KVS `remoteAgent.onLaunchMode` (`OnLaunchMode`); iPhone/iPadOS + macOS unified window only | `.startNewConversation` |
| Active-conversation pointer | App-Group ONLY `remoteAgent.activeConversationID` + `remoteAgent.activeConversationActivity`; **PER-DEVICE** — never KVS/synced/on-the-wire; Watch keeps its own App-Group copy under the same literals (see Active-conversation pointer lifecycle) | none |
| Misc | `kvsSchemaVersion` (KVS, diagnostic-only — MUST NOT gate behavior) · onboarding-complete + `device_has_been_used` (gates `UserIdentityManager` iCloud-adoption) → App Groups, NOT `.standard` · device-local UI state → plain `UserDefaults` | — |

**Keychain.** `kSecAttrSynchronizable: true` on every secret read/write/delete/enumerate; `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlock` (Watch ControlWidget pre-unlock requirement; also the only sync-compatible accessibility — `…ThisDeviceOnly` is rejected for synchronizable items). `ensureKeychainMigrated()` (top of every secret read, flag-guarded) does a one-time local→synchronizable copy, self-triggering across processes (Shortcuts/CarPlay), not just app launch.

**Per-ref accessors.** Canonical accessors take a `RemoteAgentRef` (URL/token/cert getters+setters, `remoteAgentSnapshot(for:)`); `configuredRemoteAgentRefs()` orders built-ins first (`allCases`) then customs (registry order) for stable picker tags; `defaultRemoteAgentRef()` / `setDefaultRemoteAgentRef(_:)` reuse the `remoteAgent.defaultBackend` pointer (the setter clears the active-conversation pointer on an actual change — see Active-conversation pointer lifecycle). `remoteAgentSnapshot(forConversationBackend:)` is the per-conversation router (parses `RemoteAgentRef(rawString:)`; a deleted custom whose registry entry is gone → nil). **`RemoteAgentBackend` raw values + the `custom_` prefix are LOCKED into the key suffixes above — renaming orphans every stored URL/token/cert/pointer.**

**Registry CRUD** (actor-serialized). Custom-gateway + custom-voice-endpoint CRUD cap at their max on ADD (returns `false` when full; an UPDATE to an existing id always succeeds); delete clears all per-ref/per-uuid slots + the roster entry. A deleted gateway's default-pointer orphan falls back to a built-in; a deleted voice endpoint's active STT/TTS pointer falls back to **Apple** (`apple-on-device`/`apple-tts` — stricter, the safe keyless default). One-time legacy migrations (`migrateRemoteAgentToPerBackend`, `migrateCustomVoiceEndpoint`, `migrateDefaultBackendToDeviceLocal`) promote single-slot configs to the per-ref/roster slots — each flag-guarded + in-process-latched, runs AFTER `ensureKeychainMigrated()`, retains legacy keys, and sets its flag only after the token copy confirms (a locked-Keychain partial pass retries on the next access). **Tolerant decoder:** reader ignores unknown KVS keys; writer writes only known schema fields; backward-incompatible changes need explicit migration code.

### Settings surface

Platform shells, capability-grouped content, ONE shared model:

- **Shells.** iPhone = master-detail full-screen sheet (`SettingsView`). iPad = full-screen sidebar+detail (`IpadSettingsView` via `.fullScreenCover` from `ContentView`'s **regular-width** branch — compact-width iPad falls back to the iPhone sheet, sidestepping `NavigationSplitView` collapse), REUSING the iOS sub-screens each wrapped in its OWN `NavigationStack` so iPad↔iPhone never fork. macOS = full-window mode swap (`MacSettingsView`) — REPLACES the conversation split (NOT a sheet, NOT a separate window); an `HStack` of a category `List(selection:)` + detail (NOT `NavigationSplitView` — the stable 4-item sidebar needs no column-collapse). Category parity iPad↔macOS: **General · Personal AI · Voice · Diagnostics · About** (Support folded into About). Visible from first launch. `SettingsViewModel`'s remote-agent state is **per-ref dicts** (`[RemoteAgentRef: T]`), ONE instance shared from the window.
- **Voice category.** Capability-first, two sections — **Voice Setup** (STT + TTS active-provider selectors + a Providers & Keys drill-in to the provider LIBRARY) + **Spoken Replies** (notification-open toggle); macOS keeps ONLY Voice Setup (its quick-lane toggle lives in General → Menu Bar). `VoiceVendor`/`VoiceVendorRegistry` is a dynamic UI-grouping layer over the STT + TTS registries (vendor `id` is UI-only, NOT a wire id; `sharedKeychainAccount` resolves the existing STT slot; one `VoiceVendor` per `CustomVoiceEndpoint`). The selector chooser is the SINGLE activation surface (tap a configured vendor → activate; "Set up…" deep-links an unconfigured one, never silent-activates). The global Language hint nests under the STT chooser (`Why:` a global STT setting on the root read as fragmentation). The per-vendor detail is PURE CONFIG, no activation rows — one shared key field + symmetric STT/TTS sections (recommended-model line → test action → Advanced model disclosure; TTS's Advanced additionally holds the voice field). Apple TTS = the system voice (no model/voice fields). The on-device Apple STT side diverges: keyless, a **Speech Recognition** section (see Apple On-Device STT). A custom endpoint opens `CustomSTTConfigBody` (required Name field). The provider library closes with a collapsed **About reliability** disclosure (`VoiceReliabilityDisclosure`, ONE subview shared iOS+macOS so the copy can't drift) — deliberately EVERGREEN copy (no provider/tier claims; with zero telemetry a specific limit claim would rot undetectably): chunked audio fetches exist for latency, provider rate limits (commonest on free tiers) are why a long reply can switch to the built-in voice mid-way, the provider's usage tier is the user-side fix, and replies always finish via the fallback.
- **Personal AI screen** (iOS `PersonalAISettingsView` · macOS `MacPersonalAICategory`) — a connection manager that renders the SAME populated layout regardless of whether any gateway is configured (gated only on `hasLoadedRemoteAgentState` so nothing flashes pre-load), since a sparse empty-state read as a lesser screen. Section order: **(1)** New-chats-use default selector (the SINGLE place to pre-pick — routing stays per-conversation, this is only the pre-pick; reads **"Not configured"** via `defaultSelectorDisplayName` when nothing is configured, never a phantom builtin default) · **(2)** Guided setup row (`PersonalAIConnectRows`) → `GuidedGatewaySetupView` (primer for a first-timer, else the lane chooser). Its prominence is CONDITIONAL — `emphasized` = `hasLoadedRemoteAgentState && configuredRemoteAgentRefSet.isEmpty` (the hydration flag is load-bearing: the set is empty pre-load, so without it a configured user flashes emphasized). Emphasized = an `AppColors.guidedSetupBlue`-tinted row card (a cool card among warm-grey ones) + subtitle + chevron + ONE sheen sweep on appear; once a gateway exists it relaxes to a calm blue row — `Why:` the row is PERMANENT, and a glinting CTA nagging a user who already connected reads as a banner, not an affordance. The sweep NEVER loops and lives in the row BACKGROUND (not masked over the label), so it cannot touch hit-testing or VoiceOver; it is suppressed under Reduce Motion, so the static treatment must carry the emphasis alone. `guidedSetupBlue` is an EXPLICIT value, not `.tint` — the app ships an empty `AccentColor` asset, so `.tint` follows the USER's System-Settings accent on macOS (a pink-accent Mac would get a pink row, losing the one cool-toned affordance that makes this read as the front door). Scoped to this row; nothing else adopts it. · **(3)** Full agent gateways (self-hosted built-ins) · **(4)** Hosted model (OpenRouter; hide-if-empty; never the auto-default) · **(5)** Custom gateways + Add-custom · **(6)** "Couldn't send" share-recovery (lives under Personal AI on both platforms). Discrete rows: name + check when configured, dimmed when not; whole-row tap = configure. Add-custom mints a draft `CustomGateway`; at the cap it stays **visible-but-disabled**; backing out of an empty unsaved draft discards silently. Startup + quick-capture continuation live in the iOS/macOS **General** category.
- **`RemoteAgentConfigBody`** (per ref, cross-platform) — ONE unified capability-driven template (`editorSections`), IDENTICAL empty or filled; persisted state (`isRemoteAgentConfigured`) drives only BEHAVIOR (status text, Forget availability, file-lane tap-gate, post-save prompt), never section STRUCTURE. The bearer-token row is a tap-in opening `SecretEntrySheet` (NO inline `SecureField` — the macOS recursion trap); what it captures is a `StagedRemoteAgentToken` INTENT (`.stored`/`.typed`/`.reuseVoiceKey`), never a raw key held by the View — resolved to the actual credential VM-side at Save/Test time (OpenRouter voice-key reuse = COPY semantics from the Keychain, never surfaced). Sections: **Quick connect** (SELF-HOSTED ONLY — omitted for the hosted-model OpenRouter) deep-links the shared guided cover to this ref; **Connection** (URL hidden when `endpoint == .fixed`; auth toggle hidden when `authentication == .locked`; secret; Test); **Model** — its OWN section, shown only where the model field applies, placed BELOW Test Connection (the action that POPULATES its `/v1/models` suggestion catalog) with a save-blocker hint when `requiresModel && empty`; **In chats** (image history); **Devices** (file-transfer nav row, saved-only, plus badge where applicable); Forget/Delete. OpenRouter's "Get an API key" link sits in the Connection footer. Built-in policies come from the `RemoteAgentBackendMetadata` descriptor (no scattered `id == .openrouter` checks). **Item B — `.systemTrustOnly` clears the pin:** `saveRemoteAgent`/`validateRemoteAgent` force `effectiveFingerprint = nil` (hiding the field is NOT clearing it; a stale buffer pin would arm a future `remoteAgentCertMismatch`). Test Connection → `GET descriptor.verdictProbePath` (`/v1/models` self-hosted/custom, auth-gated; OpenRouter → `/v1/key`, whose 401-on-bad-key validates the key its public `/v1/models` can't — see Architectural Invariants; not HEAD — see Remote Agent Round-Trip). TOFU "Trust & Save" banner renders on iOS AND macOS whenever a per-ref untrusted cert is pending. Save + Cancel in the shared `bufferedEditorChrome` top bar (single commit point; Test is validate-only); at rest renders NO status (a bare `.valid` only means "saved"). `rebaselineOriginals()` (re-snapshots URL/name/model/cert AND `originalAuthKeyless`) on appear/save/import/forget. Gateway names retain the 40-character display cap; model identifiers are trimmed around the edges but otherwise stored in full because they are opaque server-owned values (KVS's documented storage limits are far above this field). **Post-save "Make default?" prompt** via the pure `shouldPromptToSetDefault(…)`: the FIRST gateway bootstraps the default silently; a genuinely NEW non-default gateway prompts; editing an already-configured one never prompts. Make Default → `setDefaultRemoteAgentRef` (re-points + clears the active pointer).
- **File-transfer page** (`GatewayFileTransferPage`, pushed from the editor's File-transfer nav row; every file-capable gateway, the row hidden for OpenRouter/`.unsupported`) is reachable **ONLY for a SAVED gateway** — the editor gates the nav row on `isRemoteAgentConfigured`, so the page itself never needs a save-first state (`Why:` a draft custom's unsaved ref would orphan file config that Cancel can't clear). Status via `fileLaneStatus(for:) -> GatewayFileLaneStatus`: `.ready` · `.needsAttention` (a tested-and-FAILED lane — `.savedNeedsTest` AND a failed staged `fileTransferTestResults` entry; `fileTransferSetupState` alone can't distinguish failed from never-tested, so the failure signal is the staged test result — NOT `fileServerValidationStates`, which only carries the URL-save verdict) · `.saved` · `.recommended` (self-hosted full agent) · `.optional` (file-capable custom) · `.unsupported` (hidden). Setup surface = the shared `FileTransferSetupContent` (intent-gated credential generate/regenerate [hidden at `.ready`; instant-commit to Keychain — mint publishes/reveals ONLY after the write proves out] + URL + an **Advanced** disclosure holding an optional **Pinned cert fingerprint** field [persists to `fileServerCertFingerprints`; same normalization as the QR path — lowercased, colon-stripped, `^[0-9a-f]{64}$`-validated] + staged Test Connection + Forget) — a **buffered editor under the shared `bufferedEditorChrome`** (the gateway editor's exact commit contract: Test Connection probes the DRAFT URL/pin + stored credential WITHOUT persisting; trailing **Save** is the single commit; Cancel/back with unsaved URL/pin edits confirms discard; the credential is deliberately OUTSIDE dirty detection). Verdicts are **signature-keyed** (`FileTransferTestSignature` = canonical URL + normalized pin + per-ref credential **generation** counter): a staged verdict displays / carries into Save only while the draft matches the tuple it probed (edit → goes dark; restore exact tuple → legitimately returns; rotation/import bumps the generation → dead), and a probe landing after the draft changed is discarded (stale-result rejection). Availability moves ONLY when the probed tuple IS the persisted tuple (pristine re-test: pass↔fail applies immediately — a Ready lane never dips mid-probe, which is what keeps the layout from flickering) or at Save when a passing verdict matches the committed tuple (Test→Save lands Ready with no second probe); the tuple+readiness transition commits via `SettingsManager.commitFileTransferConfig`/`commitFileTransferVerdict` — ONE actor hop, no suspension points, one notification — so a concurrent `fileTransferSnapshot` can never observe Ready over a mixed half-written tuple (and a Save/re-test costs one settings-changed reload, not one per setter). `fileLaneStatus`'s `.needsAttention` is signature-gated to the PERSISTED tuple — a failed probe of an edited draft never paints the saved lane red. Dirty detection runs against the VM's persisted mirrors (`fileServerPersistedURLStrings`/`fileServerPersistedPins`); `cancelFileTransferEdit` reverts buffers to them and retires an abandoned DRAFT verdict (a persisted-matching one survives). Hosted by `GatewayFileTransferPage` (the Settings-side home) and presented by the composer `.needsSetup` tile via the `FileTransferSetupGuideView` sheet (`context: .composer` — adds the escape hint, `.interactiveDismissDisabled` while dirty, and auto-dismiss gated on **ready AND clean**, observed as one combined value so external readiness can never eat unsaved edits). The editor's nav-row badge and the page's status both read `GatewayFileLaneStatus`'s shared display extension (`shortLabel`/`meaning`/`tint`), so the two surfaces can't drift. Covered by `GatewayFileLaneStatusTests` + `FileTransferDraftVerdictTests`.
- **Unsaved-edits guard.** `bufferedEditorChrome` consumers (`RemoteAgentConfigBody` + `CustomSTTConfigBody`) mirror `isDirty` onto a shared `editorHasUnsavedChanges` (re-asserted on `.onAppear` so a File-Transfer child-push round-trip can't lapse it); every outer exit routes through ONE discard confirm (macOS Done/Esc + sidebar category switch via a vetoing selection `Binding`; iOS `.interactiveDismissDisabled`). The flag ALSO fences `.settingsDidChangeRemotely → loadSettings()` (deferred via `pendingRemoteReload`) — `Why:` a reload rebuilds the URL/model/cert buffers the fields bind to, so a write-on-change control or a remote KVS sync would clobber live typing mid-edit.
- **Per-conversation picker** (`ContentView`/`ConversationLibraryView`/`MainWindowView`) enumerates `configuredRemoteAgentRefs()`, keeps the "≥2 configured ⇒ show, new-chat only" gate, stamps `selectedRef.rawString` into `createConversation(backend:)`. Once a thread has turns the picker is gone; gateway identity persists in the nav title, and **Clone & continue is folded into that title** — clone-eligible (bound thread with turns, gateway available, AND ≥1 OTHER configured) gains a tap-to-open lock/clone sheet (clone-only — never re-bind). A gone/unconfigured bound gateway renders a "no longer available" banner + a Clone action. `ConversationStore.cloneConversation(id:toBackend:)` copies the text turns into a fresh conversation bound to the chosen ref, leaving the original a read-only archive — clone, never re-bind, so a different agent is never handed a history it didn't produce.
- **Startup** (global, iPhone/iPadOS + macOS unified window): On-launch picker — Start-new (default) vs Resume-last, resolved one-shot at cold launch by `resolveInitialConversationID`/`resolveActiveConversationOnLaunch` (display-only "what to SHOW" via the TTL resolver + most-recently-active fallback, WITHOUT the gateway re-check; NOT the quick-capture send resolver).
- **Attachments** (global): NO user knob. Inline vision = a fixed `1568` px long-edge EXIF/GPS-stripped JPEG at `q=0.7` (vision-tile sweet spot, cost is pixel-based); file-transfer uploads the original raw bytes. No share auto-send toggle (shared content is ALWAYS sent on the Share-Extension's picked target — the OFF position would need a non-existent composer-draft staging path).
- **Quick-capture continuation policy** (iOS/macOS General): "Add to last conversation" over the 5-way `SessionContinuationPolicy` (default 30 min); display polarity is INVERTED from the case names (`Always`=`alwaysContinue` … `Never`=`alwaysNew`; menu lists `allCases.reversed()`). PER-DEVICE App-Group-local, NO KVS write; the Watch follows the iPhone (or an iPhone-set Watch override) via the broadcast envelope's `sessionPolicy` slot. The Watch's own "Follow iPhone"-first row lives in the iPhone-hosted `WatchSettingsView`.
- **Apple Watch** (Settings → Setup row, **always shown** — Watch support is unconditionally discoverable whether or not a Watch is paired; iPhone-only): pushes `WatchSettingsView` (Watch default-gateway picker + Set up Apple Watch). The Watch is on by default; the `watch.enabled` KVS flag + `WatchSettingsReader.isWatchEnabled()` gate are retained with NO iOS toggle UI.
- **About → Open Source Licenses.** ONE shared `LicensesView` (iPhone push from `AboutDetailView` · iPad push from `AboutPane` · macOS sheet from `MacAboutCategory`): three rows (Conduck license / NOTICE / third-party notices) each opening the full text as selectable monospaced plain text, lazily loaded from `Bundle.main` on appear with a visible could-not-load fallback (never a crash). Backing resources = byte-identical copies of the repo-root `LICENSE` / `NOTICE` / `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` bundled from `Resources/Legal/` (synced-folder auto-membership — no pbxproj entry); `LegalNoticesResourceTests` pins each copy byte-identical to its root original AND every `Package.resolved` pin present in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. `Why:` Apache-2.0 §4 + MIT notice preservation for the bundled packages and the vendored Silero model — an obligation of every distributed build, so the screen ships in Community and Official builds alike.
- **Reset-onboarding** MUST write to `UserDefaults(suiteName: Constants.appGroupID)`, NOT `.standard` — `SettingsManager` reads/writes the App-Groups suite; a reset against `.standard` is a silent no-op.

**Setup recipes** (per-backend links) each cover (a) enable the OpenAI HTTP endpoint, disabled by default (OpenClaw `gateway.http.endpoints.chatCompletions.enabled=true`; Hermes `API_SERVER_ENABLED=true`); (b) set a bearer token or run keyless (`Authentication: None` on a private/restricted network where the network IS the auth); (c) expose over HTTPS (Tailscale Funnel / Cloudflare Tunnel / LAN + self-signed-pin); (d) `conduck-connect.sh` automates a–c + emits the pairing payload (see Gateway Setup & Pairing); (e) a SELF-BUILT agent with no HTTP server follows the **adapter contract** (`Constants.adapterContractURL` → conduck.com/setup/adapter/v1/, raw mirror `/setup/adapter/v1.md`) — the normative v1 spec an AI coding tool implements to put a minimal OpenAI-compatible front door (two routes, bearer, loopback-only; TLS/exposure stays with `conduck-connect`) in front of the user's agent; the guided custom lane's `adapter` step copies a brief that delegates to the hosted build brief (`/setup/adapter/build.md`, narrowed to stop before exposure/pairing — the app owns those) with a self-contained contract-aligned fallback, and links the human build guide (`Constants.adapterBuildGuideURL` → `/setup/adapter/build/`) beside the contract.

### Active-conversation pointer lifecycle

The pointer (`remoteAgent.activeConversationID` + `remoteAgent.activeConversationActivity`) selects which `Conversation` the next HEADLESS quick capture appends to. **PER-DEVICE + IMPLICIT-ONLY:** App-Group UserDefaults only — never KVS, never synced, never sent upstream. Each device keeps its own quick-capture thread; the Watch keeps its own App-Group copy under the same key literals (disjoint stores). `Why per-device:` a Mac hotkey capture must not retarget where the next wrist/Action-Button capture lands.

- **Governed by `SessionContinuationPolicy`** (per-device; the Watch receives the Watch-effective value via the broadcast envelope's `sessionPolicy` slot). Both `SettingsManager.resolveActiveConversationID(now:)` (iOS/macOS) and `WatchSettingsReader.resolveActiveConversationID` (watchOS) read their own storage then delegate to the shared pure `SessionContinuationPolicy.resolvedConversationID(id:lastActivity:now:)` (single source of truth — the enum is a member of both targets, so the two surfaces can't drift): `alwaysNew` → nil (mint fresh); `alwaysContinue` → the id; timed → continue iff `max(0, now − lastActivityAt) < ttlSeconds`. **Branch, never `elapsed < ttl` arithmetic for the extremes** — a backward clock jump makes `elapsed` negative and `< 0` would flip `alwaysNew` to "continue" (`ttlSeconds` is `nil`, not a 0/∞ sentinel, for the two extremes).
- **Resolve-time default-gateway re-check.** Quick captures resolve through `SharedInboxRouting.resolveQuickCaptureConversation(now:settings:store:)` — TTL resolve, then the fetched record must be bound to the CURRENT default gateway, else nil → mint fresh on the default (`Why:` the default may have been re-pointed since the stamp; an implicit capture must follow the default, never a stale binding). The Watch mirrors the guard inline. **Display-only launch reads skip the gateway re-check** (they pick what to SHOW, not where a capture sends).
- **WRITERS (implicit captures only):** iOS `ConverseIntent`; macOS quick-lane sends (`stampsQuickPointer: true`; `retry()` inherits provenance via the in-memory `quickStampMessageIDs`, so a stale retry downgrades FAIL-SAFE to never-stamp); Watch headless/Ask-hint/mint paths. **NEVER stamp:** in-app/window threads, notification-tap opens, Share-Extension drains (read-only landing), CarPlay (own session pin), pinned Watch composer turns, macOS `explicitConversation`. `Why:` browsing, sharing, and explicit picks must not retarget where the next quick capture lands.
- **Consulting surfaces:** iOS Action Button, Watch headless triggers (ControlWidget/Action Button), macOS menu-bar popover/hotkey (re-resolves per capture, guarded by the quick VM's in-flight flag). In-app composers append to the visible thread (ignore the policy). **Watch in-app "Ask" = ALWAYS new (policy-bypass)** — a deliberate-start surface mints a fresh conversation every tap.
- **CarPlay is fully isolated** (neither reads nor writes the pointer): its session pins the per-session in-memory `CarPlayRecordingService.sessionConversationID` (one thread across all turns even under `alwaysNew`); a post-drive phone capture is UNAFFECTED (continues a TTL-fresh pre-drive quick thread else mints — the drive never feeds the quick lane).
- Successful implicit turn → re-stamp `lastActivityAt = now`. `clearActiveConversation()` callers: Forget gateway / URL change (gated, below), default-gateway re-point, `deleteConversation(id:)` (compare-then-clear: only when the deleted row IS the pointer) / `deleteAll()` (unconditional) — both `#if !os(watchOS)`. The macOS popover's "New chat" is NOT a clear — `.explicitNew` mints fresh and STAMPS it.
- **ONE pointer per device** (not per-backend); a recovered conversation's backend comes from `Conversation.backend`.
- **Per-backend URL change OR Forget gateway → clear the local pointer ONLY when the active conversation is bound to THAT backend.** A stored history was built for one agent/host; switching the host mid-thread would hand a changed agent a thread it never saw — but a change to an *unrelated* backend must not disturb the active thread.
