A token-only / auth-only change does NOT clear (host unchanged; `saveRemoteAgent` gates the clear on `urlChanged`). On URL save: auto-re-run Test Connection. **Do not block Settings save during an in-flight turn** — the turn holds its history/URL/token in memory; the clear applies to the NEXT turn.
- **Default-gateway re-point → clear the local pointer UNCONDITIONALLY (LOCAL-ONLY).** `setDefaultRemoteAgentRef` clears THIS device's pointer inside the no-op-guarded setter → the next headless capture mints a fresh thread on the new gateway. Re-selecting the SAME default is a no-op. **No remote propagation** (the default is device-local). The CarPlay chooser is a session-local override and does NOT call this setter (see Per-Surface → CarPlay); the Watch clears its OWN pointer on accepting a couriered default change (see Cross-Device Sync). `Why:` without the local clear, a changed default appears not to take effect while a recent thread keeps continuing on the old gateway.

---

## Local Diagnostics

On-device health checklist folding every connection/voice/file/sync/capability check into one Settings screen — sections **Connection** (one row per gateway, each with its file-server lane nested beneath), **Voice**, **Capabilities**, **Sync** — **FOR THE USER** (a self-hoster debugging their own setup), never a support-intake funnel: no backend, no telemetry, nothing auto-sent. A one-line **summary verdict** sits above the Test-everything button: amber `attentionCount` ("N items need attention" — failed/warning rows + `activeVoiceSetup` warnings + `fileLanes.needsAttention` + the live Watch block's `attentionCount`) whenever anything visible is amber/red; green "Checks passed" ONLY once `checksSettledGreen` (sweep has run ∧ nothing in flight ∧ no `.running` row — config reads alone never mint a green); nothing while settling. Placement: iOS `SettingsView` push · iPad/macOS sidebar category (parity list above) · plus **Troubleshoot** deep-links from environmental-error surfaces app-wide (below). `DiagnosticsRunner` (`@MainActor @Observable`) seeds the `[DiagnosticCheck]` list a shared `DiagnosticsView`/`DiagnosticsContent` renders, talking to the service singletons via atomic snapshot accessors ONLY — never `SettingsViewModel` editor state.

**Tier contract (LOAD-BEARING — the privacy + cost guarantee).** `DiagnosticTier` gates when each probe may fire:
- `runAutoReads()` (screen open) — LOCAL reads ONLY (config-shape, permission STATUS mic/speech/notification via the status API never `request*`/`ensureRequested`/`headProbe`, `NWPathMonitor` one-shot, iCloud account status). Zero egress to user infra, zero billing, zero TCC prompts. **Two-phase (load-bearing — don't merge):** the fast config reads seed the full checklist + section visibility FIRST (layout locks; the network + iCloud rows seed `.running`), THEN the two slow probes (`NWPathMonitor` first delivery + CloudKit `refresh()`) settle those rows IN PLACE — `Why:` seeding everything only after the slow probes flashed an empty checklist and dropped the amber Copy button bottom-ward on first open. Run-once (`autoReadStarted` latch neutralizes the `.task`-on-`Group` multi-fire). The Mac/iPad Settings hosts own the runner (survives sidebar switches) and PRE-WARM it off-screen via a host-root `.task` — Settings always opens on a non-Diagnostics category, so the checklist populates invisibly and the first Diagnostics paint IS the final layout (`Why:` the phase-1 config reads still yield the main actor across their `await` hops, so an on-mount seed reflows the sections above the Copy button while visible; pre-warming moves that single reflow before the tab is shown).
- `runConnectionChecks()` (the SWEEP — barrier phase of the `runAllTests()` run below) — the ONLY place gateway reachability (`testConnection`, `descriptor.verdictProbePath`, per configured ref) + the cloud-STT auth probe + the per-gateway file-server reach/auth probe (`FileServerClient.probeReachability` — one NON-mutating ranged GET of an impossible key; updates each lane's `reachAuth`, NEVER `fileTransferAvailable`) fire; skips in-process Apple. All run in ONE `withTaskGroup` (`ConnectionProbeResult` sum type) and end with `reprobeConnectivity()`. `Why` behind a tap: a LAN/Tailscale gateway can raise the **Local Network** prompt and some STT auth probes POST (billable).
- `runAllTests()` (the single top **Test everything** button) — runs `runConnectionChecks()` as a BARRIER, THEN concurrently fans out the mutating file write test on EVERY configured lane (`lanesToWrite`) + the paid STT transcription + the **Watch health pull** (iOS, `diagnostics-pull` — free/non-billable/no prompt, fail-soft when the wrist is asleep; in the FAN-OUT not the barrier, because it needs no ordering against the file probes and a slow wrist must not hold the write tests hostage for its ~5 s budget — that budget alone also disqualifies it from `autoRead`; see the Watch row below); deliberately does NOT run `runVoicePreview` (voice PLAYBACK is a physical side effect — the device speaks aloud — so it stays its own tap). SILENT, and NO preflight gate: each callee SELF-STAGES (the write test re-runs reach→auth→write→read from scratch; the transcription's own error IS the diagnostic), so a "skip unless the sweep's reachAuth passed" gate would be redundant AND wrong (it would false-skip a keyless custom STT + a `.warning`/unconfirmed lane — the exact lane the write test resolves). Restamps `lastChecked` after the fan-out. `isBusy` (ANY test in flight — the full run OR a single per-row action) gates both the button `.disabled` and `runAllTests`'s own entry guard, so a full run can't race a manual row test and vice-versa; row buttons gate on `isRunningAllTests` only (two DIFFERENT per-row tests still run concurrently). The paid/audible row actions ALSO guard at the runner level against the sub-render double-tap window (a queued tap entering before the button re-renders disabled): transcription + the file write test on `!isTestingConnections` (sweep phase only — the run's own fan-out calls them with the flag cleared), preview on `!isRunningAllTests` (the run never calls it).
- `runTranscriptionTest` / `runVoicePreview` / `runFileTransferTest(for:)` — the individual billable/mutating tests, invoked BOTH by `runAllTests` (except `runVoicePreview`) and their own per-row buttons; each non-re-entrant (own in-flight flag, unconditional reset; the file test guards per-ref via `fileTransferTestRunning: Set<RemoteAgentRef>`, and additionally `!isTestingConnections` — a manual write can't start DURING the sweep phase, where both would write the lane's `reachAuth`). Transcription = provider-aware `STTClient.transcribe` (NOT the OpenAI-only `STTConnectionTestSuite`). Preview preflights the missing-key case (else `previewSample` silently Apple-falls-back → false pass). The staged PUT→GET→DELETE file test is the ONLY thing that sets a lane's `writeVerified` + persists `fileTransferAvailable` (a fail downgrades it), per gateway.
- `refreshConfig()` (`.settingsDidChangeRemotely` · deferred-to by the appear/foreground paths) — re-reads the LOCAL config+permission snapshot and REBUILDS the checklist from the SAME builder as `runAutoReads` (no connectivity re-probe, no billing, no prompt), carrying each prior probe/test result forward ONLY when its input is unchanged: `connection.network` always; `gateway.*` only if its per-ref gateway signature (`hash(url,token,authScheme,pin)`) matches; `sync.icloud` only while still signed in (a now-absent ubiquity token yields the fresh signed-out warning, not a stale green); `voice.stt.auth`/`voice.stt.test` only if the STT signature matches; `voice.tts.preview` only if the TTS signature matches (the `voicePreview` DISPLAY enum resets with it — in flight, the run's own guard resolves to `.idle` instead); each file lane's `reachAuth` AND its staged-checklist drill-down (`fileTransferResults`) only if its per-ref file-config signature (`hash(url,credential,pin)`) matches; permission + `sync.events` rows always rebuilt fresh. The voice signature is `id#hash(apiKey, customModel, resolved BYO-endpoint url|model|auth|pin, TTS voice)` — `Why:` fixing a typo'd key OR editing a custom endpoint's URL/auth/pin posts `.settingsDidChangeRemotely`, and the changed signature RESETS the stale result instead of carrying one bound to the old config. An unchanged config rebuilds an IDENTICAL array (no visible change — the flicker-lock holds); a genuine provider change restructures the Voice section (speech row ⇄ key row) with the insert animation suppressed. macOS `MacSettingsView` ALSO observes `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` (gated `selection != .diagnostics`) so the off-screen persistent runner re-derives WHILE the user is still in the Voice tab. Probe-generation guard (uniform): every result-producer captures its input signature at dispatch and DROPS an outcome whose signature moved mid-flight — the sweep's `voice.stt.auth` + `gateway.*` + file-lane reach outcomes, `runTranscriptionTest`, `runVoicePreview` (resolves the display enum to `.idle`), and `runFileTransferTest` (which compares against the LIVE persisted lane config, because it PERSISTS `fileTransferAvailable` — the old server's verdict must never stamp the new config). `Why:` a live config switch can't false-green (or false-red) the new setup with the old setup's result.
- `refreshConfigAndConnectivity()` (Diagnostics appear · `scenePhase → .active` · macOS `NSApplication.didBecomeActiveNotification`) — `refreshConfig()` THEN `reprobeConnectivity()`, which re-runs the two slow local probes (`NWPathMonitor` + CloudKit account status) IN PLACE so a recovered network / fixed iCloud sign-in reflects without a relaunch. A single `connectivityProbeInFlight` latch wraps BOTH the initial `runAutoReads` phase-2 AND `reprobeConnectivity`, so a foreground trigger firing during the first probe no-ops rather than racing a second. `Why:` a permission flipped in system Settings, or a provider switched in the Voice tab, or a network that just came back, reflects without reopening or relaunching. `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` stays on the config-only `refreshConfig()` (cheap, bursty).

**Voice section — "Active setup" + checks.** A top **Active setup** block names the active STT + TTS providers (`VoiceVendorRegistry.vendor(forSTTPresetID:/forTTSProviderID:)?.shortDisplayName` — the SAME source as Voice Setup: "Apple"/"OpenAI"/a custom endpoint's user-given name), each with a health glyph (`.warning` when a provider that NEEDS a key has none — a keyless BYO endpoint (auth `.none`) with a URL is correctly configured, never amber; the amber glyph always carries a one-line gloss + combined accessibility element, never stands alone). It lives in `activeVoiceSetup`, a UI-only display state OUTSIDE `checks` — `Why:` a user-named custom endpoint must never reach `copyBlock()` (allowlist); the copy block still carries only the sanitized `STT:`/`TTS:` archetype. TTS has NO probe row, so this block is the summary's ONLY TTS-config coverage — `attentionCount` counts `activeVoiceSetup` warnings too, else a green "Checks passed" could sit over an amber setup row. The checks below adapt to the active STT: the **Speech recognition** permission row (`voice.speech.permission`) shows ONLY for Apple on-device STT (the `SFSpeechRecognizer` grant is irrelevant to cloud STT — no perpetual N/A dash); the **Transcription key** probe row (`voice.stt.auth`) shows ONLY for cloud STT — retitled **Transcription endpoint** for a keyless BYO endpoint, whose probe runs WITHOUT the missing-key short-circuit and answers "does the endpoint accept requests?" (the probe honors the snapshot's resolved `customConfig` — per-uuid URL, effective auth scheme, cert-pinned session — exactly like the real transcribe path; never the legacy singleton slots / archetype bearer / `URLSession.shared`). Microphone + Speech Recognition stay in Voice (task-oriented troubleshooting: every prerequisite for recording/transcription remains beside the functional test), but are ACTIONABLE rather than passive: `.notDetermined` → neutral **Not requested yet** + explicit **Allow**; `.denied` → failed + **Open Settings** (TCC cannot re-prompt); `.restricted` → failed explanation only (Screen Time/MDM owns repair); granted → green/no button. An explicit **Allow** tap is the ONLY Diagnostics path that may prompt. The request participates in `isBusy` and refreshes the checklist in place after the prompt. `runAutoReads`, `runConnectionChecks`, `runTranscriptionTest`, and therefore **Test everything** remain non-prompting for these grants; the Apple in-process transcription runner checks current Speech authorization without calling `requestAuthorization`. The Voice section (`shouldShowVoiceSection`, pure) shows when a voice provider is configured OR mic is GRANTED, AND ALSO when mic is DENIED / Apple-speech denied-or-restricted even with no keys — `Why:` the fresh Apple-STT user who tapped "Don't Allow" must SEE the red mic row; `.undetermined` stays hidden when no other Voice relevance exists (no nagging a user who never asked), but renders with its neutral Allow action whenever Voice is already visible.

**Connection gateway rows.** One row per configured gateway from `gatewayDisplayOrder: [GatewayDisplayEntry]` — a UI-only display model OUTSIDE `checks` (focused/active-sorted, covering the non-file-capable `openrouter` too), titled with the gateway's REAL name via `DiagnosticCheckRow.titleOverride` (the custom label, or a `Custom gateway N` numbered fallback). The row's live status/glyph is read from the paired `checks` row (`connectionCheckID`), whose title stays GENERIC ("Custom gateway") — `Why:` `copyBlock()` reads `check.title`, so the user name lives only in `gatewayDisplayOrder`/the view, never the report. The `N` in the fallback + the anonymous `custom-gateway#N` copy tags all share ONE canonical config-order ordinal (`customOrdinals`, carried on `FileLaneState.customOrdinal`) so screen and report never disagree.

**Per-gateway file lanes.** A file server is a PER-GATEWAY capability, so each FILE-CAPABLE gateway (`fileTransferSupported`; `openrouter` excluded) renders a **File server** sub-row nested beneath its Connection gateway row (no standalone Files section) from `fileLanes: [FileLaneState]` — held OUTSIDE `checks` like `activeVoiceSetup` so a custom gateway's user name never reaches `copyBlock()`. Three tiers map to DISTINCT fields so a reach pass never reads as "attachments work": `configured` (`.autoRead` — URL+credential both present), `reachAuth` (the `.networkCheck` non-mutating probe — tops out at `.warning` "Unconfirmed — reachable, sign-in looks OK", NEVER `.passed`: a ranged GET's pass signal can be faked by a read-only server or a wrong base path, so only the write test earns green; `.passed` is set solely by that write test, always together with `writeVerified`, and the badge folds it to `.verified`), `writeVerified` (the per-lane explicit staged write test OR persisted `snapshot.available` — the ONLY "uploads work" proof; the mutating test is modeled per-lane on `FileLaneState`, not as a check tier). `FileLaneState.badge` (the single source the row glyph AND `attentionCount`/`needsAttention` read) checks a FRESH reach/write FAILURE BEFORE `writeVerified` — `Why:` a lane verified in a past session whose server later dies must show `.failed`, not a stale-green "Verified", and must register in the summary. A file-capable gateway with no file server stays NEUTRAL (`.notSetUp`), never a warning. Reach mapping (`FileServerClient.classifyReachability`, hardened — "404 = auth OK" is not protocol-universal: 404-before-auth / authenticated-404-on-wrong-path / redirect-to-login): `404`→reach/auth OK, `401`/`403`→auth failed, `200`/`206`/`416`/`3xx`→suspicious (a Control-UI/login page), else→fail-closed inconclusive; transport error→unreachable. Only the staged write test certifies.

**Send-ability + capability rows.** `connection.gateway.none` (`.autoRead`, `.failed`) when `configuredRemoteAgentRefs()` is empty — the fail-closed set ALSO catches cross-device token skew (a synced-URL ref with no bearer token in the synced Keychain is dropped). `connection.gateway.focused.missing` when a Troubleshoot deep-link's `focusedRef` isn't among the configured refs (surfaced EVEN beside a healthy gateway; names the KIND only). `connection.gateway.partial` (`.autoRead`, `.warning`) when `partiallyConfiguredRemoteAgentRefs()` is non-empty — a ref with a synced URL that is NOT send-able here (bearer token / required model missing on this device): the fail-closed drop a healthy sibling gateway would otherwise mask entirely; anonymous COUNT only, and the focused ref is excluded when `focused.missing` already names it. The **Capabilities** section (`.capability`, standing — always shows since Notifications always builds; `showsCapabilitySection` = any `.capability` row exists) groups the OS-permission rows surface-gated: **Notifications** (all), `capability.camera` (`.autoRead`, iOS — `AVCaptureDevice.authorizationStatus(for:.video)`; self-gating relevance: shows ONLY on `.denied` **and** camera hardware present — a denial provably implies the picker prompted once, whereas `.restricted` (Screen-Time/MDM) exists WITHOUT any camera attempt, so it is deliberately row-less — copy-block fact only — to never nag users who never touched the feature; `.warning` never red — camera is optional), `capability.screenRecording` (`.autoRead`, macOS — `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess()` for ⌘⇧2 Screenshot & Ask, relevance-gated on `screenRecordingCaptureAttemptedKey` — written once the feature works or its system ask has fired (never before Conduck is registered in the pane the row deep-links), and read ALONE so the row survives a re-signed install and reads denied until the current code signature is granted (TCC consent is per signature); an unavailable grant shows **Open Settings**, with turn-it-off-and-on-then-quit-and-reopen guidance — covers both an unadopted fresh grant and a grant recorded under a previous signature), `capability.shareInbox` (`.autoRead`, iOS+macOS, hidden when healthy — `SharedInboxDrainer.diagnosticStuckCount()`, DRAINER-OWNED classification: published envelopes older than 10 min counted ONLY once a full drain has completed this process (envelope age runs from queue time, so a cold launch straight into Diagnostics must not count shares the foreground drain hasn't had its claim pass on) + processing envelopes without a live in-flight owner or live background converse task — a `submitted` envelope with a live task is an in-flight long turn, never "stuck"; the same row also warns when `share-targets.json` is missing/undecodable while ≥1 gateway is configured — a broken appex picker), `capability.storage` (`.autoRead`, all, hidden ≥500 MB — `volumeAvailableCapacityForImportantUsage` on the App-Group container; <500 MB warn, <100 MB stronger copy still `.warning`; nil probe = UNKNOWN → hidden, never green). `voice.pendingRetry` (`.autoRead`, Voice section, hidden when none — `PendingRetryStore.diagnosticSnapshot()`: metadata + audio-file EXISTENCE, no audio load; names the REMAINING minutes of the 10-min TTL + the platform's recovery surface (composer / menu-bar voice window); an orphaned file gets "record again"; presence FORCE-shows the Voice section via `shouldShowVoiceSection(hasPendingRetry:)`). `UIApplication.backgroundRefreshStatus` + Low Power Mode stay in the iOS **Copy Diagnostics** facts only: Conduck uses background `URLSession`, not `BGAppRefreshTask`, so that broad setting cannot honestly certify user-facing readiness and has no visible pass/fail row. `connection.network` detail carries `NWPath.isConstrained` ("Low Data Mode is on…" — detail only, status stays `.passed`: a deliberate user setting is not a fault); `isExpensive` is copy-block-only metadata. **Local Network hint** (iOS INFERENCE — Apple exposes NO readable status): `HostReachabilityClass.classify` reduces a gateway/file-server host to a locality ENUM (private-LAN / `.local` / Tailscale `100.64.0.0/10` + IPv6 ULA/link-local; `.tailscale` transport hint but NOT `.funnel` which is public egress) — on a FAILED probe of a local-classed host while `connection.network` is green, a generic "check Local Network permission" hint appends to that row's detail. Only the bool crosses into runner state, never the host.

**Apple Watch row + live health query (iOS).** `sync.watch` (`.autoRead`) — INFO not red: hidden if unpaired; the **master switch is checked FIRST** (`PhoneSessionManager.isWatchEnabled == false` → a neutral `.notApplicable` "Watch sync is turned off" row, never a misleading connectivity readout); warn ONLY on paired-but-app-missing; a paired-but-unreachable watch is merely asleep. The detail ALWAYS carries the last-turn recency (`PhoneSessionManager.lastSuccessfulWatchTurn` — never → "this iPhone hasn't observed a successful Watch reply yet" end-to-end nudge, phrased as the PHONE's observation; else the relative date), plus a courier line when the newest settings broadcast FAILED to deliver. **Courier observability:** the settings `transferUserInfo` payload carries a marker (`watch.broadcast.kind = settings-broadcast`) so `session(_:didFinish:error:)` + the outstanding-transfer count select settings couriers ONLY — relay transcript replies ride the same queue and must never count; delivery stamps (`watch.broadcast.lastSuccessAt`/`lastFailureAt`, App-Group, date only, opportunistic forensics not a ledger) are cleared on `sessionDidDeactivate` (a Watch switch must not inherit the old watch's failures). **Live query:** the "Test everything" fan-out's Watch leg + a per-row **Check** button (shown only on the live `.passed` row; the health sub-block + its attention lines are likewise gated on the live row form — a disabled/unpaired row never renders or counts a stale snapshot) run one `diagnostics-pull` `sendMessage` round trip (5 s budget, `WatchHealthTransport` seam; the reply/error/deadline race resolves through the lock-backed one-shot latch `LockedOnce` — the file's single once-primitive, also guarding the `NWPathMonitor` probe — and NOT a task-group deadline). The claim stays NARROW: a reply proves "the Watch app responded + its local state", never gateway/STT reach from the wrist. Reply facts (`WatchHealthState`, held OUTSIDE `checks` like `fileLanes` — survives `refreshConfig()` by construction, NEVER mutates the row's status): watch app/OS versions, relay pending-queue depth ("N recordings waiting"), the watch's OWN mic + notification grants (amber lines when denied — counted into `attentionCount`, same rationale as `activeVoiceSetup`), and settings-courier standing — the verdict compares ONLY the watch's PERSISTED agent-envelope high-water vs the phone's last-assembled envelope ts (`watch.broadcast.lastAgentEnvelopeTs`, stamped in `assembleSettingsPayload()`), reported observationally ("accepted <relative>" / "hasn't received the latest yet") — never a bald "up to date" (the phone re-mints timestamps per assembly and the watch STT high-water is in-memory). Fail-soft: no reply → "Watch didn't respond. Open Conduck on your Watch, then check again." with the LAST GOOD snapshot preserved beneath ("Last check from <relative>"); a version-less `[:]` reply (old watch build / unknown kind) → `unsupported`, never an all-unknown report. Wire + skew rules: Cross-Device Sync → Interactive diagnostics pull.

**Copy Diagnostics** (`copyBlock()`) — paste-safe summary the user chooses to share (copy-only, no email). ALLOWLIST by construction: app/OS/device class, provider ARCHETYPES (`_<uuid>` stripped), counts, auth-scheme mix (+ `partial=` count), network qualifiers (`constrained`/`expensive`), permission enum names (incl. `Camera:`), per-check pass/fail + `AppError` code+slug on failure, anonymous ordinals (`custom-gateway#1`, `custom-stt#N` on the STT rows when a custom endpoint is active) + role tags, per-lane file facts (`file[<kind>]: configured/reachAuth/verified` — backend kind or anonymous `custom-gateway#N`, DERIVED from `fileLanes` so the report matches the screen live), Background-Refresh (`(lpm)`-suffixed under Low Power Mode)/Screen-Recording/Watch capability facts (enum names + bools + a recent/stale/never turn bucket), the Watch courier + health lines (`Watch courier: lastSuccess/lastFailure buckets + outstanding count`; `Watch health:` freshness/queue-depth/permissions/versions or the no-response reason incl. the numeric `WCError` code — DERIVED live like the file facts), and the silent-failure line (`ShareInbox: stuck=N targets=ok|broken · PendingRetry: none|parked(code, Nm left)|orphaned · Storage: ok|low|critical|unknown`). PHYSICALLY never calls a secret accessor — every field is a primitive from the local reads; NEVER a URL/host/token/key/cert/custom-model/endpoint-label/raw-error/file-path/message content. `DiagnosticsExplainer` (pure) delegates failure copy to `AppError.from(errorCode:)`'s `errorDescription`/`recoverySuggestion`; opaque (provider-message) codes forced to a generic cause so live provider text never reaches screen/copy.

**Troubleshoot deep-link (app-wide).** `DiagnosticsFocus(errorCode:ref:)` — a failable value type carrying ONLY `{code, ref}` (never message/URL/model/text) — is the SINGLE gate: init returns nil when the code is nil OR `AppError.isTroubleshootable == false` (a deny-list excluding local-audio + self-evident content errors: record-again, image/audio-too-large, chat-too-long, mic-busy, no-speech, settings-load). A surface shows a Troubleshoot affordance IFF it builds a non-nil focus. `TroubleshootButton(focus:)` is the reusable primitive for every IN-SCENE-GRAPH surface (send-error banner, file-transfer setup test, home retry card) — a peek `.sheet` over a FRESH focused runner (dismiss returns in place; the slide-up masks the one section-populate reflow → no persistent-host prewarm needed). The macOS menu-bar popover is outside the scene graph (raw `NSHostingController`, can't present a sheet) → routes through `MenuBarCoordinator.pendingDiagnosticsFocus` (paired with `pendingSettingsCategory = .diagnostics`) → `MainWindowView` → `DiagnosticsRunner.setFocus(ref:code:)` on the PERSISTENT runner, applied BEFORE the category shows (focused card on first paint) with an `.onChange(initialFocus)` fallback for the Settings-already-open case. Invariant: `pendingDiagnosticsFocus` is nilled at every non-diagnostics Settings entry (non-nil ONLY in flight to Diagnostics). Send-error state is encapsulated so code+message can't diverge: `setSendError` (`sendError` + `sendErrorCode`) / `setSendNotice` (message, nil code) / `clearSendError` — every set/clear routes through them. The focused runner seeds a cause+fix card (`focusedRef`+`focusedErrorCode` → `DiagnosticsExplainer`) and promotes the failing gateway's row to lead Connection.

**Sync-events row** reads the redacted ring buffer (`CloudSyncMonitor.recentSyncEventLines()`); warns ONLY on a CURRENT failure streak (newest-last tail all-FAIL), NOT historical recovered errors — `Why:` a `.warning` bubbles to the summary as "needs attention", so counting cold-start blips CloudKit already retried past cries wolf. Raw error count still ships in the copy block.

**Notifications row** (Capabilities section, always built) — the headless-delivery guarantee: `.notDetermined` → neutral **Not requested yet** + explicit **Allow**; `.denied` OR authorized-but-`alertSetting == .disabled` → warning + **Open Settings**; authorized with alerts enabled → green/no button. A silenced config still DELIVERS Shortcut/background/share replies, but INVISIBLY — a false-green if auth alone were checked. `Why:` a local notification is the ONLY feedback channel for those headless paths, so a suppressed one is a silent failure. Diagnostics may prompt ONLY after the row's explicit **Allow** tap; screen-open and **Test everything** paths remain non-prompting.

**Runner lifetime.** `MacSettingsView`/`IpadSettingsView` OWN the runner (`@State`, injected via `DiagnosticsContent(runner:)`) — the Settings `detail` `switch` tears the category down on each sidebar change, so a self-owned runner would re-seed from empty and the amber Copy button would flicker on return. iOS push + banner sheet self-own a fresh runner (navigation, not a tab-switch).

---

## Conversation Store

Local, CloudKit-synced **authoritative store** of every turn — single source of truth for *both* display and the upstream context (rationale in Remote Agent Round-Trip). Conduck uses its own iCloud container.

### Data model — two-level

`Conversations.xcdatamodeld`, model-level `usedWithCloudKit`, all three entities `codeGenerationType=class` + `syncable`. **Every attribute Core-Data-`optional`** — `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` forward-compat requirement, common mistake; a non-optional attr breaks CloudKit mirroring. Current model = `Conversations 6.xcdatamodel` (`.xccurrentversion` repointed; never edit a prior version in place — explicit versioning for CloudKit-schema auditability).

| Entity | Attributes | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `Conversation` | `id` UUID · `title` String? · `titleSnippet` String? · `createdAt` Date · `lastActivityAt` Date · `sessionID` String · `backend` String · `hideEarlierPhotos` Bool? (default NO) | `sessionID` = **local** conversation identity, NOT sent to the gateway (client-owned history); store-identity only. `lastActivityAt` = list sort key, bumped on every append. `backend` = a `RemoteAgentRef.rawString` (`openclaw`/`hermes`/`openrouter`/`custom_<uuid>`) — the per-conversation routing key every send path resolves the bound ref from (see Architectural Invariants for the binding lock; raw values locked in Settings & Storage). `titleSnippet` = denormalized first-user-line (≤60 char), written ONCE on the first user-turn append — the list-row title source (no gateway gives a real `title`), so the per-row-fetch-averse Watch list shows a meaningful row with zero extra fetches. `backfillTitleSnippetsIfNeeded()` (App-Group-flag-guarded one-shot) fills pre-field rows; CloudKit propagates to the Watch. `hideEarlierPhotos` = photo compat mode ("Keep chatting without photos", nil==false): OUTBOUND-only — the assembler replaces historical image parts with the canonical adapter-contract disclosure; store + UI keep the images; reversible via the thread banner ("Try photos again"). |
| `Message` | `id` UUID · `role` String (`user`/`agent`) · `text` String · `createdAt` Date · `sourceDevice` String · `status` String? · `failureCode` Int32? · `failureWireCode` String? · `failureHadHistoryImages` Bool? · `outputScanDone` Bool? | `sourceDevice` = base device (`phone`/`watch`/`mac`/`carplay`) optionally suffixed `-text`/`-voice` to tag turn **modality** — free-string encoding = zero migration; the bubble chip splits on the first `-`. `role` as String not a CD enum (CloudKit-simple). `status` = send-state (`sending`/`sent`/`failed`; **nil = legacy = `sent`**, no backfill); only attachment-bearing or in-flight turns set it (headless/agent turns stay nil). The three `failure*` fields = the persisted failure classification behind the inline delivery-error row (`failureCode` = `AppError.errorCode`; `failureWireCode` = the adapter-contract `error.code` when the gateway sent one, validated through `AdapterWireCode` on read — presence upgrades hedged→confident copy; `failureHadHistoryImages` = dispatch-time fact that the FAILED request carried historical image parts — gates the poisoned-chat copy; a render-time thread scan would over-claim because the image-history policy demotes images to file refs). NEVER raw server text; written only by `failTurn`, cleared by any `sent` flip. `outputScanDone` = retroactive output-scan marker on Watch/CarPlay-landed agent turns (see Agent File Transfer → Retroactive scan); nil on every other turn. |
| `Attachment` | `id` · `mimeType` · `data` Binary (`allowsExternalBinaryDataStorage` → CloudKit Asset when large) · `thumbnailData` Binary? (~50 KB preview) · `width`/`height` · `byteSize` · `sequence` · `createdAt` · `filename` · `isServerReference` Bool? (default NO) · `storedKey` String? · `previewData` Binary? (external storage) · `previewKind` String? | One image OR text-file OR server-reference blob on a `Message`. Images: full JPEG in `data`, no `filename`. Text/code: extracted UTF-8 in `data` + `filename` for the chip + inline-splice; a DUAL-TEXT file ALSO carries `storedKey` with `isServerReference=false` (inline retained AND a byte-faithful server copy uploaded). **Server references** (Agent File Transfer): `isServerReference=true` + `storedKey` (`<8hex>__<name>` on the user's file-server), `data` EMPTY, `filename`=original. `AttachmentRecord.isText` = `!isImage && !isServerReference`. Server refs MAY additionally carry a bounded preview snapshot for the wrist (Agent File Transfer → Preview enrichment): text ≤128 KiB in `previewData` + `previewKind="text"`, image thumbnail in the ordinary `thumbnailData`; `AttachmentRecord` decodes `previewKind` but NEVER `previewData` (lazy single-row fetch via `fetchPreviewText`). |

Relationships: `Conversation.messages` →> `Message` + `Message.attachments` →> `Attachment`, both **Cascade**. **No Core Data ordered relationship** — `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` rejects `NSOrderedSet`; order messages in code by `createdAt` (optional `sequence` Int tiebreaker), attachments by `Attachment.sequence`. `Sendable` snapshot structs `ConversationRecord`/`MessageRecord`/`AttachmentRecord` decouple from `NSManagedObject` for actor/SwiftUI safety. **`AttachmentRecord` NEVER carries full image bytes** (memory discipline — inlining bytes blows up list memory) — only metadata + the small `thumbnailData` + (text mimeTypes only) decoded `extractedText`; image bytes load on demand via `loadAttachmentData`. New attrs are additive (optional) → lightweight inferred migration, no migration code.

**Conversation-list search (unified — iPhone/iPad/macOS + Watch).** Filter = `tier-1 ∪ tier-2`. **Tier-1** = instant in-memory match over `title` + `titleSnippet` (`[.caseInsensitive,.diacriticInsensitive]`). **Tier-2** = whole-history content match via `ConversationStore.searchConversationIDs(containing:)` — ONE `NSFetchRequest<NSDictionary>(entityName:"Conversation")` with `resultType=.dictionaryResultType` + `propertiesToFetch=["id"]`, predicate `ANY messages.text CONTAINS[cd] %@` on a `newBackgroundContext()`, returns `Set<UUID>` (matches mid-thread + agent turns, scroll-independent; projects the direct `id` attr, never a relationship keypath). Tier-2 `[cd]` folding is deliberately mirrored by Tier-1's `[.diacriticInsensitive]` so both behave identically. **Why a read-path predicate, NOT a denormalized `Conversation.searchText` column:** a synced field would force a CloudKit Production schema redeploy + a write-path field + cross-device-append drift — the predicate has none (read-only, zero schema). Only per-surface difference is DISPLAY: phone/pad/Mac rows show a last-message preview subtitle; the Watch list stays fetch-averse (title + time) and enters search via a `TextFieldLink` (watchOS has no programmatic `.searchable`).

### Storage + sync

`ConversationStore` actor wraps `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` (`NSPersistentHistoryTracking` + `.NSPersistentStoreRemoteChange` fan-in; two-level CRUD). **Load is async single-flight:** `ensureLoaded()` stores one `Task` (`shouldAddStoreAsynchronously = true`, continuation-bridged `loadPersistentStores`) — concurrent first touches await the SAME load, a failed load is sticky (every later call rethrows the same error; `ConversationHistoryAssemblerTests` pins the contract), and no thread ever parks on a semaphore. **Reads AND writes never touch the `viewContext`:** every fetch runs on a fresh `newBackgroundContext()` inside `perform`, mapping to the `Sendable` snapshot structs before returning — a fresh context registers nothing (results authoritative by construction, no `shouldRefreshRefetchedObjects` needed), and a fetch stuck behind CloudKit-sync SQLite contention blocks a pool thread, never the UI (the Watch froze 87 s on exactly that lock when reads ran on the main-queue context; `fetch.* slow` os.Logger lines watch for regressions). Every write runs the same way — a fresh context from the private `newWriteContext()` factory (the ONE place the `NSMergeByPropertyObjectTrumpMergePolicy` invariant lives; a bare `newBackgroundContext()` defaults to the THROWING error merge policy, which on the `try?` best-effort writes would silently drop a save on its first CloudKit-import conflict), with `save()` awaited inside `perform` BEFORE `postDidChange()` fires — observers refetch committed rows, so read-your-own-write holds by construction. The `viewContext` is deliberately unconfigured and unused: auto-merge stays OFF, so a CloudKit import storm fans zero per-save merge passes onto the main queue; anyone reintroducing `viewContext` work must restore `automaticallyMergesChangesFromParent` + a merge policy. **Remote-change fan-in is debounced:** each `.NSPersistentStoreRemoteChange` logs `remote.change n=` then schedules the `.conversationsDidChange` post through `RemoteChangeDebouncer` (@MainActor; 300 ms trailing + 1 s max-latency cap enforced SYNCHRONOUSLY at schedule time, so a main-queue backlog can't starve the cap) — a CloudKit import storm collapses to ≤1 refetch/s per observer instead of 1:1, and the trailing edge always fires (never-drop). Local writes (`postDidChange()`) post immediately — user-visible latency is never debounced. **SQLite store lives in the App Group container** — a headless capture intent runs in a separate process and shares this sqlite; the default Application Support path is per-process-private, so a wrong store location silently splits data. The simulator/test seam (`init(inMemory:storeURL:)`) uses a plain `NSPersistentContainer` (`cloudKit:false`) — `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` fatal-asserts on a sim with no iCloud account. `configureSyncOptions(on:cloudKit:)` sets ONLY history-tracking + remote-change-post; lightweight migration + schema inference rely on the `NSPersistentStoreDescription` defaults (no migration code). **CloudKit private database = the user's own iCloud**, developer-blind (no-backend identity; see Privacy & Security). Production attaches `cloudKitContainerOptions(containerIdentifier: iCloud.ai.gigaduck.agentrelay)` (frozen reverse-DNS namespace; both `.entitlements` carry `icloud-container-identifiers` + `icloud-services: CloudKit`). **Op note:** deploy the CloudKit schema to Production before any TestFlight/App Store build (dev builds use the Development auto-schema environment).

### Sync robustness (silent + one actionable surface)

`NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` owns its own CKDatabaseSubscription + push internally — app-level `registerForRemoteNotifications` is NOT required and is **deliberately absent** (NSPCKC owns its subscription; an agent "fixing" missing push registration would be wrong). No public force-fetch/force-export exists, so the design SURFACES state, never commands sync. Four behaviors, all silent except the last:

| Behavior | Where | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Early store warm-up** | `ConversationStore.warmUp()` at launch (iOS + macOS; watchOS already does it) | Stack loads at launch so NSPCKC mirrors immediately, not lazily on first list view. |
| **Foreground snapshot refresh** | iOS/macOS post `.conversationsNeedLocalRefresh`; only the list + detail VMs observe it (each `reload()`s) | Re-reads the LOCAL store on foreground so an import that landed while suspended (whose remote-change fired before a VM existed) still shows. A DEDICATED name, NOT `.conversationsDidChange` — the latter drives share-target/menu-bar/CarPlay observers (unwanted every foreground). NOT a sync pull (no force-fetch API). |
| **Silent diagnostics** | `CloudSyncMonitor` observes `eventChangedNotification`, fetches via actor-side `recentSyncEventSummaries()` (returns `Sendable SyncEventSummary`) | `os_log` + a capped App-Group ring buffer of REDACTED summaries (event type / succeeded / error domain+code / store id — never message text, record ids, or `localizedDescription`). The actor-side fetch keeps the non-`Sendable` container/events off the main actor; the launch/foreground fetch catches events fired while suspended (the live observer misses those). |
| **iCloud-unavailable surface** (ONLY visible chrome) | `CloudSyncMonitor.iCloudUnavailable` (gated on `CKContainer.accountStatus()` + `CKAccountChangedNotification`) drives a dismissible banner + a settings row | True ONLY for user-actionable states — `.noAccount`/`.restricted` + a LIVE `CKError.quotaExceeded` event. NEVER for `.couldNotDetermine`/`.temporarilyUnavailable`/network/throttle/first-sight errors (system retries → log only). Dismissal is sticky per outage episode (App-Group `iCloudBannerDismissedKey`), auto-reset when the account returns to `.available`. QA: `-ConduckQAForceICloudUnavailable` (gated on `QAMode.isActive`) forces it on. |

### Who writes it

Both Trigger Architecture entry points feed one store: capture → STT → `appendMessage(role:.user)`; gateway reply → `appendMessage(role:.agent)`. Target selection is per-lane: in-app/window composers append to the VISIBLE thread; headless quick captures select via the per-device active-conversation pointer (stale/cleared/`alwaysNew` → mint a new `Conversation`); CarPlay appends to its session pin. "Continue" a past conversation = open it and append — the client re-sends that conversation's stored history as context, so resumption works entirely from the local store.

**Attachment writes + send-state.**
- `appendMessage(...:status:attachments:[AttachmentDraft])` — inserts + saves on a background write context like every store write (a multi-MB blob save near the main thread hitches the UI); text turn + attachments commit in the SAME single save.
- `addAttachments(messageID:attachments:)` — appends drafts to an EXISTING message on a background context. Backs the Agent File Transfer crash-safety ordering: the agent bubble persists FIRST, then `FileTransferOutputDetector` output files attach after.
- `loadAttachmentData(for:)` loads full image bytes on demand (full-screen viewer + prior-turn data-URI assembly — the snapshot path never carries them).
- **Send-state recovery.** `updateStatus(messageID:to:)` flips one turn; `markPendingUserTurns(conversationID:to:)` flips ALL still-`sending` user turns in a conversation. The **iOS background converse delegate** (`BackgroundRemoteAgent` `recordReply`/`postTurnFailed`) is the AUTHORITATIVE writer — it runs even after an OS suspend+relaunch killed the foreground continuation, so the bubble spinner can't stick. `markPendingUserTurn(messageID:to:)` is the EXACT-message variant: the conversation-wide flip ALIASES sibling concurrent in-flight turns (a long headless think + an in-app follow-up — whichever resolves first would flip both), so any lane that threaded the user `Message.id` flips only its own turn. Failure-classification writers (`failTurn`/`failPendingUserTurns`), the atomic retry claim (`beginRetry`), the clear-on-`sent` rule, and the compat-mode flag writer (`setHideEarlierPhotos`) are specified in Remote Agent Round-Trip → Send-state lifecycle. `sweepStaleSendingUserTurns(olderThan:excludingConversationIDs:)` is the launch-time recovery sweep (default 30-min grace > the 600 s resource timeout + CloudKit sync skew; excludes conversations with a live background task): a force-quit (or macOS quit mid-foreground-send — macOS has no background URLSession delegate at all) kills the process before any flip, and the Retry chip needs `status=="failed"`. **Answered-turn guard:** a stale `sending` turn with a LATER agent message in the same conversation flips to `sent` NOT `failed` — the delegate appends the reply first (crash-safety ordering), so a landed reply above a still-`sending` turn means it was delivered; marking it `failed` would float a Retry chip → duplicate send.
- **Share-drain idempotency:** `appendMessage(id: UUID? = nil)` — supplied id + existing message is a no-op returning the existing snapshot (no duplicate insert, no `lastActivityAt` re-bump — a re-bump would float a stale thread — and no change post); the existence probe runs INSIDE the write's own `perform`, on the same context as the insert, so no suspension window sits between probe and insert (not atomic across two CONCURRENT same-id appends — CloudKit forbids unique constraints — but the only id-supplying caller is the serialized `SharedInboxDrainer`, which passes the manifest `uuid` as `Message.id`; see Share Extension exactly-once). Text-only callers default to a fresh UUID, source-compatible.

### Context assembly + trim policy (the cost lever)

Each turn's upstream `messages[]` is built from the active `Conversation`'s stored turns (oldest→newest) + the new user turn — the only place context is constructed, identical for every backend (see Remote Agent Round-Trip → Request shaping). Trim policy (`Constants.swift`, conservative — the user pays their own LLM bill):
- **All turns:** include the last `contextMaxTurns` (default generous — voice threads are short). Older turns dropped from the *sent* array only; the store keeps everything for display.
- **Prior-turn images are RETAINED, not dropped**, subject to the per-gateway `ImageHistoryPolicy` (see Agent File Transfer → Image history replay). `ConversationHistoryAssembler.assemble` resolves prior-turn image bytes via async `loadAttachmentData` for ALL SIX dispatch surfaces (in-app ×2, headless intent, CarPlay, Watch, share drainer) — the single choke point — then the synchronous priorTurns builder takes the honesty floor on any unresolved/missing image (disk ref or unavailable note, never bare text). Prior-turn text-file attachments re-splice inline (same fenced format). The Watch always runs `.recent` (no per-gateway policy lookup; App-Group-only, never broadcast).
- **Photo compat mode** (`Conversation.hideEarlierPhotos`): the assembler's POST-pass (`ConverseRequest.substitutingHistoricalImages`) replaces every historical `image_url` part IN PLACE with the canonical adapter-contract disclosure ("An image was attached in this earlier message, but this adapter cannot inspect it. Do not infer its contents.") — AFTER policy routing, so it touches exactly the parts that would go on the wire (reference/expired turns are already text). Outbound-only; the store and UI keep the images; the CURRENT turn's photos are never substituted (the explicit "Resend without photo" action drops that one turn's photo content instead). Applied at the choke point → every surface (send, retry, share, intent, CarPlay, Watch) behaves identically.
- Trimming affects only what is *sent*, never *stored*/*displayed*; a trimmed request still resumes coherently.

### Drift boundary

The store holds *the conversations the user had through Conduck* — not a universal mirror of everything the agent knows (turns via Telegram/WhatsApp/other clients aren't here). Under client-owned history this is by design: whatever Conduck sends in `messages[]` *is* the entire context the agent gets for that thread. The agent's server-side *configuration* (persona, tools) still applies. Surface honestly in onboarding/docs.

### Chat-UI rendering deps

Display layer over the store:

| Concern | Decision + reason |
|---|---|
| **Chat thread shell** (iOS + macOS + Watch) | Hand-rolled SwiftUI: `ScrollView` + `LazyVStack` + `MessageBubble` + `ScrollViewReader` auto-scroll (Watch `List`; CarPlay `CPListTemplate`). No library spans all four platforms (`ExyteChat` iOS-only, `MessageKit` UIKit). |
| **Markdown rendering** (agent replies, iOS + macOS) | `Textual`, pinned by revision to the **`GigaDuckAI/textual` fork** (MIT; `swift-markdown-ui`'s successor). Renders each reply as ONE selectable surface so `.textual.textSelection(.enabled)` gives free drag-select across blocks — a per-block `VStack` could not. `TEXTUAL_ENABLE_TEXT_SELECTION` is defined for iOS/macOS/macCatalyst/visionOS only (NOT watchOS/tvOS, so excluded surfaces compile clean). **Selection-layer isolation (load-bearing — do not remove):** Textual's whole-document selection overlay keeps in-drag state in an `NSView`/`UIView` that a parent-driven re-render of the `StructuredText` resets mid-drag. So the agent body renders through a dedicated **`Equatable` `AgentMarkdownBody` leaf keyed on `text` alone + `.equatable()`**, and `MessageBubble` is itself `Equatable` — unrelated thread churn (read-aloud `speakState`, turn boundaries) can no longer re-touch the selection layer. The fork additionally delivers the layout collection through a non-interactive `NSViewRepresentable`/`UIViewRepresentable` (immune to SwiftUI's same-frame `onChange` drop) — fixes short replies that lay out in a SINGLE frame, where the populated layout never reached the selection model and a drag-select never started. Identity must stay stable: **no `.id(...)` on the leaf, no `.drawingGroup()`** (rasterizes and kills selection); the tight `(messageID, text)` equality key is what preserves Textual's internal `@State` lifecycle. |
| **Attachment render** (user bubbles, iOS + macOS) | Inline image grid keyed on `thumbnailData` (`AttachmentImageGrid`); tap → swipeable full-screen pinch-zoom gallery (`AttachmentFullScreenView`, `TabView(.page)`; thumbnail instant, full bytes via `loadAttachmentData`). The per-page pan `DragGesture` is gated `including: scale>1 ? .all : .none` — at scale 1 the pan must yield to `TabView` paging else horizontal page-swipes are eaten. Text/code files render as file chips. Send-state footer (user turns): `sending`→spinner · `sent`/nil→clean; a `failed` turn shows nothing in the footer — the delivery error row carries it. |
| **Delivery error row** (failed user turns, iOS + macOS) | Persistent inline row UNDER the failed bubble (delivery metadata — never a fabricated assistant bubble, never in outbound history): title + safe cause + actions, all derived from the PERSISTED `failure*` fields via the pure `DeclinedTurnPresentation.classify` (one classifier for row AND toast — the two can't drift). Copy is a frozen table (do not reword); vocabulary **"gateway", never "model"** (the client can't attribute adapter vs engine). Confident copy ("Photo declined"/"Chat blocked by an earlier photo") needs a validated wire code — poisoned additionally needs `failureHadHistoryImages==true`; heuristic/legacy rows stay hedged ("couldn't"/"may be"); generic failures show the `AppError` copy + Troubleshoot (Diagnostics deep-link) when troubleshootable. Actions: "Try again" (atomic `beginRetry`) · "Resend without photo" (photo turns with other resendable content → enables compat mode + `retry(omittingPhotos:)`) · "Keep chatting without photos" (poisoned case → enables compat mode, no resend). **Toast rule:** the transient `sendErrorBanner` shows only when the failed row is OFFSCREEN — each bubble reports real viewport visibility via `onScrollVisibilityChange` (NOT lazy-lifecycle `onAppear`) into view-local state keyed by `sendErrorMessageID`; pre-flight errors with no row (nil id) always banner. The compat-mode state shows a persistent top banner ("Earlier photos are hidden from this gateway in this chat." + reversible "Try photos again"). |
| **Gateway identity badge** (overview surfaces, NOT the open thread) | `GatewayBadge` colored circle + monogram, hued per-gateway via `RemoteAgentBadgePalette`. **Invariant:** built-in reserved colors (OpenClaw orange, Hermes teal, OpenRouter slate) are held OUTSIDE `customPalette`, so a custom's auto-assigned hue can never collide with a built-in (`ShareTargetsSnapshotWriterColorTests` asserts disjointness). Leads each conversation-overview row (phone/pad/Mac list, CarPlay recent-picker via `ImageRenderer`→`UIImage`, Share-sheet picker). Gated `configuredRemoteAgentRefs().count >= 2` (single-gateway → no badge). NOT inside the open thread (nav-title already names the bound gateway). Unresolvable ref (deleted custom) → no badge. |

**Per-surface application:** iOS + macOS render the agent bubble via `Textual` `StructuredText`; user bubbles = plain `Text` + `.textSelection(.enabled)` with the attachment grid/chips above. **Watch** renders synced image **thumbnails** inline (never full bytes; `[Image attached]` fallback), per-attachment file rows via `AttachmentRecord.watchDisplayClass` (synced text/code ≤128 KB AND server refs with a synced text preview = tappable → `WatchAttachmentTextView`; server refs with a thumbnail render it inline; preview-less server refs / oversized / unsynced = passive markers — see Per-Surface → Apple Watch), and plaintext replies — agent bubbles collapse Markdown links to their label (`ReplySanitizer.linkCollapsed`: a raw `[name](target)` is wrap-noise on the wrist and an agent file link can leak the gateway host's filesystem path; same collapse on the iPhone/macOS conversation-list preview line), while the FULL Markdown strip (`ReplySanitizer.spoken(_:)`) stays TTS-only. **CarPlay** = voice only; the picker shows titles/dates, never thread text. Rejected (don't revisit): native `Text(AttributedString(markdown:))` (inline-only — silently drops headings/lists/tables/code blocks LLM replies emit routinely).
```

---

## Cross-Device Sync

### iPhone → Watch broadcast (envelope-only)

`PhoneSessionManager` broadcasts via `WCSession.transferUserInfo` (never `applicationContext` — keeps the envelope the sole atomic carrier; avoids a torn read). `applicationContext` carries only non-secret metadata (user ID + `stt.preferredLanguage`).

**STT envelope** (`Constants.sttActivePresetEnvelopeKey`): `STTBroadcastEnvelope { presetID, apiKey: String?, customModel?, ttsProviderID?, ttsApiKey?, ttsVoice?, ttsCustomModel?, timestamp }`.
- `apiKey`/`ttsApiKey` are **Optional, NOT empty-string sentinel** — encoder OMITS the dict key when nil. `""` would round-trip as `Optional.some("")` and trigger keyless-vs-empty drift on the Watch. Wire-locked.
- `presetID` carries `custom-openai_<uuid>` for a custom STT endpoint (Watch relay accepts by prefix).
- TTS fields additive + omit-when-nil; tolerant decode → un-upgraded Watch ignores them. A TTS-only update ships even when STT is keyless-Apple (if a cloud TTS is keyed).
- **Monotonic timestamp** — Watch discards older envelopes. `updateActiveSTT` commits STT + TTS under the SAME timestamp + App-Group cold-launch mirror: `watch.activeTTSProviderID` / `watch.ttsVoice` / `watch.ttsCustomModel` (all under the same monotonic-timestamp guard); `ttsApiKey` is in-memory only, NEVER disk.
- 250ms iPhone-side broadcast debounce; rebroadcast on `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` (first onboarding key paste reaches the wrist).

**Interactive settings PULL.** `transferUserInfo` is a lazy queue — a fresh Watch can record before queued envelopes drain (stale `apple-on-device` default → misrouted relay). `pullSettingsFromPhone(maxWait:)` sends `{kind: settingsPullMessageKind}` via `sendMessage`; the iPhone replies with the SAME payload the broadcast queues (+ non-secret `stt.preferredLanguage`), applied via the SAME inline-awaited decode/commit path as `didReceiveUserInfo`. Idempotent under the monotonic guards. Triggers: Watch activation / reachability-gain, and a pre-routing guard in `WatchRecordingService` (`lastEnvelopeTimestamp == 0` + reachable → 1.5s budget, proceed on timeout). Single in-flight task; the pull is an opportunistic freshness upgrade, never a gate.

**Explicit user re-send** (Settings → Apple Watch → Sync, LAST section — a recovery control, not a primary setting). `resendSettingsToWatch() async -> WatchResendOutcome` (`queued`/`activationPending`/`notPaired`/`watchAppNotInstalled`): cancels any pending debounced broadcast (a tap must not double-enqueue 250ms later), preflights via the pure `resendPreflight(activationState:isPaired:isWatchAppInstalled:)` (precedence activation→pairing→install; **never consults `isReachable`** — `transferUserInfo` queues for background delivery by design), then reuses the same send core as the broadcast (`sendSettingsToWatch()`) and reports `.queued` only after `transferUserInfo` returns its transfer object. **Copy honesty (no ack protocol exists):** the result line says "queued for delivery", NEVER "Watch updated"; the quiet caption below reads the `didFinish` success stamp (`watchBroadcastLastSuccessAtKey`) phrased as a delivery-layer fact ("Last settings transfer completed …" — `didFinish` is transfer completion, not an apply acknowledgement), live via `UserDefaults.didChangeNotification`.

**Interactive diagnostics PULL (phone → watch).** `{kind: watchDiagnosticsPullMessageKind}` sent BY THE PHONE via `sendMessage` WITH a replyHandler — the only replyHandler message toward the Watch. **Dispatch safety:** WCSession routes by the SENDER's replyHandler presence — the Watch's `session(_:didReceiveMessage:replyHandler:)` receives ONLY replyHandler-bearing messages, while the phone's relay-reply ship (`replyHandler: nil`) routes to the no-reply handler. The responder extracts primitives on the framework queue, wraps the reply closure in a thread-safe one-shot (`WatchOneShotReply` — exactly-once is a hard guarantee, not a code-path promise), hops to MainActor, and replies with `WatchDiagnosticsReporter.makeReply` — flat `Constants.WatchDiagnosticsReplyKey` primitives ONLY (`diag.v` schema stamp, app/OS versions, STT + agent envelope high-waters, `AppleRelayPendingQueue.entryCount`, the watch's own mic/notification enum names, `isReachable`; never a URL/token/name). **Skew, both directions fail-soft:** old watch (no responder) → the phone's errorHandler fires → same path as a timeout; unknown kind on a new watch → immediate `replyHandler([:])`, which the phone decodes as `unsupported` (a reply without `diag.v` is never a health report); every reply key is optional on decode. **Courier marker:** the settings broadcast payload additionally carries `watch.broadcast.kind = settings-broadcast` (additive; the Watch decoder ignores unknown keys) so the phone's `didFinish userInfoTransfer` stamps + outstanding-transfer counts select settings couriers only — relay replies ride the same `transferUserInfo` queue and must never move the settings-delivery stamps. Consumer: Local Diagnostics → Apple Watch row.

**Secrets.** STT keys + bearer tokens sync iPhone/iPad/Mac via iCloud Keychain (`kSecAttrSynchronizable`); macOS/CarPlay pick up directly (→ Privacy & Security). The **Watch is the exception** — non-sync Keychain → receives secrets via the WCSession envelopes.

Watch KVS observer is un-gated: `ubiquityIdentityToken` is ALWAYS nil on watchOS, so a guard on it dead-codes the observer. `loadFromiCloudKVS()`'s `activePresetID` hydrate is `lastEnvelopeTimestamp == 0`-guarded (parity with customModel/TTS) so an observer-fired reload can't stomp a fresher envelope.

**Multi-gateway broadcast** (`remoteAgent.activeMultiEnvelope`): `RemoteAgentMultiBroadcastEnvelope { backends: [RemoteAgentBroadcastEnvelope], defaultBackendRef: String, timestamp, sessionPolicy: String? }`; a legacy single `RemoteAgentBroadcastEnvelope` ships alongside for back-compat. Each sub-envelope wire dict: `backend` (ref `rawString`) + `url` + omit-nil `name`/`model`/`colorID`/`monogram`/`token`/`certFingerprintHex`/`activeSessionID`, plus two ALWAYS-encoded fields:
- `token: String?` Optional, NOT empty-string sentinel (same trap as STT `apiKey`).
- `authScheme` (`.bearer`/`.none`) ALWAYS encoded; decode defaults a missing key → `.bearer` (fail closed). A `.bearer`+nil-token envelope PRESERVES the Watch's stored token — the Watch goes keyless ONLY on an explicit `.none`. Never infer keyless from a missing token (→ Architectural Invariants).
- `fileTransferAvailable: Bool` ALWAYS encoded, decode defaults a missing key → `false` (un-upgraded phone → wrist omits the delivery clause, keeps the spoken clause). Carries the phone-computed READY gate (`fileTransferReadySnapshot != nil`); the Watch persists it per-ref (wholesale map replacement per accepted envelope) and uses it ONLY to gate the per-turn file-delivery instruction on wrist sends — file transfer UI/downloads stay an iPhone/iPad/Mac capability.

Decode requires only a NON-EMPTY `backend` ref string (no `RemoteAgentBackend(rawValue:)` enum gate) so `custom_<uuid>` refs round-trip; an unparseable ref resolves downstream to `remoteAgentNotConfigured`, never a decode failure that strands the whole envelope. The Watch stores per-ref tokens (Keychain `remoteAgent.token.<ref-suffix>`) + per-ref URL/cert + a persisted `[CustomGateway]` roster that DOUBLES as the known-custom-refs index (`allCases` can't enumerate customs, so a dropped custom's App-Group slots are GC'd by reconciling this index on each multi-update). Per-conversation binding; unknown bound ref → not-configured error, no reroute (→ Architectural Invariants). Multi-envelope ships only when ≥1 gateway is configured (courier gate). "No token to KVS" + monotonic-discard + "Watch can't edit gateway" all hold.

`defaultBackendRef` = the **Watch-effective default** (`watchEffectiveDefaultRef()`), NOT the iPhone's own — the iPhone-set Watch override iff still configured, else iPhone device-local ("Follow iPhone"); the iPhone self-heals a dangling override before sending. The Watch persists the remote-agent high-water timestamp in `watch.lastRemoteAgentEnvelopeTimestamp` and hydrates it from App Group BEFORE applying any queued envelope — else a process restart lets an OLDER queued `transferUserInfo` regress the default. Watch default reads are App-Group-only. On accepting a NEWER envelope whose `defaultBackendRef` differs, the Watch clears its OWN active-conversation pointer (prevents a stale A→B→A-within-TTL revival continuing the old thread) — ONLY on an actual change, never a same-value re-delivery.

`sessionPolicy` = the **Watch-effective `SessionContinuationPolicy`** raw value (`watchEffectiveSessionContinuationPolicy()`), per-device, cached in App Group `remoteAgent.sessionPolicy` (default `.minutes30`); an unknown raw or a nil leaves the prior cache intact (→ Settings & Storage owns the policy semantics).

### Watch → iPhone Apple-relay (Option B)

See Apple On-Device STT → Watch Option B audio relay. Wire protocol locked (inline `sendMessage` fast path + wake-ping + reply-channel upgrade); convergence via claim-token requestID — enqueue-first durable queue, 30s/120s timeouts, same-ID retries, late-reply `reconcile`, iPhone idempotency ledger.

### Concurrent agent turns (no contended lock)

Conduck is keyless (pins no server session), so concurrent turns on one conversation don't contend a server lock; the reason differs per backend. **OpenClaw** gives each keyless request a unique random session key → unique file + lock, no collision. **Hermes** derives a STABLE session from the opener, so two devices on one conversation map to the same session + sandbox dir with no server-side serialization — conversation content stays authoritative from each request body (no transcript bleed), but concurrent tool runs could race the shared sandbox. Either way, two requests → two independent CloudKit appends, ordered by `createdAt`. No `remoteAgentSessionBusy`, no 423/lock-retry path (→ Error Taxonomy). Soft UX hint (not correctness): after a successful turn, wait `interTurnQuietPeriod` (default 500ms) before another *device* fires on the same conversation — cuts interleaved-bubble churn + the Hermes shared-sandbox race window.

### KVS forward-compat

iCloud KVS is async cross-device sync — an older version can receive newer-version writes. Reader MUST tolerate unknown keys. Backward-incompatible schema changes need explicit migration code at the time of the change.

---

## Error Taxonomy — `AppError`

`enum AppError: LocalizedError` + `CustomNSError` (extension). `CustomNSError` is required so Shortcuts surfaces the friendly message — without it Shortcuts shows the raw Swift error. Per-case props: `errorCode: Int`, `isRetryable: Bool`, `maxAttempts: Int`, `shouldPreserveForRetry: Bool` (`PendingRetryGuard` reads them). **Numeric codes are stable + gappy by design** (Shortcuts user-facing continuity) — fill gaps when adding, never renumber. `AppError.from(errorCode:message:)` is the inverse of the `errorCode` getter and round-trips every emitted code across the Watch relay wire; the Watch decodes the numeric slot, so a missing inverse mapping collapses a failure to a blank banner. Codes carrying an associated `Error` (1/7/99) can't reconstruct it → fall through to `.apiFailure(message:)` so the wire message still surfaces.

**`maxAttempts` model:** transport-layer blips (3 = `noInternetConnection`/`networkError`/`requestTimeout` — DNS/dropped-packet often self-heals on retry 2); upstream-outage retryables (2 = `sttProviderUnreachable`/`sttServerError`/`sttTooManyRequests`/`apiFailure`, cloud-TTS `ttsProviderUnreachable`/`ttsEmptyAudio`/`ttsRateLimited`); remote-agent retryables **and every non-retryable case** (1 — a non-retryable case never loops anyway; the user gets a banner with an explicit Try Again rather than a silent burn of their own LLM budget). `shouldPreserveForRetry = true` ONLY for `persistentNetworkFailure`/`sttProviderUnreachable`/`sttServerError`.

**Two copy layers, deliberately not one.** `AppError.errorDescription`/`recoverySuggestion` are the APP-WIDE strings (`remoteAgent.error.*` keys) — surfaced by Diagnostics, CarPlay, the Watch relay — and are **frozen**: rewording a code's copy is a cross-platform parity break. They are written for a user who is NOT in the editor ("Open Settings and…"). `SettingsViewModel.friendlyGatewayMessage(for:category:keyShapeLooksWrong:)` is the GATEWAY-EDITOR mapper (`remoteAgent.editor.*` keys) — the one place the user already has the field on screen, so it never says "Open Settings" — and it is the single mapping for every gateway-probe surface (per-ref editor, guided setup, pairing import); a second copy silently diverges and the flow keeping the stale one goes on swallowing new failures. **Editor remedies dispatch on `descriptor.category`, never on backend identity:** a `.hostedModel` provider is a service the user does NOT operate, so the self-hosted remedies ("check the gateway is running", "check the gateway logs", "verify the bearer token for your gateway") describe a machine that does not exist. `keyShapeLooksWrong` is a **`Bool`, never the key** — that is what keeps the mapper secret-free by construction rather than by care.

### STT cases

| Code | Case | Retryable | maxAttempts |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | `sttAuthFailed` — HTTP-401 key rejection ONLY (Apple TCC denial routes to 51, not here) | ❌ | 1 |
| 13 | `sttQuotaExceeded` (Mistral/Gemini/OpenRouter 429 — billing-fatal) | ❌ | 1 |
| 16 | `sttTooManyRequests` (OpenAI/ElevenLabs/Qwen 429 — transient rate-limit) | ✅ | 2 |
| 17 | `sttServerError` (5xx) | ✅ | 2 |
| 18 | `appleSpeechModelNotInstalled` (per-locale `SpeechTranscriber` model absent — user downloads) | ❌ | 1 |
| 20 | `sttProviderUnreachable` | ✅ | 2 |
| 23 | `sttMissingAPIKey` | ❌ | 1 |
| 25 | `sttDecodingFailure` | ❌ | 1 |
| 34 | `sttCustomEndpointNotConfigured` (BYO custom STT — no base URL stored) | ❌ | 1 |
| 35 | `sttCustomCertMismatch` (BYO custom STT — pinned fingerprint changed) | ❌ | 1 |
| 51 | `speechPermissionDenied` (Speech-Recognition TCC off — distinct copy + relay code from 8: a permission toggle, not a rejected key) | ❌ | 1 |
| 54 | `appleSpeechLanguageUnsupported` (explicit non-English language not in Apple's on-device set — recovery is "switch to a cloud provider", NOT "download"; distinct from 18; crosses the Watch relay wire) | ❌ | 1 |

Custom-STT transient/unreachable/auth reuse 16/20/8 (no separate codes); only not-configured + cert-mismatch are custom-specific (34/35) — a user-side config problem, non-retryable.

### Audio cases (properties of the user's input, not the provider — never STT-prefixed)

| Code | Case |
|---|---|
| 11 | `audioInvalid` |
| 14 | `audioMissingData` |
| 21 | `noSpeechDetected` (empty transcript: provider returned 2xx with no recognizable speech — genuine silence or unintelligible audio; funnels via `STTResponseDecoder` from every cloud provider on all surfaces (Watch/iOS/macOS/CarPlay), plus CarPlay's VAD no-speech endpoint. Copy is neutral — never blames the user's voice/mic) |
| 22 | `audioTooLarge` |
| 24 | `audioProcessingFailed` |
| 53 | `audioMicBusy` (macOS only — `SpeechExclusivity` mic-lease refused a concurrent capture; never crosses the relay wire) |

### TTS cases (cloud read-aloud; all `shouldPreserveForRetry = false`)

| Code | Case | Retryable | maxAttempts |
|---|---|---|---|
| 36 | `ttsProviderUnreachable` (408/5xx transport/server) | ✅ | 2 |
| 37 | `ttsSynthesisFailed` (bad voice/request — 400/404/422) | ❌ | 1 |
| 38 | `ttsEmptyAudio` (2xx, zero/undecodable bytes; Gemini preview text-token hiccup) | ✅ | 2 |
| 39 | `ttsUnauthorized` (401/403 — key missing text-to-speech scope) | ❌ | 1 |
| 40 | `ttsRateLimited` (402/429 — rate-limited / out of quota/credit) | ✅ | 2 |
| 41 | `ttsContentBlocked` (Gemini `promptFeedback.blockReason` safety filter — 200 with no audio) | ❌ | 1 |
| 42 | `ttsCustomEndpointNotConfigured` (BYO custom TTS — no base URL stored) | ❌ | 1 |
| 43 | `ttsCustomCertMismatch` (BYO custom TTS — pinned fingerprint changed) | ❌ | 1 |

A failed spoken reply is **never queued** — the text reply is already on screen and `ReplyVoice` falls back to Apple synth on every TTS failure (retryables still retry in-call ≤2× first). **The 4xx split is load-bearing for the surfaced message** (settings sample / Test): 401/403 reads as a KEY-scope problem (e.g. an ElevenLabs key granting only `speech_to_text` 401s on TTS), 402/429 as a credit problem (OpenAI `insufficient_quota`) — NOT "check the voice name" or "check your connection." Mapped in `TTSStatusMap`. See Text-to-Speech.

### Remote-agent cases (`remoteAgent` prefix keeps the abstraction visible)

| Code | Case | Trigger | Retryable |
|---|---|---|---|
| 12 | `remoteAgentNotConfigured` | URL or token missing | ❌ |
| 19 | `remoteAgentUnreachable` | connection refused / DNS / initial-connect timeout | ✅ |
| 26 | `remoteAgentAuthFailed` | HTTP 401 / 403 | ❌ |
| 28 | `remoteAgentTimeout` | URLSession resource budget, or HTTP 408 (reuses this string) | ✅ |
| 29 | `remoteAgentServerError` | HTTP 5xx — incl. OpenClaw contention surfacing as **HTTP 500** (wire body `internal error`) | ✅ |
| 30 | `remoteAgentCertMismatch` | pinned fingerprint mismatch | ❌ |
| 31 | `remoteAgentInvalidResponse` | JSON decode fail / missing `choices[0].message.content` | ❌ |
| 32 | `remoteAgentVisionUnsupported` | model can't take image content | ❌ |
| 33 | `remoteAgentImageTooLarge` | gateway 413 / "image too large" | ❌ |
| 52 | `remoteAgentOutOfCredits` | HTTP 402 — hosted provider out of credits (OpenRouter); `RemoteAgentStatusMap` 402 arm, ahead of 5xx | ❌ |
| 55 | `remoteAgentModelUnavailable` | model id invalid / delisted / not a chat model (body-aware, 400/404) | ❌ |
| 56 | `remoteAgentContextTooLong` | history+attachments exceed the model's context window (body-aware, 400) | ❌ |
| 57 | `remoteAgentRateLimited` | HTTP 429 — provider rate-limit / free-tier daily cap; `RemoteAgentStatusMap` 429 arm. Non-retryable so auto-retry never worsens the limit; the per-turn Retry chip is always available | ❌ |
| 58 | `remoteAgentEndpointUnexpectedResponse` | probe got a 2xx whose body is NOT JSON (`validateProbeBody`) — an HTML page or plaintext. The OFF-by-default OpenClaw chat endpoint serves Control-UI **HTML at 200** — this is the case that made a dead gateway read "Connected". Copy still must NOT assert "a web page" (a proxy's plaintext error lands here too). Also the keyEnvelope wrong-shape exit (OpenRouter is Conduck-managed; 62's adapter-contract copy can't help there) | ❌ |
| 59 | `remoteAgentEndpointNotFound` | HTTP 404 on the PROBE route — classified in `performTestConnection`, never in the shared status map (a send-path 404 can mean model-not-found) | ❌ |
| 62 | `remoteAgentEndpointWrongEnvelope` | probe got a 2xx that IS valid JSON but not the required `modelListEnvelope` (bare array, `{"models":…}`, `{}`) — the JSON twin of 58, split so the copy can name the exact rule (top-level `data` array + the adapter-contract URL): the likeliest failure of a home-built custom adapter. `endpointDisabledRemedy` pairs with 58 AND 62 (a disabled endpoint answering `{}` is the same trap wearing JSON) | ❌ |
| 60 | `remoteAgentModelRequired` | gateway demands an explicit model and none was sent (Ollama / vLLM / default-less LiteLLM). Body-aware at 400/404 **and 422** (FastAPI validation envelopes); distinct from 55 — there's no name to *check*, one must be *set*. The commonest failure of a hand-configured custom gateway (its model field is `.optional`, so an empty save isn't blocked) | ❌ |

**Body-aware errors are mapped before the Int status map.** `RemoteAgentClient.mapBodyError(status:body:)` runs FIRST on a 400/404/413, first match wins in this order: vision-unsupported (`/unsupported.*content/i`·`/image.*not.*support/i`·`/support.*image/i`·`/image.*input/i` → 32) · image-too-large (413 or `/image.*too.*large/i` → 33) · context-too-long (`/maximum context length/i`·`/context_length_exceeded/i` → 56) · model-unavailable (`/not a valid model/i`·`/no endpoints found for/i`·`/embedding model/i` → 55) · else nil. **Vision is checked BEFORE model-unavailable** so OpenRouter's `404 "No endpoints found that support image input"` (→32) stays distinct from `404 "No endpoints found for <model>"` (→55). Matching prefers the structured top-level `error.message` (so `metadata.raw` provider-wrapper text can't false-match), falling back to the whole body. Runs in BOTH the foreground `decodeReply` and the `BackgroundRemoteAgent` delegate; pure-status rejections (402/408/429) live in `RemoteAgentStatusMap` so they cover all send paths (CarPlay, Test Connection) with no body. **Never logs the body** (may carry user/model content).

**Code 27 reserved.** Client-owned history pins no server session, so no per-session lock is ever contended — OpenClaw contention surfaces as HTTP 500 (→ `remoteAgentServerError`), NOT 423. Code 27 stays a reserved gap; `remoteAgentSessionBusy` / `lockRetry*` do not exist — do not reintroduce (contradicts the client-owned-history topology).

### Agent file-transfer cases (44–50)

`maxAttempts = 1` for all seven (fail-fast — the staged item + a visible Retry own retry, NOT `PendingRetryStore`; `shouldPreserveForRetry = false` for all). **NEVER name the credential in any string.**

| Code | Case | Trigger | Retryable |
|---|---|---|---|
| 44 | `fileTransferNotConfigured` | URL or credential missing | ❌ |
| 45 | `fileTransferUnreachable` | connect refused / DNS / connect-timeout | ✅ |
| 46 | `fileTransferAuthFailed` | file-server 401/403 | ❌ |
| 47 | `fileTransferCertMismatch` | pinned fingerprint mismatch (file-server host) | ❌ |
| 48 | `fileTransferServerError` | file-server 5xx | ✅ |
| 49 | `fileTransferUploadFailed` | PUT failed/incomplete | ✅ |
| 50 | `fileTransferFileUnavailable` | stored file gone on retry / probe 404 (user re-attaches) | ❌ |
| 61 | `fileTransferNotAFileServer` | the staged test's GET returned bytes ≠ the bytes just PUT — a login page / dashboard / SSO wall impersonating a WebDAV root. Distinct from 48 (`…ServerError`): the server is fine and its logs show a clean 200, so telling the user to "check the logs" sends them hunting a bug that isn't there | ❌ |

STT failure → user sees the STT error, the agent hop never fires. STT success + agent failure → a `remoteAgent*` case. The two hops are independent for error mapping. Each surface (Watch reply view, iOS notification, Mac popover, CarPlay error TTS) maps cases via the localized-error pattern.

---

## Onboarding

**`OnboardingContainerView` is a FLAT, linear 3-step flow — identical on iPhone/iPad/Mac (no watch/CarPlay first-run):** `OnboardingStep { welcome, enableVoice, completion }`. **Gateway setup is DEFERRED out of onboarding** (the heaviest, most decision-forcing step, demanded before the user has seen the app) — it lives in Settings → Personal AI as the re-runnable Guided Setup (below), reached at point-of-need from the app's unconfigured empty state. The gateway is the only REQUIRED config (see Architectural Invariants). **No STT step** — on-device voice defaults to Apple keyboard dictation (no model download, works on first mic tap; see STT Provider Registry + Apple On-Device STT), so there's nothing to set up here; cloud / high-quality on-device is an opt-in in Settings → Voice. Navigation runs off the CONSTANT `OnboardingFlow.orderedSteps = [welcome, enableVoice, completion]` (not raw enum order); `previousStep()` walks it and the top-leading Back affordance shows on every step except `.welcome`. `OnboardingFlowTests` pins `allCases`/`orderedSteps` as a regression net against a gateway/STT/notification step ever leaking back into the enum.

**Step scaffold (`OnboardingStepScaffold`):** mascot+title+body in a greedy `ScrollView`; the primary CTA is a VStack SIBLING below it (NOT `.safeAreaInset`) so scrolled content can never render beneath the CTA at large Dynamic Type — a clipped/buried CTA at accessibility sizes was the failure mode. Content width-capped only on macOS + iPad-regular. **Guided-wizard panel (macOS + iPad-regular, `.top` placement only):** the whole step (content + pinned footer) is height-capped (`Constants.Layout.onboardingPanelMaxHeight`) and centered in the region below the chrome band (the host's `onboardingChromeInset` moves OUTSIDE the panel as region padding) — a tall window never strands the CTA at its bottom edge, and the panel height is constant across steps so mascot + CTA hold their y through transitions. Off on iPhone, at accessibility type sizes, and for the first-run `.center` flow (all render exactly the un-capped layout, which is also what a window shorter than the cap degrades to).

**Welcome = value-prop, no consent gate.** Always-enabled CTA — on-device STT is local (needs no consent) and cloud-STT / gateway egress is disclosed per-destination at each config choice (a no-backend BYO-key conduit collects nothing → "Data Not Collected", see Privacy & Security). A passive Privacy/Terms footer line (`LegalFooterLinks`, `Constants.{privacyPolicy,termsOfService}URL`) is ACCESS, not a consent gate.

**`EnableVoiceStepView` (mic + Speech Recognition priming, before `completion`) — PIVOTAL.** Its CTA requests the microphone (`VoicePermissions.requestMicrophone()`) then, back-to-back, Speech Recognition (`ensureSpeechRecognitionForActiveProvider()`) — which fires ONLY when the active STT provider's `transport == .inProcess` (Apple on-device) AND `.notDetermined`; cloud STT skips the speech ask. During onboarding Apple is the default, so the speech ask always fires here. **NON-BLOCKING:** any denial shows soft "enable later" copy and STILL advances (the gateway is the only required config). **Why request BOTH up front:** the in-app record path only *checks* Speech Recognition — `AppleSpeechRunner.transcribe` guards on `SFSpeechRecognizer.authorizationStatus()` and throws `speechPermissionDenied` WITHOUT ever prompting — so an Apple-default user who never opened Settings would hit a permission error on first record instead of a prompt. Granting both here also covers the surfaces that can NEVER prompt later: Watch relay (transcribes phone-side), CarPlay, headless App Intent.
