It also fire-and-forget-warms the Standard model (`AppleSpeechPreparer.prepareStandardIfAuthorized()`) so the first capture isn't a cold race. **Stale-TCC / skipped-user fallbacks:** the iOS in-app recorder + macOS dictation call `ensureSpeechRecognitionForActiveProvider()` BEFORE entering `.recording` (a denial never wastes a recording); the Setup-Guide Action-Button step preflights it for the headless path (below).

**No contextual mic-gate** — composer mic taps go straight to `startRecording()`; the first tap records immediately and a missing on-device model self-heals inline (see Apple On-Device STT → Model lifecycle), never a redirect.

**Landing + `completion`.** Onboarding lands directly on the conversation screen: gateway configured → the composer-ready start state; gateway unconfigured → the empty state (mascot + "Connect your personal AI to start"). Its **"Connect Personal AI" CTA and the locked composer DEEP-LINK straight into Guided Setup** — they don't drop the user on the Personal AI list to hunt for a button. iOS/iPad present Settings → Personal AI AND auto-open the guided cover (an `autoOpenGuidedSetup` flag on `SettingsRoute`, consumed once via a `didAutoOpenGuided` latch from a `.task` that first `await`s `awaitRemoteAgentStateLoaded()` — so a not-yet-hydrated VM never misclassifies a configured user; the cover stays hosted at the Settings NavigationStack root per the iOS-26 pushed-cover rule, and is presented **item-based** — `.fullScreenCover(item:)` over `GuidedGatewayPresentation`, destination + presence committed as ONE value via `GuidedGatewayHostState.present(initialPath:)` (a two-field Bool-switch + path shape races on iOS 26: the first present builds content before the path write lands and opens the chooser instead of the deep-link; a one-runloop-tick deferred flip does not reliably close the race either); `SettingsView`/`IpadSettingsView` carry `.id(route.id)` so the latch resets per presentation). macOS opens the guided overlay directly (same `MainWindowView` owns `guidedHost`) and also opens Settings so the primer's manual exit has the Personal AI list to land on. The iOS/iPadOS composer is NEVER auto-focused on launch (focus only on user tap; an armed one-shot re-fired on `NavigationSplitView` remounts and popped the keyboard on every iPad open — voice-first identity); macOS self-focuses on appear (desktop cursor, no on-screen keyboard). Onboarding does NOT request notification auth (neither `completeOnboarding()` nor any step). The container's `onComplete:` callback notifies `RootView`'s conditional-rendering gate without a presentation chain.

**macOS completion carries a default-ON "Launch at Login" checkbox** (`SMAppService.mainApp`) — a PENDING preference, NOT a live status mirror, committed ONLY on the "Let's go" tap (`commitLaunchAtLogin()` registers/unregisters the delta), NEVER on appear. Why: proactive on-appear registration is the App Review 2.4.5(iii) consent hook; a pre-checked box + explicit finish = consent (unchecking before finishing registers nothing). The Settings mirror (`MacGeneralCategory.setLaunchAtLogin`) commits per-toggle and, on `.requiresApproval`, opens System Settings ▸ Login Items rather than silently snapping the toggle off.

**Settings ▸ Setup Guide (`SetupGuideView`, iOS/iPadOS only)** is a re-runnable 3-step flow: Shortcut install → Action-Button binding → notification soft-ask, with slide transitions + a progress bar; step 2 ADVANCES to step 3 (doesn't dismiss), step 3 dismisses. Its entry card presents unconditionally — no gateway gate (binding a trigger is a deliberate action). The Action-Button step **preflights Speech Recognition before handing off:** if Apple STT is active and Speech Recognition is `.notDetermined` it requests it (the headless `ConverseIntent` path can only CHECK, never prompt → a fresh Apple-default user's first Action-Button capture would otherwise throw `speechPermissionDenied`); denied/restricted BLOCKS the handoff, cloud STT skips. The Shortcut step opens the bundled `GigaAction.shortcut` via `UIApplication.open` / `NSWorkspace.open`.

**Notification authorization is primed by the Setup-Guide notification step and backstopped lazily everywhere else.** The central idempotent helper `NotificationPermissions.ensureRequested()` (requests `[.alert,.sound,.badge]` only when `.notDetermined`, never blocks; no-op under XCTest — the system prompt hangs the unsigned host) fires at every FOREGROUND moment that can lead to a notification: (a) the first committed foreground converse dispatch — **iOS ONLY** (`ConversationDetailViewModel`, `#if !os(macOS)`; macOS surfaces replies via the menu-bar unread dot, not a notification), (b) the share-drain/recovery foreground moment (`SharedInboxDrainer`, kept on macOS). Why foreground-only: a background/headless context can't PRESENT a prompt. The "Not now" deferral flag (`Constants.notificationsDeferredKey`, App-Group bool — LOCKED legacy string `"notifications_deferred_in_onboarding"`; renaming silently resets every user's deferral) is honored by EXACTLY ONE backstop — the iOS composer send — so a tapped "Not now" isn't undone by the next send; never cleared; the genuinely-headless backstops (`ConverseIntent`, share-drain) IGNORE it (there a notification is the sole feedback channel). Presentation logic: see Notification Presentation.

**Guided gateway setup (`GuidedGatewaySetupView`, Settings → Personal AI, re-runnable).** A LANE-AWARE state machine — `Step { primer, chooser, fork(lane), headsUp(lane), readiness(lane), adapter(lane), helper(lane), commands(lane), success(ref), hostedModel, hostedModelEdit }` over `GatewaySetupLane { .fullAgent, .custom }`. Both self-hosted lanes walk `fork → headsUp (iOS/iPadOS; macOS forks straight to readiness) → readiness → helper → commands → success`, differing only in copy and help-page context; the in-app Commands step always runs `bash conduck-connect.sh --setup`, whose gateway picker reports detected OpenClaw/Hermes installs without choosing one. The `.custom` readiness step additionally carries a bordered-secondary escape ("I built my own AI — it's not a server yet", `onAdapterEscape` — `nil` on `.fullAgent`, whose footer is unchanged) into `adapter(.custom)` → `GatewayAdapterBriefView`: the self-built-agent hatch — keep the agent as-is, copy a normative brief (`clipboardBrief`, content-lock-tested in `GatewayAdapterBriefTests`) into the AI coding tool that built it — pointer-first to the hosted build brief (`/setup/adapter/build.md`), narrowed to stop before exposure/pairing (this flow owns those via `conduck-connect`), with a self-contained fallback aligned to the contract at `Constants.adapterContractURL` (both raw `.md` URLs hardcoded in the brief) — which generates a small OpenAI-compatible adapter. **Workflow-ownership boundary — the brief's load-bearing rule, test-locked:** the brief is pasted into an AUTONOMOUS coding agent, so it hands over the adapter-CHECK invocations in COMPLETE runnable form (download + `CONDUCK_TOKEN` + explicit loopback URL — a bare `--check-adapter` blocks on an interactive URL/token prompt, fatal for a non-interactive agent) and NO exposure/pairing invocation in any spelling; exposure and pairing are named in prose only, because the absent command is the control and first-person phrasing is merely intent. A calm in-card amber lock row carries the irreducible warning (the adapter can trigger the agent's tools — keep it private, require a token) — the flow's SINGLE semantic use of amber (readiness/helper capability-row icons are accent-tinted — the blue-glyph/neutral-text `AccentGlyphActionLabelStyle` vocabulary; the commands hand-off panel is neutral glass). **Button vocabulary, every step:** exactly ONE filled accent CTA per screen — the footer's way-forward; every in-content action (copy buttons, escapes, Validate key) is a bordered stroke secondary. **Desktop-handoff = ONE screen (iOS/iPadOS only):** the steps after the fork are desktop tasks rendered on a phone, so the create branch's first stop is the dedicated `headsUp(lane)` step (`GatewayHeadsUpView`, `conduck-waving` mascot) — commands are coming, a computer is the comfortable place to run them, and the lane-correct site page has everything ready to copy: `Constants.setupCommandPageURLDisplay(custom:)` (`/setup/agent/` full-agent leads with `--setup`; `/setup/custom/` custom leads with `--check-server`, whose interactive PASS can continue into setup, and links onward to the adapter contract). The URL renders as a display-only selectable mono chip, never a tappable Link (opening it on the phone is the detour the screen prevents). NO other step repeats the handoff — the fork carries no desktop footnote (the heads-up is its very next screen), the adapter step links the build guide + contract only via its all-platform learn-more sentence (`adapterBuildGuideURL` · `adapterContractURL`), and the commands step leads with the command block plus the neutral return-cue panel ("Come back here and scan or paste it to finish"). macOS never shows the heads-up (already at a computer; the fork routes straight to readiness, so the step never enters its back-stack). The hosted-model (OpenRouter) lane is NOT a `GatewaySetupLane` — it has its own `hostedModel`/`hostedModelEdit` pair — but it **terminates on the SAME `success` step** every self-hosted lane does: `success` carries only a `RemoteAgentRef?` (no lane), so EVERY path that connects a gateway confirms itself on one screen, and no path ends by silently dismissing the sheet under the user. Navigation is a `backStack` (Back pops one step; reaching `success` CLEARS it, so footer Done + Close are the only exits). The caller passes its **LIVE `SettingsViewModel`** (never a fresh one) so a save reflects in the Settings screen behind the sheet; `init(viewModel:initialPath:onDismiss:onPrimerManual:showPrimer:customLaneAvailable:)` — the pure, unit-tested `initialStep(initialPath:showPrimer:)` routes the start (`nil`/`.later` → `primer` when `showPrimer` else `chooser`, `.selfHosted` → `fork(.fullAgent)`, `.hostedModel` → `hostedModel`, `.quickConnect(target:)` → `commands` in the target's lane — built-in → `.fullAgent`, custom → `.custom`, with the pairing import LOCKED to that ref; a never-constructed OpenRouter target maps defensively to `hostedModel`; a lane deep-link never shows the primer). The guided LANES carry NO manual-entry escape — `fork` offers only its two guided branches, so the guide never competes with itself; hand-editing a URL/token stays reachable OUTSIDE the guide (the primer's `onPrimerManual` → Personal AI list, and that list's gateway rows / "+ Add custom gateway", which mints the same custom draft). The Custom card is hidden when `customLaneAvailable == false` (caller at the custom-gateway cap). The whole flow (and onboarding) paints `SetupAtmosphereBackground` — a static `MeshGradient` warm-black base + a one-time NEUTRAL key-light fade behind the mascot and two faint cool corner ambients, all full-bleed at any window size (no looping drift; static final composite under Reduce Motion). The backdrop carries no nameable color: the mascots are the only saturated element (their `onboardingMascot` shadow is neutral black, not tinted), and the fork's recommended-pick card border is accent, so the adapter lock row stays the flow's single amber.

**`GatewayPrimerStepView` (step 0 — first-run orientation).** Resets the surprising "Conduck has no AI of its own" expectation before the chooser: headline **"How Conduck works"** — an ORIENTATION title, deliberately not a task title, because this is the only non-task screen in the flow (every other step titles a job: where does it live · is it running · create the code · copy the command). A task title here ("Connect your AI") both mislabels the screen and collides with the `fork`'s "Connect your AI server" three steps later — a ≤2-sentence body that names NONE of the three lanes (listing them would just spoil the chooser one screen later) and avoids the misleading universals ("host a model" — a self-hosted gateway usually calls a CLOUD model; "run a server" — false for the hosted OpenRouter lane), landing on: Conduck has no AI of its own — think of it like a **remote control** that talks to the AI you set up and run yourself. **The remote-control image is the whole payload** — it carries the ownership invariant (the AI is YOURS: you stand it up, you run it, you pay for it) in one beat, and it survives every lane (a self-hosted agent, a custom endpoint, and a hosted model on the user's own key are all "the AI you set up and run yourself"). Do NOT trade it for a literal enumeration of what the user must supply — the concrete asks land on the screens that actually make them (`readiness` asks for the server, `hostedModel` asks for the key) — the `conduck-card-tower` mascot with a one-time settle, and THREE ranked exits — PRIMARY filled "Guide me through it" → `chooser`; SECONDARY bordered "Set up manually" → the Personal AI list (`onPrimerManual`, a REQUIRED closure — no lane, no draft mint; the flow's ONLY manual escape); TERTIARY `Link` "Read the setup guide" → `Constants.setupGuideURL` (opens Safari WITHOUT dismissing). The container's top-trailing ✕ Close ("not now" → chat) is the fourth, unmarked exit. **Gating:** `showPrimer` is CALLER-RESOLVED at each cover-construction site as `!SettingsManager.hasSeenGatewayPrimer() && !viewModel.hasAnyConfiguredRemoteAgent` (never re-derived from stale VM state in `init`) — so a configured user re-running Guided Setup, or a lane deep-link, skips it. The device-local flag `gateway_primer_seen` (App-Group only, NEVER KVS; synchronous read + write via static `SettingsManager.hasSeenGatewayPrimer()`/`markGatewayPrimerSeen()`) is marked seen on "Guide me through it" OR "Set up manually" — NEVER on Close (a Close-without-choosing re-shows it).

**`PairingImportSheet` is a CONTAINER-level sheet, not a `Step`** — reachable from the fork ("I already have a code") and the commands step ("scan/paste"). **Its `onConnected` fires ONLY on a VERIFIED gateway probe (`connectedRef`)** — never on a mere save or an unresolved cert — and is consumed on dismiss to advance to `success(activeLane, ref)`; a failed/cert-pending import leaves `connectedRef` nil → stay put (the sheet shows its own recovery), never a false "Connected". Why: advancing on a save alone would claim a connection the probe never confirmed. Import mechanics: see Gateway Setup & Pairing.

**`GatewayChooserStepView` (the lane chooser)** is NEUTRAL by design — no card badged or emphasized; the only steer is section order (own-AI first, hosted last): "Your own AI" = the full-agent card ("OpenClaw or Hermes server") + the Custom card ("An AI you built — or a custom server"); "Hosted" = OpenRouter ("OpenRouter with your key"), last. Each title carries its prerequisite so a button-scanner learns what the option requires without reading the primer. Reached from the guided-setup sheet — after the primer when `showPrimer`, else directly (whose top-trailing Close is the exit) — no "set up later" link.

**`GatewaySetupSuccessView` (terminal success — SHARED by every lane).** Reached from a verified pairing import (self-hosted lanes) AND from a completed OpenRouter save (hosted lane). **LANE-AGNOSTIC BY CONSTRUCTION:** it takes only `connectedRef` and derives every line from it — no lane parameter, no lane branch. That property is load-bearing (it is what lets the hosted lane, which is not a `GatewaySetupLane`, reuse the screen verbatim); do NOT reintroduce a lane to special-case copy — add per-ref facts, resolved the way the existing rows are. Resolves the gateway BY REF against the cached roster (so a second gateway names ITSELF, not the first built-in) and surfaces honestly: the display name (proper noun / user label, verbatim, never localized or logged); file-sharing readiness (`fileLaneStatus(for:) == .unsupported` OMITS the row rather than inventing a state — the hosted model's case, `fileTransferSupported: false`); and new-chats routing — only the first-ever gateway bootstraps the default (see Architectural Invariants), so a later one gets a quiet "pick it under New chats use" line instead of overclaiming. Robust to `connectedRef == nil` → generic "ready" copy, per-ref rows dropped. A single cross-device footnote ("Set up once — this carries over to your other devices") sits OUTSIDE the summary card BY DESIGN: the card reports only VERIFIED per-ref state, while the note is an unconditional claim about the storage mechanism (non-secrets on iCloud KVS, secrets on the synchronizable Keychain) that was never probed for this user — keeping it in a separate register preserves the card's never-claim-an-unverified-state contract.

**`HostedModelGatewayStepView` (hosted-model / OpenRouter detail).** Classifies itself ONCE after `refreshRemoteAgentState()` hydrates (classifying a cold `configuredRemoteAgentRefSet` would misread a configured user as first-time): `.setup` (not configured) → editable key + required-model body, whose successful Connect fires `onConnected` → the shared `success(ref)` step (`.builtin(.openrouter)`), so the hosted lane confirms itself exactly like the self-hosted ones; `.manage` (configured) → a confirmation card + "Change model or API key" pushing `hostedModelEdit`, whose Done calls `proceed` (dismiss) — nothing connected THIS session, so there is nothing to confirm. **The advance is keyed on a save that COMPLETED this session (`saveRemoteAgent` ok), NOT the probe verdict** — a passing `/v1/key` probe whose save then fails (e.g. unsigned-sim Keychain) must never push the user onto a screen that says "Connected"; the probe proves the KEY, only the save proves the CONFIG. `onConnected` is a REQUIRED closure (a missing wiring is a compile error, not a silent "saved, but the user is left on the form"). Title/icon are honesty-gated: "connected" ONLY once the `/v1/key` probe passed this session (`remoteAgentLiveValidated.contains(ref)`), else "set up". `OpenRouterKeyReuseCallout` appears only in setup when a voice OpenRouter key exists and no gateway key is stored yet (`reuseVoiceKeyForOpenRouterGateway()` copies the key but not the required model — copy, not a live link). A partial-sync banner shows when a synced model is present with no stored key (model on KVS, key on Keychain); focusing the key field sets `editorHasUnsavedChanges` so a late KVS reload can't clobber typing. **A user edit of the key retracts the verdict** (`onChange(of: pendingKey)` → `.unset`, mirroring `HostedModelEditStepView`): a verdict describes the key that was PROBED, so leaving it up accuses a key that may now be correct. Guarded on `connecting`, because a successful `connect()` blanks `pendingKey` after the save — the one write that is not a user edit, and without the guard it would stomp the `.valid` the save just wrote. The `.invalid` row wraps (`.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)` + `Spacer(minLength: 0)`); without it the `HStack` sizes the `Text` to its ideal SINGLE-LINE width and a multi-sentence remedy truncates mid-word.

---

## Localization

**V1 ships English-only UI.** `LocalizedStringResource` discipline enforced day one — future localization is a translation pass, not a refactor. UI chrome stays English while Apple on-device STT transcribes any Apple-supported language (the onboarding language-picker labels are system endonyms via `Locale.localizedString`, not catalog strings).

- **Two per-target catalogs, NO shared one:** `Localizable.xcstrings` for iOS and Watch. `InfoPlist.xcstrings` skipped — Apple reads the `Info.plist` value directly when the catalog is absent.
- **Convention:** SwiftUI `Text("…")`; App Intents `LocalizedStringResource("…")`; programmatic `String(localized:)`. **App Intent descriptions MUST NOT contain platform names** ("iPhone", "Mac") — Apple rejects ITMS-90626.
- **`AppError`** (62 cases) uses namespaced explicit keys (`<domain>.error.<case>` + `.recovery`).
- All STT/TTS/remote-agent/CarPlay/Watch/composer user-facing copy is catalogued (per-provider keys disambiguate, e.g. `settings.stt.provider.openai.placeholder`). Two correctness rules: backend picker labels **"OpenClaw" / "Hermes" are proper nouns — do not translate**; **NO `mistral.*` keys** — vendor display name resolves from the active preset's `displayName`, not a key path.

---

## Privacy & Security

Recap — each rule owned at its home section:
- **Audio** capture → STT → discard, every site `defer`-cleans; no user accounts (Keychain UUID). → Audio Pipeline.
- **No backend = no DPA** (boundary is the device; STT-provider + gateway DPAs are the user's, BYO-key). Conduck never sees/logs URLs, tokens, replies, or backend type; secrets stay in Keychain, optionally iCloud-Keychain-synced (E2E, developer-blind). Bearer redacted in clients; never log audio/transcripts/replies/URLs/tokens, never reveal keys in errors. → Settings & Storage.
- **TCC re-checked at every Apple `transcribe`** (no silent cloud fallback on revoked auth). → Apple On-Device STT.
- **Conversation store = on-device + user's own iCloud (CloudKit private DB)**, developer-blind. → Conversation Store.
- **Attached-image EXIF/GPS stripped before send** — `ImageProcessor` re-encodes via `CGImageDestinationAddImage` (the bare CGImage), NOT `…FromSource` (which bleeds GPS/camera-serial). Why: FromSource leaks location/camera-serial. → Agent File Transfer.
- **Screenshot capture** (iOS `Screenshot & Voice` entry, macOS ⌘⇧2) can contain anything on-screen — captured PNG is in-memory only (never written or logged), sent inline ONLY to the user's own gateway, ONLY on explicit user trigger. macOS needs a Screen Recording TCC grant, preflighted up front and rationale-first (Conduck's alert always precedes the system ask, so the dialogs are strictly sequential; consent is per code SIGNATURE — a re-signed install is a new TCC client and gets the system dialog again). Same EXIF strip applies at send.

**App Store privacy label = "Data Not Collected"** (BYO-server, Mastodon-client precedent):
- Apple's "collect" = off-device transmission letting us (the developer) or an embedded third-party SDK access data beyond real-time servicing. Conduck has neither: no backend, no analytics/ad/crash SDK, no IDFA. All egress (cloud STT audio, agent round-trip) goes to the user's own configured providers under the user's own key. Hardcoded provider URLs don't change this (a URL constant ≠ their SDK). On-device STT is no transmission; sync is the user's own iCloud. ⇒ not collected. No ATT required (no IDFA/third-party analytics).
- **Fallback only if App Review forces it:** the conservative two-type declaration (Audio Data + Other User Content; App Functionality; Not Linked; No Tracking). Label is editable post-submission → ship "Data Not Collected" first; App Review notes pre-empt the metadata-accuracy challenge. This is a don't-re-litigate decision.

---

## Build-Time Posture (pbxproj)

| Setting | Value | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE` | `NO` (all 6 configs) | Hand-authored `Info.plist` via `INFOPLIST_FILE` is the only safe path for non-allowlisted keys (`UIBackgroundModes`, scene manifest). |
| `INFOPLIST_KEY_UIApplicationSceneManifest_Generation` | `NO` (main target Debug+Release, both sdk variants) | CarPlay defense — Xcode auto-gen silently drops the key; template refresh can re-introduce `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE = YES`. |
| `*_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` | 26.5 (all schemes) | Pins `SpeechAnalyzer` availability + Watch ControlWidget regressions. |
| `PBXFileSystemSynchronizedBuildFileExceptionSet` | Excludes `Info.plist` from Xcode 26 synced-folder auto-bundling (main / watch / widget) | Otherwise `Info.plist` processed twice → "Multiple commands produce" failure. |

**Build identity (Community/Official split):**
- Every identity-bearing setting resolves through `Configs/Identity.xcconfig` (project-level `baseConfigurationReference`, Debug+Release): `CONDUCK_BUNDLE_ID_BASE`, `CONDUCK_IDENTITY_NAMESPACE`, `CONDUCK_GROUP_ID`, `CONDUCK_ICLOUD_CONTAINER_ID`, `CONDUCK_DISPLAY_NAME`, `CONDUCK_ENTITLEMENTS_VARIANT`, `CONDUCK_DEVELOPMENT_TEAM`, plus single-source `MARKETING_VERSION`/`CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION`. Committed defaults = neutral **Community** identity (`com.example.*`, empty team, display name "Conduck Community", Community entitlements). `#include? "Identity-Override.xcconfig"` (private, never published in the public repo) flips the build to the frozen **Official** identity (`ai.gigaduck.AgentRelay`, team `<YOUR_TEAM_ID>`, CarPlay). Precedence trap: never re-add these settings to a pbxproj `buildSettings` dict — a dict value silently shadows the xcconfig.
- Main-app entitlements are a variant pair selected by `CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS = Conduck/Conduck-$(CONDUCK_ENTITLEMENTS_VARIANT).entitlements`: `Conduck-Official.entitlements` carries `carplay-voice-based-conversation` (restricted — breaks provisioning for ungranted teams), `Conduck-Community.entitlements` is identical without it. All other entitlements files are single, `$(CONDUCK_*)`-parameterized.
- Runtime identity is sourced from Info.plist keys (`ConduckIdentityNamespace`, `ConduckAppGroupID`, `ConduckCloudKitContainerID`, widget `ConduckControlKind`) — `Constants.identityNamespace` + `nonisolated` derived constants with FROZEN suffixes; share extensions and the widget read their OWN bundle's keys (they can't import `Constants`). Never hard-code an `ai.gigaduck.*` string in code.
- Drift guards: `OfficialIdentityLockTests` (ConduckTests) + `OfficialIdentityWatchLockTests` (ConduckWatchTests) pin every persistent official identifier (App Group, keychain service, background-URLSession ids, CloudKit container, widget control kind via the embedded appex); both self-skip under a community identity.
- Display-name trap: `INFOPLIST_KEY_CFBundleDisplayName` must stay `$(CONDUCK_DISPLAY_NAME)` at every target — on `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE = YES` targets (iOS share ext) the merged INFOPLIST_KEY silently shadows the in-file plist value, so a re-introduced literal breaks only the Community identity and no official-parity check catches it.

**Cross-target embeds (main `Conduck` target embeds 3 appexes + the Watch app):**
- **Watch app** (`Embed Watch Content`) + its `PBXTargetDependency`: singular `platformFilter = ios`. Why: macOS builds otherwise fail "embedded content built for watchOS". Singular `ios` IS honored.
- **`ConduckShareExtension.appex`** (iOS): singular `platformFilter = ios`. **`ConduckShareExtensionMac.appex`** (macOS): PLURAL `platformFilters = (macos, )` on BOTH the embed build-file and the `PBXTargetDependency`. Why: the singular `macos` token is silently ignored (only legacy `ios`/`maccatalyst` honor singular) → a macOS appex would embed into the iOS build. The plural array is mandatory for the macOS appex; the iOS appex's singular `ios` works.

**Cross-target source patterns:**
- **Approach A (membership exception):** one source file, multiple targets — `Services/STT/*`, `Services/AudioCompressor.swift`, `Utilities/Constants.swift`, `Models/AppError.swift`.
- **Approach A trap:** `AppleSpeechRunner.swift` uses file-level `#if !os(watchOS)` instead — `PBXFileSystemSynchronizedBuildFileExceptionSet` membership exceptions are non-functional in this pbxproj's synchronized groups (Xcode 26.5).
- **Approach B (file duplication):** byte-identical copies in separate target folders — `RecordNoteIntent.swift` + `WatchRecordingCoordinator.swift` (watch app ↔ widget extension). Mirror edits to BOTH copies.

---

## Testing Seams & Isolation

Seams that keep the XCTest suite deterministic + 0-failure green on the iOS Simulator (the authoritative signal). Each is load-bearing — reverting one reintroduces the named failure.

- **Simulator runs CloudKit-OFF (production path — DO NOT revert):** `ConversationStore.init()` switches the container CLASS on `#if targetEnvironment(simulator)` (plain `NSPersistentContainer` on sim, `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` on device) AND calls `configureSyncOptions(cloudKit: false)`. Suppressing only `cloudKitContainerOptions` is INSUFFICIENT — the CloudKit container reaches `CKContainer.default()` during metadata migration and fatal-asserts (`CKContainer.m:748`) on a sim with no iCloud entitlement (silent process exit). Sync is verifiable only on device.
- **Core Data test-seam:** `ConversationStore(inMemory:storeURL:)` uses a plain `NSPersistentContainer` (NOT the CloudKit subclass) — `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` rejects a non-SQLite store with NSCocoaError 134060 on iOS 26.5 even with nil `cloudKitContainerOptions`. Do NOT "restore" the CloudKit container in the seam.
- **Screenshot-mode store seam:** `ConversationStore.shared` swaps to a fresh in-memory, CloudKit-off store when `-ConduckQAScreenshotMode` is active (`#if DEBUG && !os(watchOS)` branch in the private init). On a signed real machine the App Group store is the founder's ACTUAL CloudKit data — the idempotent seeder would skip (real chats appear in captures) and any mutation would sync to real iCloud. Plain `-ConduckQAMode` keeps the production store on purpose (persistence-sensitive QA flows). QA seeding has TWO call sites: iOS `RootView.task` + macOS `AppDelegate.applicationDidFinishLaunching` (RootView never mounts on a Mac-only install) — a new surface entry point needs its own seed call.
- **Test-isolation (dual-store wipe):** any suite driving a setter that DUAL-WRITES App-Group `defaults` AND iCloud KVS (e.g. `setCustomSTTURL`) MUST wipe BOTH in setUp/tearDown — a defaults-only wipe leaves KVS residue that `resolveStoredURL` / the migration-flag read picks up when `ubiquityIdentityToken` is non-nil on the sim (fluctuates).
- **iCloud-suspend seam (signed-in sim):** the dual-wipe closes the synchronous leak but NOT the async one — `handleiCloudChange` mirrors real-iCloud `ServerChange` echoes of sibling-suite KVS writes into `defaults` AFTER `wipeAll()`, re-minting state mid-test (bites EMPTY-state asserts). `SettingsManager.setICloudSyncSuspendedForTesting(_:)` (`#if DEBUG`, default off) forces `iCloudAvailable=false` + early-returns the observer; set true in setUp / false in tearDown (restore is load-bearing — `SettingsManagerICloudSyncTests` needs the mirror live).
- **CarPlaySettings-observer minting race:** for URL-seeding/minting runs the suspend seam doesn't help — `CarPlaySettings.shared.init()` registers a `.settingsDidChangeRemotely` observer → `ensureCustomVoiceEndpointMigrated()`; any sibling settings write can run a spurious no-URL migration in the flag-cleared / URL-not-seeded window → sets the persistent flag → the real minting run early-returns (`0 endpoints minted`). Minting tests call `clearMigrationGateForMinting()` AFTER seeding the URL. Production is unaffected (flag persists once-ever; the re-trigger is latch-guarded).
- **Unsigned macOS test-host CloudKit flake:** `platform=macOS` full-suite intermittently kills processes with `EXC_BREAKPOINT` in `PFCloudKitContainerProvider containerWithIdentifier` (unsigned-host entitlement trap on the production `ConversationStore.shared` container, distinct from the seam; victim suites shift run-to-run). The **iOS-sim full run is the authoritative signal** — use it, not `platform=macOS`. Keychain-touching cases XCTSkip/guard on the unsigned sim (signed-only).
- **Safari-capture guard tests (edit the paired files TOGETHER or the suite reddens):** `ConduckTests/WebPageCaptureTests` reads the appex sources off disk and pins (a) the appex `WebPageCapture.swift` mirror byte-identical to the canonical `ConduckShareExtensionMac/WebPageCapture.swift` below its header, (b) the two `ConduckWebCapture.js` copies byte-identical to each other, (c) the three 128 KiB literals equal (JS `MAX_BYTES` = `WebPageCapture.maxCaptureBytes` = `Constants.webPageCaptureMaxBytes`). Editing one appex copy / one literal without the others → red suite. The JS output is UNTRUSTED (a page script can replace the bridge payload), so `parse` re-validates every field — the suite also locks default-fill on type miss, the 128 KiB re-clamp, `originalByteCount` floored at the returned size, and the empty-selection → page-scope fallback.
